# OKModel — Decisions log (companion to okmodel-api-design.md, draft 26)

Every decision states what was chosen and why. The spec states the rules; this file keeps the history.


| # | Topic | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Internal goal | Understandability is measured in CI with type budgets; safety and correctness are measured by the isolation check and the migration linter (section 1). A feature that makes the model harder to understand as it grows is removed or redesigned |
| D2 | Positioning | Independent library (like sently). OKE will later replace Drizzle in `store.sql` with OKModel and adapt to it; OKModel's design is not constrained by OKE |
| D3 | Versioning | All work in 0.x through milestones M0–M5; 1.0 is a gate with conditions |
| D4 | Databases | Postgres, then SQLite, then MySQL/MariaDB, all within 0.x; others as community dialect packages |
| D5 | Dialect separation | Import paths `okmodel/pg`, `okmodel/sqlite`, `okmodel/mysql`; common subset `okmodel/sql` |
| D6 | Drivers | Not built in; zero-dependency adapters at `okmodel/<dialect>/<driver>` |
| D7 | Responsibilities | `defineConfig` = tooling; `schema()` = the model; `connect()` = runtime |
| D8 | Tables | One file per table; `table(name, fields, options)`; references by name; types via `Register`, with explicit-schema types for multi-schema projects |
| D9 | Column types | `t.*` = database types only, per dialect; codecs in the schema; `bigint` codec defaults to `"string"` |
| D10 | Picklists | `.picklist([...])` compiles to a `CHECK` constraint, not a database enum |
| D11 | Validation | Inline `.validate([...])` or options section; one place per field; base rules derived from the type; transforms in the rule array |
| D12 | Validation at write time | `validation: false` by default; overridable per table and per call; validated values are not re-validated |
| D13 | Standalone validation | `tasks.insert.validate(x)` throws; `tasks.insert.check(x)` returns a result; `tasks.insert` is a Standard Schema |
| D14 | Input and output protection | Unknown keys stripped; `.guarded()` fields never filled from input (keys and trait fields automatically); `.hidden()` fields excluded unless selected by name |
| D15 | Operators | Tagged helpers (`lt(x)`, `startsWith(x)`, `has({...})`), never plain objects; runtime identifier validation; allowlisted `filters()` for user-driven filtering |
| D16 | Traits | Replace spreads; defaults in the schema; explicit opt-out |
| D16a | Presets | Named, typed query refinements on a table are called `presets` (formerly scopes); `tasks.filters()` is the allowlisted builder for client-supplied filters |
| D17 | Record lifecycle | Three operations with fixed meanings: `delete` = permanent removal of the matched records (active or archived), `archive` = reversible removal from the active dataset, `restore` = return to the active dataset. The `archivable()` trait provides the capability; how archived rows are stored is a strategy, not a different meaning |
| D18 | Tenancy vs ownership | Ownership is a field plus a preset; tenancy is an isolation concept with strategies |
| D19 | Tenancy inheritance | Defined in the schema; every table isolated by default; `via` or `global(reason)` exceptions; tenant-scoped uniques and composite FKs |
| D20 | Errors | One `OkmError`; six categories with default HTTP statuses; `from`, `summary`, `fields`, `toHttp`, `log`, `match` |
| D21 | Generics | Types keyed by table name; `TablesWith`, `TablesWithColumn`; `Tx` is a `Client` |
| D22 | Extension | Two user mechanisms: traits (table level) and read-only hookm in `connect()`. Custom types via `t.custom()`, custom operators as tagged helpers. Internally one contribution protocol, kept private until 1.0. No `plugin` concept |
| D23 | Transactions | Capability `interactive` or `batch`; `db.batch()` everywhere |
| D24 | Migrations | Declared renames; expand/contract classification; compatibility-based drift check; linter seeded from Squawk, strong_migrations and Atlas; safe rewrites generated |
| D25 | Type strategy | Decided by the M0 spike (inferred vs emitted row types), measured as described in D114. **Decided: emitted by default (D120)**; inferred stays as the zero-build mode |
| D26 | Programmatic schema API | Exposed in M2 (the catalog builder already exists for `okm pull`) |
| D27 | General rule | Defaults flow down from schema to table to call; exceptions are declared where they happen |
| D28 | Client entry | `connect()` |
| D29 | Removed | Branded IDs (added type weight for rare benefit) |
| D30 | Architecture | The catalog is the center; queries flow logical intent → rules with provenance → final safety verification → physical plan → dialect SQL |
| D31 | Operation guarantee | One logical operation per call; for reads and relation loading the statement count depends on query shape, never on result size; writes may be chunked by input size inside one transaction. A logical operation is not a method name: one method may carry several modes |
| D38 | API taxonomy | Core operations `find`/`insert`/`update`/`delete`; lifecycle operations `archive`/`restore` on archivable tables; variants are modes of those operations; `sync` is a relationship operation; `tx`/`batch` are execution primitives; guarantees are listed separately. A separate method exists only when the result shape or the logical intent differs |
| D40 | Archive strategy | `archivable()` defaults to the `column` strategy (M1). The `table` strategy is deferred until archived snapshots can follow schema migrations |
| D39 | Engine requirement | `schema({ requires: { postgres: ">=17" } })`; no user-facing schema version or edition |
| D32 | Inspection | `inspect()`, `sql()`, `explain()`; every implicit behavior carries its source; the internal IR stays private in 0.x |
| D33 | Fingerprints | Deterministic, parameter-free fingerprints for every query |
| D34 | Object identity | Deterministic, stable constraint and index names stored in the catalog; ownership states `managed` / `external` / `ignored` (M2) |
| D35 | Semantics | Behavior of small, dangerous APIs specified up front and tested on every driver |
| D36 | Conformance | Driver conformance suite; migrations verified against an empty database and the previous release's catalog |
| D37 | Non-goals | Identity map, lazy loading, nested writes by default, authorization framework, lowest-common-denominator API, cache in the kernel |
| D41 | Presets naming | Presets stay bare methods (`tasks.pending()`); collisions fail with OKM1040, a reserved-name list protects future methods, and `okm upgrade` renames on collision |
| D42 | Documents | The spec is normative; decisions live in `okmodel-decisions.md`; research in `okmodel-gap-research.md` |
| D43 | Extensions | One `extension()` contract with three sources: built-in (Postgres versions, and outside Postgres such as pgvector and PostGIS), and developer-provided (project file or npm). Listed in `schema({ extensions })` |
| D44 | Extension TS | Types are immediately available from the definition; `okm generate` produces SQL only, never TS |
| D45 | Extension built-ins | Definitions are produced by our CI from real Postgres containers per major version plus a naming overlay, and shipped as source; users never run the generator |
| D46 | Extension lifecycle | add/upgrade/move/remove through migrations with expand/contract classes; no `CASCADE`; no downgrade; owned objects are `external`; `CREATE EXTENSION` runs only from the migration role |
| D47 | Extension settings | Runtime settings are per operation (`SET LOCAL` in a short transaction), not per connection |
| D48 | Extension safety | Extensions add types, operators, functions and index methods only; they cannot alter queries; duplicates and undeclared use fail at build |
| D49 | Database objects | The catalog family includes views, materialized views, functions, triggers, policies and grants, on one contract (kind, identity, owner, canonical definition, column-level dependencies, provenance). Raw SQL is an escape hatch, not a second object system; the contract is frozen only after the M0 catalog-object spike |
| D50 | Views | Materialized views are a separate kind (no replace; refresh and indexes). Views are defined with the query builder or SQL with declared columns; read-only in the client |
| D51 | Functions | `fn()` with signature identity, volatility and typed calls; the same kind as extension functions; plpgsql needs `dependsOn`; `SECURITY DEFINER` needs `search_path` |
| D52 | Triggers | Top-level `triggers` list (one place); traits contribute triggers and functions with provenance |
| D53 | Object planning | Incompatible replace and column changes under a dependent view are planned as drop dependents, alter, recreate; never `CASCADE` |
| D54 | View tenancy | Views over tenant tables inherit tenancy; the tenant key or `global` is required; `security_invoker` by default under `rls`. P83 sets `WITH (security_invoker = true)` on those views and omits it for every other strategy. A materialized view does not set it |
| D55 | Roles and grants | `roles: { migration, app }`; the catalog emits grants and default privileges for the app role |
| D56 | Cancellation | Every operation accepts `{ signal, timeout }`; kind `cancelled`, never retried |
| D57 | Row locks | `find` accepts `lock` and `wait` inside a transaction; advisory locks on `tx` |
| D58 | Sensitive fields | `.sensitive()` redacts values from logs, errors, inspection and fixtures |
| D59 | Backfill | `backfill()` is a planned migration step: batched, resumable, tenant-aware |
| D60 | Materialized views | Materialized views are in M1 with views: they share the dependency, ordering and recreate machinery; they stay a separate kind; populate is a planned data step |
| D61 | Ownership | Ownership (`managed` / `external` / `ignored`) is part of the catalog contract for every object kind from M1; M1 implements `managed` and `external`, M2 adds `ignored` and explicit `owner:` |
| D62 | M0 rule | M0 validates and measures; findings are classified (bug, contradiction, DX, performance, missing capability); no new API surface for imagined edge cases |
| D63 | Driver capabilities | Same semantics, not same features. Dialect (SQL capabilities) and driver (execution capabilities) are separate axes; every driver flag gates the API in types and fails clearly at runtime; every declared capability has a passing conformance test (CI); the compatibility table is generated from test results |
| D64 | Separation | Catalog (`schema()`), connection and routing (`connect()`), migration target (`defineConfig()`) are separate; no connection details in `schema()`; `connect()` first argument is a string or a topology (revised by D90: the registry is `tenancy.registry`) |
| D65 | Namespaces | Every object has an explicit namespace, emitted SQL is always qualified, the default namespace is a recorded setting; namespace is static or a template stored logically in snapshots; no `microservice` concept |
| D66 | Cross-catalog | Other catalogs' objects are `external`, verified against an exported catalog, foreign keys only within one database |
| D67 | Routing | Superseded by D91. Original: default primary, explicit `.replica()`, WAL-position read-your-writes, capability gate, primary-required operations structural in the taxonomy (the last three carry over) |
| D68 | Migration targets | The engine works on a list of primary targets from M1; shared and tenant classes; history and checkpoints per target; new tenants provisioned from the snapshot; migrations never touch replicas |
| D69 | Runner | One `TargetRunner` for `migrate apply` and `backfill()`; run state in the control database |
| D70 | Registry | One tenant registry shared by runtime and tooling; tenant ids are sanitized before entering identifiers. Its shape and place in `connect()` are revised by D90 and D98 |
| D71 | Release trains | M1 ships as trains 0.1–0.4, each tested and published; execution follows `okmodel-execution-plan.md` |
| D72 | Prepared statements | Driver flag `prepared` (`named`, `unnamed`, `none`); fingerprints name statements only where allowed |
| D73 | Catalog hash | The hash is stored in `okm_meta` for an O(1) startup check; a serialised catalog is emitted for cold start |
| D74 | Portable core | No `node:*` in the core, explicit context, `erasableSyntaxOnly`-compatible API, no runtime dependencies, provenance with source locations |
| D75 | Dev experience | `okm dev` uses PGlite when no database is configured; dev N+1 warning from fingerprints; nearest-name hints; `fix` on errors; per-code docs generated from the registry |
| D76 | Agents | `okm catalog export --json` ships; an MCP server for coding agents is deferred (D85) |
| D77 | Plan output | The plan shows locks with row estimates; classification is advisory |
| D78 | Escape ladder | Each escape step keeps the maximum safety it can (rule 15) |
| D79 | Repository checks | `docs:check`, `publint`, `arethetypeswrong`, API report, core purity and size budget run in CI |
| D80 | Observability and PR tooling | `okmodel/otel` and a GitHub Action in M2 |
| D81 | Layers | Five layers with downward-only dependencies (contracts, dialects, adapters, runtime, tooling); enforced by an import-graph check in CI |
| D82 | Driver contract | Public `Driver` contract: `execute` returning `{ rows, count, notices }`, `DriverError`, capability flags; codecs stay in the dialect; conformance covers value representation |
| D83 | Error mapping | Adapters normalise to `DriverError`; the dialect maps it to `OkmError` kinds; drivers never construct `OkmError` |
| D84 | Normalisation | Comparison pipeline introspect, normalise, compare; database-rewritten expressions are compared through a scratch-database round trip, not as text |
| D85 | MCP | Deferred: a separate package outside the core if ever built |
| D86 | Protected targets | `protected` belongs to the Target; blocked operations need `--allow-protected` (OKM1850); not tied to `NODE_ENV`. The exact operation policy is D100 |
| D87 | Execution lifecycle | `timeouts` keys (`acquire`, `statement`, `transaction`, `idleInTransaction`); cancelling inside `tx` rolls back; `onNotice` hook |
| D88 | Testing principles | Behavioural tests on real databases, no database mocks, file checks only for artifacts; capability registry is data (a manifest per dialect and version) |
| D89 | Vocabulary | Target = logical deployment destination (migrations, registry); Topology = one primary plus replicas of a Target; Endpoint = one physical address with a role, owning one pool; Pool; Connection. Chains: Tenant → registry → Target → Topology → Endpoint; Operation → Router → Endpoint → Pool → Connection. Reason: "target" meant both the migration destination and the primary or replica, which would blur the design and the docs. Endpoint is internal, never public API |
| D90 | connect() shapes | `connect()` takes a string or a topology; the tenant registry is not a connection and is passed as `tenancy: { registry }`. Reason: registry, connection and routing are different concerns; a registry as first argument reads as if it were a connection. Revises D64 and D70 |
| D91 | Automatic read routing | Configured replicas automatically take eligible reads; `.primary()` requires the primary; `.replica()` requires a replica and fails with OKM1843 rather than reading the primary (also with no replicas configured); normal reads fall back to the primary or fail (`fallback: "error"`, OKM1844); an operation is routed once; internal read-only transactions follow the operation's routing; only user `tx()` and writes require the primary. Reason: making the developer write `.replica()` on every read defeats configuring replicas; consistency, not a default, is what protects correctness. Supersedes D67 |
| D92 | Replica selection | Candidate filtering (health, consistency and lag, capacity) then a selection strategy (`weighted` default, `roundRobin`, `leastConnections`, `latencyAware`, or a function). Lag is a constraint, not a strategy; weight, in-flight count and latency are strategy inputs |
| D93 | Pools | One pool per endpoint; endpoint selection and connection selection are separate; queries never see URLs, pools or connections; acquire waits up to `timeouts.acquire` then OKM1846 and never spills into another endpoint's pool |
| D94 | Transaction affinity | `tx()` reserves one connection on the primary; statements, savepoints and batches inside stay on it; not subject to replica selection |
| D95 | Commit position | Session watermark = WAL position read after commit on the committing connection (`pg_current_wal_insert_lsn()`, an upper bound, safe); replicas are eligible when their replay position reaches it; replay positions are monotonic, so a cached value is a lower bound; position read failure marks the session position-unknown; root client has a conservative client-wide watermark; `consistency: "session"` default, `"eventual"` and `.replica({ consistency })` opt out; position reading is a dialect/engine capability. M0 verifies the mechanism and its cost. Reason: PostgreSQL returns no commit LSN to clients, so the honest mechanism is stated and measured, not assumed **Confirmed by M0, wording amended (D121).** |
| D96 | Atomic batch | Atomic `batch` is part of the Driver contract, not a capability: all-or-nothing, ordered results, failing index, whole-batch timeout and cancellation, savepoint inside `tx()`, primary-required; an adapter that cannot provide it is not a driver; conformance `batch.atomic` runs on every adapter. Reason: the same API must not have weaker atomicity on one driver |
| D97 | Outcome unknown | A commit or batch whose result never arrived raises `outcome_unknown` (OKM1401, category `internal`, never retried), never a claim of rollback. Reason: automatic retry could apply a write twice |
| D98 | Target resolution | Plans and run state hold Target names, never connection details; each Target is resolved at execution time by its resolver; pools are keyed by resolved configuration and capped with LRU eviction; an authentication failure re-resolves once (credential rotation); the registry resolves per role (`app`, `migration`) and may `create(id)` databases |
| D99 | Provisioning | A new Target is provisioned from the head snapshot on an empty target (`migrate apply`, `provision()`), without replaying history; `okm migrate check` proves snapshot ≡ replayed history (OKM1521); required rows are declared with the table option `reference` (insert-if-missing, `expand`); other data DML in migrations is flagged (OKM1542); non-empty targets are refused (OKM1851). Reason: history replay would drop reference data and re-run backfills against empty tenants |
| D100 | Protection policy | One `assertTargetPolicy` for the CLI, engine, backfill, seed, runner and provision. Allowed: read-only commands, `expand`, `reference`, `provision` on an empty target. Blocked: `contract`, unclassified steps, `push`, `backfill`, `seed`, history repair, unless `--allow-protected` (per invocation, no environment variable, selected targets only). Protection never affects runtime `connect()` |
| D101 | Placement | The Target / Topology / Endpoint / Pool / Router runtime, one pool per endpoint, transaction affinity, the atomic batch contract, the resolver, the protection policy and snapshot provisioning are M1 foundations from the first train; replica behaviour (routing, selection, position, `connect({ primary, replicas })`) is train 0.5 inside M1 (no longer M2); the registry, multi-target runner and tenant provisioning API stay M5 |
| D102 | Driver contract shape | `open(config)` returns a `DriverPool` per endpoint (`execute`, `batch`, `reserve`, `stats`, `close`); a reserved `DriverConnection` carries every transaction; pool statistics feed selection strategies |
| D103 | Migration failure | A migration is atomic in one transaction where PostgreSQL allows it; steps that cannot be transactional are split out by the planner and marked; every step checkpoints in the target's history; a failed run stops at the failing step and a later `apply` resumes without repeating finished steps (a failed concurrent index is dropped and rebuilt); `apply` holds a session-level advisory lock per target, a second concurrent apply fails with OKM1522. Reason: partial failure is the normal case on large databases and must be resumable and non-racing |
| D104 | Forward-only | No down migrations. Recovery is a new forward migration or an application rollback, which `expand` keeps safe; `contract` runs only after the new release is confirmed. Reason: down scripts are rarely correct on real data |
| D105 | Rollout control | With many targets: `--target`, `--class`, `--canary <n>`, `--concurrency <n>` (defaults 2 for schema-per-tenant, 8 for database-per-tenant), `--max-failures <n>` (default 3, halts a migration that breaks every tenant); `okm migrate status` shows every target's version and state (single-target status ships in M1). Reason: staged rollout and early halt are how a fleet-wide migration is made safe |
| D106 | Contract gating | A `contract` step applies to a tenant only if it has every earlier migration, and to the shared target only after it succeeded on every tenant in scope. Reason: no tenant may reference something already removed **Amended by D123 (gating over the whole registry).** |
| D107 | Second pass | After a multi-target run the runner reads the registry again and processes tenants created during the run |
| D108 | Environments and previews | An environment is a named Target: `targets` in `defineConfig` (M1; `database` is the shorthand for `default`), the tenant registry is `tenants`; with several targets `--target` is required (OKM1853); an unprotected target aliasing a protected database is refused (OKM1852). Previews are ephemeral databases created by infrastructure and filled by `migrate apply` from the snapshot (plus optional seed); rehearsal is applying pending migrations to a clone of production; OKModel never drops a database. Reason: nothing about an environment is inferred from `NODE_ENV` or a URL, and a preview must not be able to act on production by omission **Amended by D122 (protection per physical database).** |
| D109 | Repository conventions | The package `okmodel` is the repository root; other packages under `packages/*` (private ones for spikes, reference app and benchmarks); `files` whitelist controls the tarball; repository owner `omqkhafi`, copyright Omq Khafi, Apache-2.0; every commit carries exactly the DCO sign-off (`Omq Khafi <omqkhafi@gmail.com>`) and the Ali Alnaghmoush co-author trailer, nothing else. Reason: heavy spike and benchmark dependencies must stay out of the published package, and attribution must be deliberate |
| D110 | Name and npm package | The project is named **OKModel** (short **OKM**), published as the unscoped npm package `okmodel`, repository `omqkhafi/okmodel`; CLI bins `okm` and `okmodel` (two thin wrapper files, `dist/okm.js` and `dist/okmodel.js`, written by `scripts/write-bin-wrappers.ts`, because `bun pm pack` lists a file twice when both bins point at one file); error class `OkmError`, codes `OKM1xxx`, config `okm.config.ts`, generated folder `.okm/`, metadata table `okm_meta`. Reason: the name is registrable on npm, short and neutral about the feature set |
| D111 | Toolchain: TypeScript 7 | The repository is fully TypeScript 7 (native compiler, `tsc`; nightly builds are `@typescript/native-preview`/`tsgo`): typecheck, declaration emit, editor. TypeScript 7.0 (released 2026-08-03) has no stable programmatic API until 7.1, so no OKModel tool uses the compiler API; type tests and type measurement follow D114, and the M0 spikes record TypeScript 7 results for editor-facing checks (autocomplete, `Register`, circularity). Lint and format use a tool that does not need the compiler API (Biome or oxlint, chosen in P01). TypeScript 7 defaults apply (`strict`, stable type ordering, no `baseUrl`, no legacy module resolution). Reason: one compiler for the user-facing experience, and no hidden dependency on an API that is still unstable |
| D112 | Module format | The package is ESM only, with TypeScript declarations from `tsc`. There is no CommonJS build and no dual-package hazard; CommonJS consumers use dynamic `import()` or `require()` of ES modules where their Node version supports it. The attw profile is `esm-only` (`node10` and `node16`-from-CJS resolutions are expected to be ignored). Reason: the core is runtime-agnostic and the catalog identity must not exist twice in one process |
| D113 | Versioning and changelog | Every merged prompt adds notes under `## Unreleased` in `changelog.md` and bumps the version with `bun run bump next`, which moves `package.json` to `<next release>-next.N` (for example `0.1.0-next.3`); the gate prompt of a release train ends with `bun run bump release`, which drops the suffix and promotes `Unreleased` to a dated heading. The first releases are 0.1.0 at P17, 0.2.0 at P30, 0.3.0 at P44, 0.4.0 at P55 and 0.5.0 at P66. Only released versions are published and tagged. CI fails a pull request whose version equals the one on `main` or whose `changelog.md` has no new entry. The `okm-ship` skill writes the notes and ends with `bun run bump next`; `bun run bump` gains the `next` and `release` kinds next to `patch`, `minor`, `major` and `--set`. Changelog area headings use the plain layer names (`contracts`, `dialects`, `adapters`, `runtime`, `tooling`, `docs`). Reason: the version and the notes must follow the work without anyone remembering to update them, and pre-release versions do not pretend to be patches of a release that has not happened |
| D114 | Type tests and type measurement | Type correctness is tested with `expect-type` style assertions (`expectTypeOf`) in `*.test-d.ts` files that the normal `tsc` typecheck compiles, so a wrong type fails the existing check on TypeScript 7 without a second runner. Type cost (instantiations, check time) is measured from the compiler's own diagnostics (`tsc --extendedDiagnostics`, and a trace when needed) by a script that runs each benchmark query on the 10, 50 and 200-table fixtures and compares with recorded ceilings. `@ark/attest` needs the TypeScript compiler API, which TypeScript 7.0 does not offer; it is used only if it runs on the TypeScript 6 compatibility package in a separate job, and is dropped otherwise. Vitest is not adopted for this: its type mode calls `tsc` and parses the output and does not measure type cost. P02 records which diagnostics TypeScript 7 actually reports, and the ceilings are set from M0 evidence. Reason: type experience is the product, so it is measured and tested with tools that work on the compiler we build with |
| D115 | Hygiene rounds | After P09 a cleanup prompt (P09A) removes spike leftovers, dedupes helpers, stabilises flaky tests and syncs docs. Every later gate (P17, P30, P44, P55, P66) ends with a lighter hygiene step before the release bump. Reason: spike code and workarounds accumulate across prompt chains and are cheapest to remove right after the evidence is recorded |
| D116 | M0 gate outcome | M0 evidence (`docs/m0-findings.md`, 68 findings) passes the gate. The catalog contract is frozen: one envelope (`kind, identity, owner, definition, dependencies, provenance`), per-kind identity as spec §5.7, declared `dependsOn` for plpgsql bodies (OKM1824), `BEGIN ATOMIC` bodies inferred, copied partition objects dropped on introspection. Findings that need a decision are D117–D127; the rest are implementation tasks already placed in the plan |
| D117 | Hash form | The drift hash is over the normalised structure, never over authoring text. View, materialized view and function bodies are judged by a scratch reprint (the server prints both sides). Reason: M0-01 and M0-34 showed text hashes drift on harmless reformatting |
| D118 | Extensions | Extension version is unpinned by default (installed version recorded for information, ignored by drift unless pinned). The raise-version path is checked against `pg_extension_update_paths` at plan time when a server is attached; offline plans mark the step `path-unverified` and apply preflight checks before any step. A non-relocatable extension `SET SCHEMA` is refused at planning (OKM1814). Members found through `pg_depend` deptype `e` are excluded from introspection (never loaded, never diffed); objects declared in `provides` stay `external`. Reason: M0-40, M0-41, M0-47 |
| D119 | Roles and grants | Roles are cluster objects: a role named in `roles` must exist and is `external` by default (checked by `okm doctor`). A declared managed role is created if missing, altered in place, and never dropped by a plan. Default privileges are keyed `(forRole, namespace, objectKind, grantee, privilege)` and emitted `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE <migration role>`. Migrations run as the migration role (login, or one `SET ROLE` at session start recorded in the run report, never inside a plan); `CREATEROLE` is checked in doctor and apply preflight. Grant identity is a structured key exempt from the 63-byte rule. Reason: M0-42, M0-45, M0-48 |
| D120 | Row types emitted by default | `okm build`/`okm dev` emit row types into `.okm/types.d.ts` (config `types: "emitted" or "inferred"`, default emitted); inferred is the zero-build mode; both give the same shapes (CI `expectTypeOf` test). Emitted names derive from table names (`Tasks`, `TasksInsert`, `TasksUpdate`); duplicate table names are rejected (OKM1023), unknown references reported at build (OKM1021). Reason: M0-08, M0-09, M0-12: emitted costs 0 consumer instantiations against 53,410 inferred at 200 tables (43,676 unbranded; ~250 per table). Supersedes the D25 wording; hover quality is unmeasured (D111), so P12 adds a manual editor check |
| D121 | Commit position confirmed | The after-commit position read is confirmed by M0 (`pg_current_wal_insert_lsn()` after commit is 40 bytes past the start of a 34-byte commit record; reading inside the transaction gives the record start, which a replica can reach before applying the commit; `pg_current_wal_lsn()` lags under `synchronous_commit=off`; 0 read-your-writes violations). Cost is one extra statement per write; pipelining (`COMMIT; SELECT ...` as one simple-query message) is optional and evaluated in P13/P63. Fallback rate under write load is measured in P64. Reason: M0-51, M0-55 |
| D122 | Protection per physical database | Protection belongs to the physical database. Under `schemaPerTenant` all tenants share one database so protection must be uniform (mixed gives OKM1852); per-tenant protection requires `databasePerTenant`. Targets that resolve to the same host/port/database must have the same protection; two unprotected targets may share a database. Amends D108. Reason: M0-58, M0-59, M0-63 |
| D123 | Contract gating over the whole registry | A shared `contract` step runs only when every registered tenant has the migration. Any partial scope (`--canary`, `--class`, `--target` subset) skips contract steps and reports "contract pending (N tenants not at this migration)". Amends D106. Reason: M0-61, M0-62 showed canary and `--class shared` ran the shared contract too early |
| D124 | Driver contract clarifications | Every timeout (statement, transaction, batch) is kind `timeout`; `cancelled` is a separate kind, never retried, and only arises from the caller's `signal`. `batchIndex` is `number` or `null` (null when failure occurs at commit, e.g. deferred constraint; the whole batch is rolled back). `DriverConnection.batch` on a connection with an open transaction uses a savepoint and never commits the outer transaction. Release resets session state (`RESET ALL`, `pg_advisory_unlock_all()`, not `DISCARD ALL`), with a CI test that a custom setting does not survive release. Reason: M0-28, M0-29, M0-30, M0-54 |
| D125 | Filters and presets | `eq(value)` is the equality form for object (json/jsonb) values; a bare object stays OKM1121 and its fix names `eq`. Preset `where` appends (AND) and the preset builder exposes only additive methods. Caller filters are recorded with provenance `caller` before presets run, so contributions can add but never remove predicates. Reason: M0-14, M0-15 |
| D126 | Safety errors | OKM1190 carries `violations` (every violated rule in stable sorted order, each naming rule and contribution; `rule`/`contribution` repeat the first). OKM1122 (runtime) rejects bad identifiers (length, NUL, control characters, unquoted reserved word). OKM1123 (build) rejects allowlisting a hidden field in a filter or sort allowlist. Reason: M0-16, M0-17, M0-18 |
| D127 | Budgets and runtime-neutral core | Type ceilings from M0: inferred 200 tables ≤ ~61,000 instantiations / ≤ ~9,700 types; 500 tables proportional ≤ ~140,000; ≤ 300 instantiations per added table; emitted consumer ≤ ~700 types; tagged-operator surcharge ≤ ~800 instantiations; check time reported, not ceilinged. Size budgets (first, finalised at P17): runtime entry ≤ 60 KB min / ≤ 20 KB gzip at 0.1 and ≤ 120 KB min / ≤ 40 KB gzip at 0.5; cold import ≤ ~15 ms on Node on the reference machine; ratchet at measured +25%, raised only by decision; `sideEffects: false`; no barrel that drags drivers into the runtime entry; a bundle-purity CI check (no npm package inside runtime bundles). Core hashing is pure-TypeScript SHA-256 in L0 (no `Bun`/Node globals). `schema()` build versus `.okm/catalog.json` load is measured in P12. Reason: M0 cold start/size tables (bundle 649 KB only because the harness barrel pulled pglite and postgres; catalog hash used `Bun.CryptoHasher`) |
| D128 | Catalog error codes | A dependency cycle in the catalog is OKM1026 and a catalog document this version cannot read (unknown version, malformed) is OKM1027; OKM1020 stays "unknown table". Tenant-id sanitizer failures are OKM1122 (identifier rejection), not OKM1120. Check expressions and index predicates are stored as text until P16 normalises them through the database, and renames do not rewrite that text (declared renames in P16 handle references). Found in P10 |
| D129 | Engineering standards | Every prompt is built for the best performance, speed, lightness and cold start, not only to pass tests. Rules: nothing is added to the runtime entry without need (tooling stays out of it, `sideEffects: false`, lazy work, no module-level computation); no duplicated logic (one helper, one place); small, obvious public API with clear names, precise types and actionable errors; no avoidable allocation or repeated work on hot paths; every report states runtime entry size, cold import, and any type-cost change; each prompt ends with a self-review against these rules before the report. Budgets in D127 stay the hard limits; these rules are the habit that keeps us under them. Added by Ali on 2026-10-02 |
| D130 | Column definition and codec errors | An invalid column definition (length, precision, scale, array rank, interval qualifier, empty or duplicate picklist or enum values) is OKM1060 at schema construction; a value a codec rejects is OKM1210 at runtime (1200 is the validation range). Messages name the accepted values. No plain `Error` is thrown for these. Found in P11 |
| D131 | Removing a picklist or enum value | The schema never records a replacement for a removed value. `okm generate` detects removals against the last snapshot and requires `--replace table.column.old=new` (`=null` for a nullable column); without it, OKM1541 with a `fix` naming the flag. No prompt: the planner never prompts (spec 19.1), which also keeps CI and coding agents working. The mapping is written into the migration as a `backfill()` step (the sanctioned data statement, so OKM1542 is not triggered): expand moves existing rows and keeps the old constraint; contract does a final sweep and swaps the constraint (`NOT VALID` then `VALIDATE`). `t.enum` follows the same rule with a type recreate in contract. A temporary rewrite trigger that would close the rollout window completely (old release writes the removed value after the expand backfill) is deferred: the window is documented and the contract sweep cleans it. Note: `ADD CONSTRAINT … NOT VALID` takes a brief ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock, so the runner applies it with `lock_timeout` and retry; `VALIDATE` takes SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE and does not block writes. Open follow-up: `.renamedFrom()` still lives in the schema until `okm check` reports it stale; whether renames should follow the same principle is a separate decision |
| D132 | Reserved options and reference names | An option that is in the types but not available in this version (validate, traits, tenancy, relations, computed, presets, policies, reference, views, functions, triggers) throws OKM1061 naming the prompt or milestone that adds it (OKM1060 stays for invalid column definitions). `.references()` takes a plain table-name string: a generic table-name argument cycles through `Register` (P12 measured the blow-up). Unknown names fail at construction (OKM1020); with emitted types the emitted file supplies the table-name union to `references`, wired when `okm build` exists (P16). Found in P12 |
| D133 | Gated measurements and ratchet after P12 | Type cost is gated on what a consumer pays: fixtures compiled against the built declarations with `skipLibCheck`; source-based numbers are reported only. Ceilings ratchet to measured +25% (D127 rule): inferred 200 tables ≤ 17,300 instantiations / ≤ 6,500 types; 500 tables ≤ 42,400 instantiations; ≤ 84 instantiations per added table; emitted consumer ≤ 700 types (kept); column sample ≤ 720 instantiations / ≤ 1,100 types. The tagged-operator surcharge keeps its source-based ceiling (800) because the declaration number is 0. The package `dist/` byte ceiling (unminified, install size) moves from 184,000 to 241,000 because `table()`, `schema()` and `emitRowTypes` joined the Postgres bundle; the figures that matter for startup are the app-bundle ceilings: 10 tables with 8 column types, tree-shaken, ≤ 48,000 bytes min / ≤ 15,800 gzip / cold import ≤ 15 ms (D127). The 0.1 runtime budget (60 KB min, D127) is already 38 KB for core + schema; watch it in P13–P16 and finalise at P17. The serialised-catalog loader must trust a build artifact (stored version and hash, no re-validation, validation only in `okm check` and dev): measured 8.2 ms to load vs 7.0 ms to build, of which 7.1 ms is validation and object building; implemented with `okm build` in P16 |
| D134 | Cold-import gate on CI | The 15 ms cold-import budget (D127) is measured on the reference machine (a developer laptop). Shared CI runners are 2–3 times slower and noisy (P12: 15.0 and 15.6 ms on a runner against 5 ms locally), so the CI gate is 25 ms on the median of five fresh imports; the local run still prints against 15 ms and any local figure above 15 ms is a finding. Size ceilings are unaffected (bytes do not vary by machine). Found in P12 |
| D135 | Entry ceilings, dist informational, capability error | The unminified `dist/` size is reported but no longer gated (it sat 821 bytes under its ceiling after P13 and would have needed a raise every prompt); each published entry has its own gate at measured +25%: `okmodel/pg/postgresjs` ≤ 12,600 bytes min / ≤ 4,500 gzip, `okmodel/pg/pglite` ≤ 10,700 / ≤ 3,900, `okmodel/pg` ≤ 66,900 / ≤ 20,900; the runtime entry (60 KB / 20 KB, D127) and the app fixture (D133) keep theirs. Adapter cold imports (7.9 and 10.1 ms) are dominated by the driver packages and are reported, not gated. A dynamic call of an operation the driver's capabilities do not allow throws OKM1111 (runtime, 1100 range), implemented with its first consumer in P15; OKM1110 stays the engine-version gate. The postgres.js adapter uses the unnamed protocol (`prepared: "unnamed"`), which stays correct behind poolers in transaction mode; a named-statement option for direct connections is evaluated in P15 with a benchmark. Found in P13 |
| D136 | App-fixture budget and entry gates after P14 | What an application ships is the app fixture (`okmodel` + `okmodel/pg` used parts + errors + whatever is added), so the 0.1 budget of D127 (60 KB min / 20 KB gzip, cold import 15 ms) applies to that fixture, not only to the runtime entry; the interim gate moves from 48,000 / 15,800 to 60,000 / 20,000 and P15 and P15B must fit inside it or stop and report a breakdown so that a reasoned budget can be decided with evidence, not guessed. P14 numbers: runtime entry 24,389 min / 8,568 gzip (+6.4 KB for `OkmError`); app fixture 45,445 / 14,875. The adapter ceilings P14 set from its own measurement (postgres.js ≤ 19,850 / 6,870, PGlite ≤ 17,870 / 6,230) are accepted as interim because `OkmError` is in those standalone bundles (acquire timeout is an `OkmError`); the published package shares one copy (bundle-purity). `OkmError` identity must exist once per process, as the catalog does. Found in P14 |
| D137 | Size gate at P15: optimize first, then staged ceilings | P15 stopped as D136 required: the app fixture with `connect` and one `find` measured 87,179 min / 27,620 gzip against 60,000 / 20,000, and `okmodel/pg` 68,427 / 21,358 against 66,900 / 20,900 (cold import 10.8 ms, fine). The biggest source modules on the path were `runtime/plan.ts` (41 KB), the postgres.js adapter (15 KB), `runtime/client.ts` (13 KB), SQLSTATE mapping plus driver errors (16 KB), tagged operators (7 KB), adapter batch (5 KB) and two row-decode paths. The interim 60,000 / 20,000 was a guess made before a planner existed, so it is replaced by evidence, but not by the raw number: P15 first runs a D129 optimisation pass on the read path (error mapping and the include planner load lazily on first failure or first include; tagged operators are one function per operator so unused ones tree-shake; adapter batch stays out of the read path until P15B/P29; no routing or topology code in `client.ts` before P60; one row-decode path; capability flags only for what is checked; no eager registries), and measures first-query latency next to cold import so lazy loading cannot hide a cost. The app-fixture gate is then set to the measured size plus 3% and may not exceed a hard cap of 75,000 min / 24,000 gzip; above the cap P15 stops again with a before and after per lever. P15B may add at most 12,000 min / 3,800 gzip over the P15 gate (one more stop-and-report if it cannot), and P17 finalises the 0.1 app budget at the measured size with the D133 ratchet (lower it whenever measured 5% or more under, never raise without a decision). `okmodel/pg` is the everything-exported entry, so it moves to measured +3% (≤ 70,500 / 22,000 unless the pass lowers it); each export must stay tree-shakable, which the app fixture proves. The public `connect` entries (`okmodel/pg/postgresjs` 47,694 / 15,263, `okmodel/pg/pglite` 45,733 / 14,673, driver external) get gates at measured +5% after the pass. The D135 standalone adapter gates stay. The named-statement benchmark runs in P15; the option ships only if it is at least 15% faster at p50 on repeated queries over a direct connection and costs 300 bytes min or less, otherwise the negative result is recorded and the unnamed protocol stays the only mode. Found in P15 |
| D138 | Startup-graph gate, CI app cold import, named prepares | After D137's lazy loading, the app-fixture gate measures the startup graph (what loads before the first successful query: 72,669 min / 23,980 gzip, gate 74,688 / 24,000); the total graph with the lazy chunks is reported next to it and not gated, so a lever cannot hide bytes. P15B may add at most 12,000 / 3,800 over that gate (86,688 / 27,800). The app fixture's CI cold import (34.4 ms; earlier samples 31 and 20.7 ms, 10.2 ms local) includes `schema()` work at import (pure-TS SHA-256, catalog build) and CI variance, so it is printed and not a failure; the 25 ms CI gate (D134) stays on the runtime entry and any local app figure above 15 ms is a finding. Lever recorded for P16: compute the catalog hash and serialisation lazily on first use instead of inside `schema()`, evaluated together with the fast catalog loader. First-include latency (dynamic import plus planning) is reported from P15B. Named prepares ship as the option `prepared: "named"` (43 to 70% faster at p50 over a direct connection, about 110 bytes min); unnamed stays the default, and the docs must say named statements are not for transaction-mode poolers. Found in P15 |
| D139 | P15B outcomes, editor check tool, P16 split | (1) Multi-statement writes (chunked inserts) run on the driver's atomic `batch`, so begin, commit, savepoint and `outcome_unknown` stay in the adapter and no second transaction path exists; this is the internal runner of P15B. The interactive reserved-connection runner (code between statements) does not exist yet and is built in P29 with the public `tx`. (2) TypeScript 7 has no language server, so `bun run editor-check` uses a dev-only TypeScript 6 package over stdio; it is never imported or bundled, and it is revisited when TS 7 ships one. (3) P15B gates: app startup graph 74,218 / 24,471 measured, gate 76,444 / 25,205; total graph 103,117 / 33,314 reported; `okmodel/pg` 69,135 / 21,558 under 70,500 / 22,000. (4) The type-cost write probe on 200 tables is 6,451 types against the 6,500 ceiling (49 left): any prompt that gets within 3% of a ceiling reports it, and ceilings are raised only by decision (the inferred worst case is not what a default emitted-types consumer pays, D120). (5) Completions inside an `insert` object offer only the property under the cursor because the single and array overloads do not contextually type the object; P16 starts by finding a signature that lists the insertable columns, or records the gap in `docs/editor-check.md`. (6) P16 was too large for one reviewable prompt and is split: P16 is the planning side (introspect, normalise, diff, generate, build, check), P16B the applying side (apply, history, `okm_meta`, targets, protected policy, startup check). Found in P15B |
| D140 | P16 outcomes: config import path, enum as a catalog object, composite probe ceiling | (1) `defineConfig` is exported from `okmodel/migrate` (tooling), not from `okmodel`, so tooling stays out of the runtime bundle; spec §3.1 now imports it from there, and P17 reviews every public subpath name before the 0.1 freeze. (2) Enum is a catalog object, not a side channel: kind `type` (identity `(namespace, name)`, already in the §5.7 table), ordered labels in the canonical definition, a dependency edge from each column that uses it, introspection from `pg_type` and `pg_enum`, planning with `CREATE TYPE`, `ADD VALUE` as a marked non-transactional step and the D131 recreate for removal. P16 stored labels in `.okm/declarations.json` and `schema().catalog` still raises OKM1020 for an enum column, so `t.enum` does not work end to end; a new step P16A, before P16B, fixes it and removes the side channel. (3) The query composite probe on 200 tables (find, filter, include, orderBy and writes) now has its own types ceiling of 7,100 (measured 6,478, about +10%), separate from the inferred-schema baseline (5,199 of 6,500, unchanged); any prompt that grows it by more than 3% reports it, and instantiations stay under 17,300; further growth is by decision (D133). (4) The lazy catalog hash is accepted as sound design, but P16 did not measure a before and after (CI app cold import 25.2 ms, local 10.1 ms against 2.1 ms for the runtime entry), so P17 profiles app cold import by module and fixes the largest item. (5) Type cost of emitted types is measured on the real output of `okm build` in P16A (consumer ceiling 700, D133). Found in P16 |
| D141 | P16A outcomes, domain gap, barrel gate | (1) Enum is a catalog object (D140 done): kind `type`, `CREATE TYPE`, `ADD VALUE` as a marked non-transactional step, recreate for a removed or reordered label, no side channel. Emitted 200-table consumer 559 types (ceiling 700); composite probe 6,479 types and 15,402 instantiations (ceilings 7,100 and 17,300). (2) Domains are still outside the catalog and `schema().catalog` raises OKM1020 for a domain column, the same class of gap as enums were; a domain becomes a `type` subkind in P40 (objects train), and until then P17 makes every not-yet-supported builder (`t.domain` among them) fail with a clear error naming the version that will support it, listed in the docs. (3) `okmodel/pg` is now 70,349 / 21,921 against 70,500 / 22,000 (151 and 79 bytes left) and the app startup graph 75,421 / 24,829 against 76,444 / 25,205. P16B must not grow `okmodel/pg`; the app startup gate may be re-set to measured +3% only inside the D138 cap of 86,688 / 27,800, and above it P16B stops and reports. P17 decides whether the `okmodel/pg` barrel gate stays or is replaced by a per-export tree-shake test plus the app-fixture gate, because every new operator or builder forces a decision on a barrel that only counts how many features exist. Found in P16A |
| D142 | P16B outcomes, startup check, connect-entry budgets | (1) Apply replays migration files; provisioning an empty target from the head snapshot (§19.6) is P53A, D123 contract gating and the rollout flags (`--class`, `--canary`, `--concurrency`, `--max-failures`) wait for the tenant registry, and D131 data steps run as plain statements until P52 batches them. (2) The stored catalog hash is read in the first dialect query through `query_to_xml`, so the statement plans even when `okm_meta` does not exist; a database with no recorded hash skips the drift check so existing databases can adopt OKModel, and P17 decides whether an explicit option makes a missing `okm_meta` OKM1520 in production. (3) Measured: app startup 76,933 / 25,444 (gate 79,240 / 26,207, cap 86,688 / 27,800), total graph 111,410 / 36,001; connect-entry gates are tied to the app startup gate and move only in the prompt that moves it, by the shared-code delta (postgres.js 39,369 / 13,671, PGlite 37,691 / 13,348); `okmodel/pg` unchanged at 151 and 79 bytes under its gate. (4) CI cold import of a connect entry includes the driver package (PGlite 24.67 ms, 1.3% under a 25 ms line), so it is reported and not gated; the 25 ms CI gate stays on the runtime entry (4.7 ms) and the 15 ms local reference applies to our own code with the driver stubbed, measured in P17. (5) The app startup budget is a per-release number: P17 sets the 0.1 budget at the measured size after a trim attempt, each release gate (P30, P44, P55, P66) proposes the next from per-feature estimates, and the D127 figure of 60 KB / 20 KB stays the runtime-entry target. (6) Refusing `push` on a target named `production` is a name heuristic, and spec §19.8 says nothing about an environment is inferred from `NODE_ENV` or a name: P17 removes the heuristic, so `push` is refused only on a target declared `protected` (and documents that production targets must be declared protected). P17 is split: P17 (hygiene, budgets, API surface freeze, unsupported-builder errors) and P17B (docs, quickstart test, recipes, compatibility table, release checklist, 0.1.0 dry run). Found in P16B |
| D143 | 0.1 budgets, API freeze rules, requireMeta | (1) 0.1 budgets, set at measured +3% (D142): app startup 77,524 / 25,641 measured, gate 79,849 / 26,410; connect entries postgres.js 39,849 / 13,815 and PGlite 38,146 / 13,505 (measured +5%); adapter incremental bytes over the runtime entry 12,074 of 15,093 and 9,608 of 12,010 (+25%); runtime entry 24,426 / 8,584 against 60 KB / 20 KB; total graph 112,001 / 36,162 printed. The `okmodel/pg` barrel (70,046 / 21,872) is printed, not gated: a per-export tree-shake test plus the app fixture are the gates, and P17B confirms that test exists and fails when an export pulls in the whole barrel. (2) `requireMeta` (default off) makes a missing `okm_meta` OKM1520 in `connect()`; nothing is inferred from `NODE_ENV` or a target name (§19.8), so a production setup declares `protected: true` on the target and sets `requireMeta: true`, and the docs say so. (3) Subpaths stay as they are (`okmodel`, `okmodel/pg`, `okmodel/pg/postgresjs`, `okmodel/pg/pglite`, `okmodel/migrate`); the empty `okmodel/testing` entry leaves the exports map until 0.4 and the name is reserved in the docs. (4) Before the freeze every export is classified stable, experimental or internal: exports of `okmodel` that only sibling entries or dialect authors need (`sha256`, `throwNamed`, `nearestName`, `catalogError`, the catalog builders, whose names `table` and `index` duplicate the ones in `okmodel/pg`) are not the application API; internals move behind an undocumented `okmodel/internal` subpath only if no bundle grows, and an API snapshot test per subpath makes an accidental export fail CI. (5) Reserved-option versions follow the plan: `manyThrough` is 0.2 (P27), not unassigned; the error text is corrected and the known-limits page lists `.hidden()`, `.sensitive()`, `.validate()` and every other reserved item by version. (6) Docs state measured numbers only (startup graph, install size, cold import, with the CI source); no size or speed claim without a number. (7) The 0.2 starting point for P30 is 91,000 min / 30,000 gzip on the startup graph (tenancy +2 KB, traits +3 KB, presets +1.5 KB, archive +2 KB, field exposure +1.5 KB; transactions and validation load on first use); it is above the D138 cap, which ends with 0.1, and P30 re-estimates it. Found in P17 |
| D144 | P17B outcomes: internal exports, docs as tests, runtime-entry gate | (1) The API surface is classified in `tests/fixtures/api-surface.json` and a new or missing export fails CI; `okmodel` now exports 43 stable names and `okmodel/internal` 69 internal ones, which cut the `okmodel` entry from 24,426 / 8,584 to 5,525 / 2,042 with the app startup graph unchanged (77,537 / 25,625). Rule: an internal export never sits on a public subpath, because editor completions show it on day one and removing it later is a breaking change; `okmodel/pg` still carries 10 internal exports (such as `mapPostgresError`, `emitRowTypes`, `compileColumn`) and `okmodel/migrate` 28, so a short step P17C moves them out before 0.1.0 is tagged, and `okmodel/migrate` keeps only `defineConfig` and its types; the CLI is part of the package and imports its helpers directly. `okmodel/internal` has no stability promise, which the README and the known-limits page say, and its exports carry `@internal`. (2) Docs are tests: the quickstart test extracts the commands from the docs file and runs them verbatim (`okm build`, `okm generate`, `okm migrate apply`, then the read and write calls), so the quickstart cannot drift from what runs; P17B's test applied the generated SQL by hand and so did not exercise `apply`. (3) With the move the runtime entry is 5,525 / 2,042 and its 60 KB / 20 KB gate no longer constrains anything: the app startup graph is the real budget; the runtime-entry gate ratchets to measured +10% (6,100 / 2,250), and the CI cold-import gate of 25 ms stays on it. (4) The release is done by Ali from `docs/release.md` (tag `v0.1.0`, then the release workflow, which refuses any other ref); the workflow can also be run from the GitHub Actions page. Found in P17B |
| D145 | P17C outcomes and the 0.2 operator set | (1) Public subpaths now carry only public API (`okmodel/pg` no longer exports `compileColumn`, `emitRowTypes`, `mapPostgresError` or the operator tag helpers; `okmodel/migrate` exports `defineConfig` and its types); the `okmodel/pg` barrel is 62,063 / 19,409, the runtime-entry gate 6,100 / 2,250, app startup unchanged at 77,537 / 25,625 (gate 79,849 / 26,410). Ali tags `v0.1.0` after merging from `docs/release.md`; the release workflow needs npm credentials or trusted publishing configured, which the dry run cannot prove. (2) The quickstart and recipe tests run against an in-process Postgres wire server in `check`, because that job has no Docker database; P20 also runs the quickstart test in the `postgres` job against real Postgres. (3) The 0.2 operator set is defined in spec §10.1 (draft 24): type-directed helpers `contains`, `containedBy`, `overlaps`, `hasKey`, `hasAnyKey`, `path(segments, op)` and `matches(q, { mode?, config? })`, plus the atomic write operators `json.set`, `arr.append`, `arr.remove` as namespace exports; a mismatch between operator and column type is OKM1124 at runtime and a type error at compile time. Extension operators (trigram, citext) move from P20 to P40, and text search on a plain `text` column is not supported in 0.2. (4) P20 keeps one function per operator (tree-shaken, barrel not gated), the tagged-operator source surcharge stays under 800 (639 now), and the composite probe may grow by at most 3% (6,479 of 7,100 now) or P20 reports per-operator contribution and stops (D133, D140). Found in P17C |
| D146 | Release 0.1.0 and how it publishes | `okmodel@0.1.0` is on npm with `latest` pointing at it and a signed provenance statement. The release workflow publishes with `npm publish` (Node 24, npm 11.5.1 or newer) through an npm trusted publisher for `omqkhafi/okmodel`, workflow `release.yml`, no environment, and stores no npm token: `bun publish` cannot do the OIDC exchange (oven-sh/bun#22423) and failed twice with `missing authentication`, which a re-run could not fix because `workflow_dispatch` uses the workflow file on the selected ref. The `v0.1.0` tag was moved once onto the commit that fixed the workflow (16e475d, pushed to `main` directly), which was safe only because nothing had been published; a published tag is never moved. The package is still built with Bun (`prepublishOnly` stays `bun run build`). npm 11 prints that it removed `bin[okm]` and `bin[okmodel]`, which is its rewrite of a leading `./`; the published manifest must keep both commands, checked with `npm view okmodel@0.1.0 bin` and a run of `okm` from a fresh install. Hardening to do: set the package's publishing access to the option that requires two-factor authentication and disallows bypass tokens (trusted publishing still works), delete any stray npm token in the repository secrets, and add a tag ruleset for `v*` (and a release environment with approval once there are other contributors), because a trusted publisher trusts any run of `release.yml` in the repository. After the tag, `bun run bump next` must move the version to the next release's `-next.N` and open a new changelog heading; P20 verifies that. Found in the 0.1.0 release |
| D147 | npm README is per version | The README on npmjs.com is the one inside each published version, and relative links in it do not resolve (the quickstart, production checklist and known-limits links showed red on the 0.1.0 page, and the page told a new reader nothing about how to use the package). So the README must be self-contained: install, a runnable quickstart with the real commands, the command list, what 0.1 does not have, measured size, and absolute GitHub links for the longer docs; a CI check fails on a relative link in the README, and the README's code blocks are run by the docs-as-tests quickstart test (D144). A README fix reaches users only with a new version, so it ships as 0.1.1; the release workflow stops hard-coding `v0.1.0` and accepts a tag equal to `v` plus the `package.json` version. Found after the 0.1.0 release |
| D148 | Keys: the quickstart exposed a gap | P17D found that the only UUID primary key is `t.id()`, which always defaults to `uuidv7()` (Postgres 18 only, OKM1812 below it), is guarded and cannot be supplied on insert; a normal column has no `.primaryKey()`, and there are no composite or natural primary keys. So the README had to teach a table without a primary key. Fix in P17G: `.primaryKey()` on a column and a composite primary key option; `t.id()` takes an explicit default (`uuidv7()` or `gen_random_uuid()`, the latter from Postgres 13) and the OKM1812 fix text names it; an id the caller supplies is allowed when the default is `none`. Until then the 0.1.1 README uses `t.identity()` (bigint identity primary key, works on every supported version). Found in P17D |
| D149 | Public board is the single source of truth for progress | The project is built in the open, so progress lives in one public place, a GitHub Project on the repository, fed by issues and pull requests and updated by automation, not by hand. One issue per roadmap item, in reader language (the internal P-number goes in the body); one milestone per release (0.1.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.4, 0.5); every pull request body ends with `Closes #N`; the built-in project workflows add new items as Todo, move a pull request to In review, and move an item to Done on merge or close; the release workflow creates the GitHub Release from the changelog section and closes the milestone. The README roadmap links to the board. The decisions and execution-plan documents stay as design records, not status. `okmodel-status.md` stops being updated once the board runs. Ali does one setup step (`gh auth refresh -s project`), nothing after. Planned in P17F |
| D150 | Release gate: no tag without the Docker-backed suite | 0.1.0 shipped with a planner bug (an identity column made `okm push` and `okm generate` fail with a second `CREATE SEQUENCE`; fixed in 0.1.1) because the round-trip tests installed their start catalogs with `renderCatalog`, which already folds the sequence, and the postgres job runs a hand-listed subset of tests on Postgres 17 only. The release workflow runs `bun run check` only, with no Postgres at all. Rule from now on: a release is published only after (1) every test, not a hand-picked list, runs against real Postgres in Docker on each supported major version (a version matrix; PGlite and the wire server never replace it), (2) the packed tarball runs the README and quickstart commands against that Postgres, (3) the release workflow depends on that job passing, and (4) after publish a smoke job installs the version from npm in a fresh directory and runs the quickstart again, failing if the version or its provenance is missing. A test that does not run in the matrix is listed with its reason. More drivers (node-postgres, Bun.sql) are added in P18 through the same conformance suite and capability registry, so a capability a driver or an environment lacks is reported as a skip with a reason, never hidden. Found in 0.1.1 |
| D151 | Supported Postgres 15 to 18, and what CI runs when | The floor is Postgres 15 (13 is past end of life, 14 ends in November 2026, 15 gives `NULLS NOT DISTINCT` and `MERGE` for later); supported majors are 15, 16, 17, 18, so D150's matrix is 15 to 18. A pull request runs the suite on 15 and 18 and the tarball on 18; the full matrix runs in the release gate before publish and weekly on a schedule; `push` runs CI on `main` only so a branch with a pull request runs once, and a newer run on the same ref cancels the older. `bun run verify` runs the same suite against Docker on the developer's machine (one version, or all with a flag); CI stays the authority for a release. The code has no floor yet: `connect()` accepts a server below 15 (OKM1801 only rejects a non-PostgreSQL server; OKM1802 only fires when the schema sets `requires`), so P17G adds an explicit version floor check with its own message. Not yet done: publish the exact tarball the gate tested, and run the tag check before the gate rather than inside publish. Found in P17E |
| D152 | Scripts exit by themselves; `await using` closes the client | A connected client gets `Symbol.asyncDispose` (attached only when the runtime has the symbol; the built declaration hides it behind a conditional type so no consumer lib breaks), and a second `close()` shares one promise. Measured: postgres.js starts two referenced timers (idle and lifetime) we cannot reach, so unref'ing alone does not help; the plain (non-TLS) pool now sets `idle_timeout: 0` and `max_lifetime: null`, opens its own sockets, unrefs them while nothing is in flight, and refs them again for each call. A finished script exits in about 0.12 s on Node and Bun instead of 30 s, and a long-lived pool keeps its backend pid across a 4 s hold (tested). A pool opened with TLS still uses the driver's socket and the 30 s idle timer; that path is unchanged and documented (P17G looks at it). PGlite already exits on its own. Cost: the app startup graph grew 77,537 / 25,625 to 79,162 / 26,185 and the postgres.js connect entry to 39,569 / 13,715, leaving 0.86% and 0.70% under their gates; no gate moved, and any further change that pulls this adapter needs a ceiling decision first (D138 cap ends with 0.1). Found in P17D2 |
| D153 | Id generators: `uuidv4` now, OKID in 0.2 | (1) `t.id()` keeps `uuidv7()` as its database default and gains `t.id({ default: "uuidv4" })` and `"none"`: `uuidv4` emits `gen_random_uuid()`, which is built in from Postgres 13 (the `uuidv4()` alias only exists in 18, so we do not emit it); P17G ships it, replacing the name `random` used in its first prompt. (2) OKID joins as an id option in 0.2 (P19), brought in as a vendored copy of okengine's `src/okid.ts`, `okid-shared.ts` and `okid-extended.ts` (about 380 lines, Apache-2.0 on both sides) because okmodel has zero runtime dependencies; okengine stays the source of truth and okmodel carries a header, okengine's test vectors and a check that fails when the copy drifts. It uses only the global `crypto.getRandomValues`, no `node:` imports, so it runs on Node, Bun, Deno and edge runtimes; CI runs it on Node, Bun and Deno, and `engines.node` is declared (undeclared today). (3) OKID is generated by the application, not by Postgres (no OKID function exists until functions land in 0.3), so it is an application-side default: it does not enter the database catalog hash and does not produce a migration when its prefix or length changes. (4) The id column is `text`. A sortable OKID orders as time only under byte order, so the column uses `COLLATE "C"`; the catalog has no column collation today (only a reserved-word list mentions it), so P19 adds collation to the catalog, create, diff and introspection. (5) The builder shape (`t.id({ ... })` options for okid, prefix, sortable, length) is proposed by P19 in its report and then written into the spec. (6) OKID is an application identifier, not a secret; sortable ids expose creation time, and the docs say so. Found when planning ids for 0.2 |
| D154 | Client-side generators through `.default()`, and a schema-level id default | (1) `.default(x)` takes a literal value or a client generator (a generator value or a function returning the column's type); a generator means the client fills the field on insert when it is omitted. The database expression form stays `.defaultSql(sql)`, as in the spec today. No separate `.generate()` / `.auto()` name: `.auto()` reads as autoincrement, `.generated()` is Postgres's word for computed columns, and Prisma and Drizzle already teach one default concept. `.default()` is client-side or a plain literal and `.defaultSql()` is the database's, and the catalog output labels a client default as `client`. Built-in generators are `uuidv4` (the runtime's `crypto.randomUUID()`), `uuidv7` (our own, so Postgres below 18 can have time-ordered ids with no database support) and `okid(options)` (D153); where they are imported from (a root export or a small `okmodel/ids` subpath) is decided in P19 against the tree-shake gate. (2) A client default is not part of the database catalog or its hash and produces no migration when it changes (D153); the column is `NOT NULL` with no database default, so a writer that bypasses okmodel (psql, another service, raw `INSERT ... SELECT`) must supply the value, and the docs say so; prefer a database default when several systems write the table. (3) The insert type makes the field optional and the select type keeps it required; a guarded id (`t.id` family) is filled only when omitted, a plain column with a client default may also be supplied by the caller. (4) `t.id(...)` takes its options per column, and `schema({ tables, defaults: { id } })` sets what a bare `t.id()` means for every table (`uuidv7`, `uuidv4`, or a client generator); a per-column option wins; the effective choice is shown in the catalog output; changing the schema default after tables exist is a type or default change and the planner classifies it like any other (a uuid to text change is a recreate and needs a declared replacement, D131). With no `defaults.id` and a declared server below 18, OKM1812 names the `defaults.id` fix. (5) `connect({ generators })` may replace a built-in generator, so tests get deterministic ids (the testing package in 0.4 uses it). All of this ships in P19 with OKID. Found when planning ids for 0.2 |
| D155 | Startup-graph gate after P17G, and who edits the spec | P17G left the app startup graph at 79,757 / 26,149 against a gate of 79,849 / 26,410 (92 and 261 bytes of headroom), so the next prompt would stop on the gate. The gate moves to measured +3%, 82,200 / 26,950, in its own commit with this decision named; from here a prompt that grows the graph reports the growth and the gate moves only by decision to measured +3%, never above the 0.2 allowance of 91,000 / 30,000 (D143). The connect entries keep their gates (postgres.js 39,849 / 13,815 with 1,192 bytes of headroom). Separately, the spec in the repository drifted from the master copy (Cursor edits in P17 and P17G); draft 25 merges both, and from now on the repository copy on the branch is read before any spec edit and a spec change Cursor makes is merged back, so there is one master. Found in P17G |
| D156 | Driver choice by subpath, and what a capability flag means | `pg` and `Bun.sql` ship as `okmodel/pg/pg` and `okmodel/pg/bun`; the default stays `okmodel/pg/postgresjs`. `pg` is an optional peer (`^8.16.0`) and Bun.sql has no package; neither import is reachable from the runtime entry. A capability flag is true only where the shared suite passed it, so the table is honest: Bun.sql `cancel`, `describe` and notices are false, node-postgres `describe` is false, and node-postgres cancels through its own 16-byte CancelRequest on a fresh socket because `Client.cancel` corrupts the live connection. All four drivers exit after the last call without `close()` (plain pool); the TLS path of node-postgres is unread because the test tree has no TLS server, and that is written in known-limits. New connect entries print their size and get gates at measured +3% when a prompt sets them. Found in P18 |
| D157 | Gates after ids (P19) | P19 added OKID, client generators and column collation, and the app startup graph grew from 79,757 / 26,146 to 82,041 / 26,716 against a gate of 82,200 / 26,950 (159 and 234 bytes of headroom); the postgres.js connect graph is at 38,942 / 13,390 (gate 39,849 / 13,815), `pg` 38,871 / 13,554 and Bun.sql 37,772 / 13,123. The growth is accepted: OKID is app-side, reachable only through `okmodel/ids`, and the runtime entry did not move (5,525 / 2,042). By the D155 rule, the first commit of P20 moves the gates to measured +3%, rounded down: app startup 84,500 / 27,500; postgres.js connect 40,100 minified with the gzip gate 13,815 kept; `pg` 40,000 / 13,950; Bun.sql 38,900 / 13,500. Nothing moves above the 0.2 allowance of 91,000 / 30,000 (D143), which leaves about 6,500 bytes of startup for tagged operators and the rest of 0.2. `engines.node` is `>=22` (Node 20 is past end of life). Found in P19 |
| D158 | Gates after the 0.2 operators, and the room left for P21 to P29 | P20 (the operator set) took the app startup graph from 82,041 / 26,716 to 82,679 / 27,196 and every connect entry to within 1% of its D157 gate (postgres.js 39,679 / 13,693; `pg` 39,821 / 13,866; Bun.sql 38,722 / 13,426); the operator SQL is a chunk loaded on first use, but the planner and the fit check are static. By the D155 rule the first commit of P21 moves the gates to measured +3%, rounded down: app startup 85,100 / 28,000; postgres.js connect 40,800 / 14,100; `pg` 41,000 / 14,250; Bun.sql 39,800 / 13,800; PGlite stays. The hard cap for 0.2 is still 91,000 / 30,000 (D143), which leaves 8,321 / 2,804 bytes for nine steps (P21 to P29: provenance and safety, field exposure, traits, tenancy, archivable, validation, relations, presets, transactions), about 900 bytes each if spent evenly. That is not enough for all of them to ship their code in the startup graph, so from P21 each step reports its growth before it builds, loads feature code on first use where it can (as P20 did for operator SQL and as `okmodel/ids` does), and stops and asks if a step would take more than its share instead of moving the cap. Also recorded: P20 removes the old `json` column-builder export from `okmodel/pg` (`json` is now the write namespace with `json.set`; the column is `t.json()`, and `jsonb` is unchanged), a breaking change for anyone who imported it, listed in the changelog and the 0.2 release notes. Found in P20 |
| D159 | What P21 shipped, and what is left of the 0.2 budget | `table()` records `file.ts:line` through one stack-reading helper (`undefined` when the runtime gives no stack, and on paths without a `/`, so Windows drive paths lose the location, which is accepted); the location sits on the catalog object and model, `inspect()` copies it onto catalog rules, the duplicate-table error (OKM1023) carries it, and `okm` prints that message; the serialised catalog and its hash leave it out, so a catalog loaded from the document has no source. `okmodel/safety` is a registry plus `safety.property` and loads only when imported; the planner calls it through a one-slot hook that does nothing while no rule is registered. Scope kept small on purpose: the write planner does not call the hook yet (the step that records write contributions wires it), and a SQL-name clash, an emitted-type clash and an unknown foreign-key table do not append a location (adding them cost 183 minified bytes). P21 took 880 minified and 350 gzip in the startup graph (83,559 / 27,546). The cap stays 91,000 / 30,000, so P22 to P29 (eight steps) share 7,441 minified and 2,454 gzip, about 930 and 306 each; gzip is the binding number. From P22 a step reports its estimate of both before building and stops if it expects more than about 900 minified or 300 gzip in the startup graph. Found in P21 |
| D160 | Gate moves for P23 to P29, and what P30 must prove | P22 grew the app startup graph by 699 minified and 265 gzip (84,258 / 27,811), leaving 842 / 189 bytes under the D158 gate and 6,742 / 2,189 under the 0.2 cap for seven steps (about 960 and 310 each). Standing rule for P23 to P29: when a step's measured growth passes a gate, the first commit moves the app startup and connect gates to measured +3% (rounded down), never above 91,000 / 30,000 for app startup, with the old and new numbers in the report; the step's own stop rule (about 900 minified or 300 gzip in the startup graph) still applies, and the cap moves only by a new decision. This replaces one decision per step. Also recorded for P30: the P22 safety rule reads only contribution text (the words shown, revealed, set), because `SafetyInput` carries no schema or query, so the property tests prove the registry works, not that data cannot leak; the P30 composition tests must therefore run real queries against real Postgres and assert on returned rows, errors and `inspect()` output, with the registry as an extra layer. Found in P22 |
| D161 | `filters()` is synchronous and validates; `parse()` is asynchronous | P22 made `filters()` return a promise so its code could load on first use. Measured: a fully synchronous `filters()` adds 971 minified and 288 gzip bytes to the startup graph (parser 344 / 109, OKM1123 check with its text 347 / 110, relation check 280 / 69), and the real parser will grow with every operator, so the parser cannot live in the startup graph. But the promise was in the wrong place: `filters()` is called once at module level, where a promise forces top-level `await`, and OKM1123 is a build-time error in the spec. Decision: `filters()` is synchronous, stores the spec and runs the OKM1123 and relation checks when called (a throw, about 179 gzip bytes); only `parse()` is asynchronous and loads the parser on first use, which costs nothing in a request handler that is already async (`const q = await taskFilters.parse(request.query)`). Spec 10.2 is updated. Found in P22 |
| D162 | P22 overspent its share; the budget for P23 to P29 | P22 ended at 84,907 / 28,017 in the app startup graph, a growth of 1,348 minified and 471 gzip over P21 (699 / 265 for the exposure rules, then 649 / 206 for the synchronous `filters()` checks, which the part-by-part estimate had put at about 179 gzip). That is about 45% over the 930 / 310 share D159 set. Left under the cap of 91,000 / 30,000: 6,093 minified and 1,983 gzip for seven steps (P23 to P29), about 870 and 283 each. Gates stand at 87,454 / 28,857 for app startup (D160, 2.9% under) and the connect entries moved with them. From P23 the stop line is about 800 minified or 250 gzip in the startup graph per step, and an estimate has to be measured on the built graph, not summed from parts. The heavy steps (tenancy, archivable, validation, relations, transactions) must keep their code behind the schema that uses them or load it on first use; a step that cannot fit that way reports its estimate before building, and the cap is raised only by a decision with Ali, never by squeezing a feature. Found in P22 |
| D163 | P23 ships over the gzip line, and the cap will need a decision | Traits were redesigned so the logic travels with the trait (`apply(model, ctx)` on the object `timestamps()` and `trait()` return) and `schema()` keeps only a loop. Measured on the built no-trait app: +661 minified and +312 gzip over `main` (85,568 / 28,329), down from +1,463 / +518; 657 of the 661 bytes are that loop. An app that uses `timestamps()` pays 88,273 / 29,144 in total, and the trait module itself is +2,705 / +815, paid only by apps that import it. The step is 62 gzip bytes over the D162 stop line (about 250) and under it in minified; it is accepted because a smaller version would only move checks out of one place into another. After P23 the room under 91,000 / 30,000 is 5,432 minified and 1,671 gzip for P24 to P29, about 905 and 278 each, and the heavy steps (tenancy, archivable, validation, relations, transactions) will not fit that if each one costs 300 gzip. Before P24 starts, either a size audit finds savings in what already ships (message text, duplicated helpers, error registry) or the cap is raised by an explicit decision with Ali; features are not squeezed to fit. Found in P23 |
| D164 | The size audit worked: the cap stands, the gates come down | The audit moved rare paths behind first use (signal and timeout setup, the watched-query finish path, checkout hold and `LISTEN` counting, row streaming, the server-version SQL, the fix sentences for OKM1102, OKM1104, OKM1105 and OKM1190), shared one thenable between the read and write handles, and shortened internals, with no change to any error code, message, SQL or catalog byte. No-trait startup graph: 83,043 / 27,666 (freed 2,525 / 663); with `timestamps()` 85,744 / 28,503; runtime entry 5,190 / 1,996; connect entries postgres.js 37,842 / 13,289, PGlite 35,545 / 12,675, `pg` 38,511 / 13,596, Bun.sql 37,412 / 13,158. Under the 91,000 / 30,000 cap there are now 7,957 / 2,334 bytes for P24 to P29 (about 1,326 / 389 each), so the cap is not raised. The gates, which were left at the old numbers (87,454 / 28,857 for app startup), come down to measured +3% in the first commit of P24: app startup 85,500 / 28,490, postgres.js connect 38,970 / 13,680, PGlite 36,600 / 13,050, `pg` 39,660 / 14,000, Bun.sql 38,530 / 13,550; the runtime entry gate stays at 6,100 / 2,250. D160 still lets a step raise them again to measured +3%, never above the cap.
