The stop line for a step is now about 1,300 minified or 380 gzip in the startup graph, and each step reports an estimate measured on the built graph before building. Known cost of the audit: the first use of each moved path loads a chunk, a few milliseconds once per process. Left alone on purpose: shorter message text, building the catalog on first use, the canonical-JSON identity fallback, nearest-name hints, async `has` / `none` / `every` SQL. Found in P23b |
| D165 | Opt-in features are objects that carry their own logic (tenancy first) | Wiring column tenancy into `schema()`, the client and the predicate emitter grew the no-tenancy startup graph by 7,178 minified and 1,969 gzip (83,043 / 27,666 to 90,221 / 29,635), five times the stop line, because `schema()` is synchronous and a plain option object (`tenancy: { key, type, strategy: "column" }`) has no code, so all the code had to be static. That would leave 779 / 365 bytes under the cap for P25 to P29. Decision: a strategy is an object imported from its own subpath that carries the logic, as traits do (D163). For column tenancy: `import { columnTenancy } from "okmodel/tenancy"` and `schema({ tenancy: columnTenancy({ key: "tenantId", type: "uuid" }) })`; the object adds the tenant column, widens uniques, builds composite foreign keys, supplies the scope predicate and the `for()` / `unscoped()` client methods, while core keeps only small hooks that do nothing when no tenancy is set. Other strategies (`schemaPerTenant`, `databasePerTenant`) are separate objects in the same subpath later, so an app ships only the one it uses. The same rule applies to archivable, validation, presets and any other opt-in feature of P25 to P29: a plain option object may only select behavior that already lives in core, and anything bigger travels with an imported object. If the redesigned P24 is still over the stop line after one more measurement, it stops again with numbers and the cap becomes a decision with Ali. Spec section 9 is updated on the P24 branch to show the imported form. Found in P24 |
| D166 | P24 gets a larger share, and tenancy is a rewrite step | With tenancy as an imported object (D165) the no-tenancy startup graph still grew by 3,736 minified and 1,104 gzip (86,779 / 28,770): the `schema()` hooks 2,058 / 651 (one call per compile phase), planner hooks 669 / 219, client hooks 852 / 193, column and model wiring 157 / 41. A tenancy app is 92,281 / 30,408 and is not gated. Decision: tenancy is the largest feature left, so P24 may spend up to 2,000 minified and 600 gzip in the no-tenancy startup graph, which leaves 5,957 / 1,734 under the 91,000 / 30,000 cap for P25 to P29 (about 1,190 / 347 each) and keeps the cap where it is. To get there the `schema()` side becomes one call: the tenancy object rewrites the table definitions once before compile (adds the tenant column, widens uniques, stages composite foreign keys as ordinary constraints), so the compiler needs no per-phase hooks. The scope predicate stays inside the planner, because every statement passes there; it is not moved to a wrapper around the public API, where relations, `has` / `none` / `every` and aggregates could slip past. If P24 is still over 2,000 / 600 after this attempt it stops with numbers and the cap becomes a decision with Ali. Found in P24 |
| D167 | P24 ships at +2,245 / +751, and what the cap measures | With tenancy as a one-call rewrite the no-tenancy startup graph is 85,288 / 28,417, +2,245 minified and +751 gzip over 83,043 / 27,666, 245 and 151 bytes over the 2,000 / 600 allowance of D166. Breakdown: schema call 507 / 192, composite foreign keys (a general compiler feature, not tenancy-only) 423 / 134, planner predicate 493 / 196, client `for` / `unscoped` 602 / 169, wiring 220 / 60. Accepted: the design is the one we want (rewrite before compile, predicate inside the planner where every statement passes) and a further round would only trade bytes between the same places. A tenancy app measures 91,504 / 30,333 and is not gated. After P24 the room under the 91,000 / 30,000 cap is 5,712 minified and 1,583 gzip for P25 to P29, about 1,142 / 317 each; the cap stays, and it holds only if archivable, validation, presets, relations extras and transactions keep their code behind imported objects or lazy chunks (D163, D165). The cap applies to the featureless fixture app (ten tables, one find); an app that uses traits and tenancy is bigger than the fixture by design, so P30 adds a reported figure for a feature-full app and decides its ceiling before 0.2, and the README size table says which app a figure is for. Found in P24 |
| D168 | P24 final numbers, tenant primary key, and the rewrite limit | P24 shipped (PR #103, 0.2.0-next.12) with the no-tenancy startup graph at 85,356 / 28,361 (+2,313 / +695 over 83,043 / 27,666; D167 said 85,288 / 28,417 before final cleanup) and a tenancy app at 91,802 / 30,411, reported and not gated. Gates moved under D160 to app 87,900 / 29,210, postgres.js 40,200 / 14,090, PGlite 37,800 / 13,450, `pg` 40,900 / 14,410, Bun.sql 39,700 / 13,940; the runtime entry is 5,190 / 1,996 under its 6,100 / 2,250 gate; the 200-table query probe is 6,947 of 7,100 (2.2% under, inside the 3% watch band). The cap stays 91,000 / 30,000, so P25 to P29 share 5,644 minified and 1,639 gzip, about 1,129 / 328 each. Two things the implementation settled that the spec had not: (1) a tenant table's primary key is `(declared key, tenant key)` with `UNIQUE` on the same columns, instead of a separate `UNIQUE (id, tenant_key)`; this lets two tenants share an id and is what the composite foreign keys point at, and the spec section 9.1 was changed on the branch to say so; (2) a unique that a trait adds after the tenancy rewrite is not widened with the tenant key (`timestamps()` adds none). This is a known limit until traits run before the rewrite or the rewrite re-checks traits; it goes in `docs/known-limits.md` and P30 tests that no shipped trait adds a unique. Found in P24 |
| D169 | P25 stops over its allowance; archivable reuses the tenancy rewrite and carries its methods | `archivable()` works and its tests pass on real Postgres, but the featureless startup graph grew to 87,449 / 28,944 (+2,093 / +583 over 85,356 / 28,361), against an allowance of 1,100 / 320 (D162 stop line). Where it sits: entry chunk +577 (schema slots for cascade checks and partial uniques), main chunk +1,516 (about 878 for `archive`, `restore`, `withArchived`, `onlyArchived` and the OKM1052 throw, about 280 for the active-set predicate in the planner, the rest the scope helper and inspect slot); the archive statement builder is a separate 7.4 KB lazy chunk and not counted. Accepting this would leave 3,551 / 1,056 for P26 to P29 (about 888 / 264 each), too little for transactions and relations extras. Decision: one redesign, no cap change. (1) The partial uniques and the cascade checks move into the object's `apply` and run in the same single rewrite pass tenancy already uses (D166), so core adds no schema slots. (2) `archive`, `restore`, `withArchived`, `onlyArchived` are supplied by the object through one generic table-handle hook shared with tenancy's `for()` / `unscoped()`, not written in core; OKM1052 is thrown by the lazy execution chunk. (3) Only the active-set predicate stays in the planner, next to the tenant predicate, because every statement passes there. Allowance stays 1,100 / 320. If the redesign is still over, it stops again with numbers and the choice (accept, or raise the cap) goes to Ali; Cursor does not decide it. The connect entries crossed their gates in this attempt (postgres.js 40,537 / 14,093, PGlite 38,246 / 13,473, `pg` 41,212 / 14,374, Bun.sql 40,113 / 13,934); gates move only under D160 at the final numbers, and only if they still exceed the old gate after the redesign. The 200-table query probe is 6,967 of 7,100 (1.9% under, inside the 3% watch). Found in P25 |
| D170 | P25 ships at +1,792 / +577; shares for P26 to P29 | The redesign of D169 measured 87,148 / 28,938 for the featureless startup graph, +1,792 minified and +577 gzip over 85,356 / 28,361, which is 692 / 257 over the 1,100 / 320 allowance. It was 301 / 6 smaller than the first placement (87,449 / 28,944). By chunk: entry +1,164 (`rewritePass`, `collectHooks`, the archive-model copy and the index predicate sit in `schema()`), main +628 (active-set predicate and the generic hook calls; `archive`, `restore`, `withArchived`, `onlyArchived` are no longer in core). Accepted: a third design would only move bytes between the same chunks, and the rewrite pass and hook collection are shared infrastructure that P26 (validation), P28 (presets) and P27 can reuse through `apply`, so they should cost less than a first feature did. The cap stays 91,000 / 30,000. Room for P26 to P29 is 3,852 minified / 1,062 gzip. Guideline shares (stop lines, each step stops with numbers when it passes its own): P26 validation 500 / 150, P27 relations extras 1,200 / 330, P28 presets 600 / 180, P29 transactions 1,400 / 400 (total 3,700 / 1,060). Any step that passes its share stops and the cap becomes a decision with Ali, as before. Connect entries stay under their gates (postgres.js 39,641 / 13,895, PGlite 37,350 / 13,263, `pg` 40,316 / 14,178, Bun.sql 39,217 / 13,741) and the app is under its gate (87,900 / 29,210), so no gate moves in P25. The 200-table probe is 6,967 of 7,100. P30 reports the feature-full figure and decides whether the entry chunk needs a size pass before 0.2. Found in P25 |
| D171 | P25 contract details settled in code | `archivable()` shipped in PR #104 (0.2.0-next.13) at the accepted size (D170): featureless app 87,148 / 28,938, archivable app 95,725 / 31,604 at startup and 155,844 / 50,118 with lazy chunks (reported, not gated); archive suite green on local Postgres 15, 17, 18 and on CI 15 and 18. Six details the spec had left open are now decided: (1) partial uniques are unique indexes, because Postgres cannot make a `UNIQUE` constraint partial; the primary key stays a full constraint, tenancy's extra unique on the primary-key columns becomes the same kind of partial index, and indexes written with `indexes()` are left as written. (2) `archive()` returns `{ count, archiveId }` where `count` is the targeted table only, so `expect` behaves as on `update`; cascade archives the named direct children in the same statement and does not follow those children's own cascade lists. (3) A bad cascade name is OKM1020 (unknown table, not archivable, duplicate, self, or no primary key) or OKM1021 (no foreign key, or more than one); a primary key is required when a table cascades or an archivable parent points at it. (4) `restore()` returns `{ count }`. (5) `ON CONFLICT` adds `WHERE archived_at IS NULL` only for uniques that are not the primary key. (6) A dynamic `archive` / `restore` on a non-archivable table throws OKM1052 in the trait method before any lazy import; the chunk throws the same code if reached without a model (this refines D169, which put it only in the chunk). Known limit recorded: cascade reaches direct children only, so grandchildren of an archived row stay active until their own parent is archived; a later step may add transitive cascade. Reusable for P26 to P28: `rewritePass` runs `tenancy.rewrite` first, then each trait object that has `rewrite(tables, casing, schemaTraits)`, once, schema traits then table traits; `collectHooks` gathers every object with `hook(target, ctx)` from tenancy and traits, and the client calls it for the client and again for each table. New opt-in features put compile-time work on `rewrite` and client or table methods on `hook`, and add no schema slot. Spec updated (cascade, `restore` return, partial indexes). Found in P25 |
| D172 | P26 stops at +3,402 / +1,077; validation redesign and the real limit | The first validation build measured 90,550 / 30,015 for the featureless startup graph, +3,402 minified and +1,077 gzip over the P25 result (87,148 / 28,938), against a share of 500 / 150 (D170). It is also over the app gate (87,900 / 29,210) and 15 gzip bytes over the 91,000 / 30,000 cap. By chunk: shared chunk (client bind, column `.validate`) +923 / +314, entry (schema packing, OKM1030) +2,479 / +773. The engine itself is a separate lazy chunk of 8,025 / 3,005 and is not in the startup total; the write chunk grew 495 / 203. A validating app measures 90,953 / 30,198 at startup, so the cost is paid by apps that never enable validation, which is the thing D163 and D165 forbid. The featureless total graph rose from 139,805 / 45,280 to 151,727 / 49,055 because `client.ts` always holds the dynamic import of the engine. Decision: one redesign, no cap change yet. (1) OKM1030 is a check-time diagnostic (spec: "types + `okm check`"), not a runtime schema step; it leaves the entry chunk. (2) `.validate()` on a column and `validate:` on a table only store their rules as data; nothing is packed or interpreted in core. (3) Everything except one small loader moves into the engine chunk or the `v` module; the client gets `insert.validate`, `check`, `pick`, `omit` through the shared `hook` (D171), not from core. (4) The loader is the only static reference to the engine, so a no-validation app's total graph does not include it. Target: within the D170 share of 500 / 150 over P25. Hard limit: the current gate, which is +752 / +272 over P25; no gate or cap moves in this step. If the redesign is over the gate it stops again with numbers and the question goes to Ali. If it lands, a size audit step (as P23b was, D164) is decided before P27, because P27 to P29 have 3,200 / 910 of shares left and the featureless app is at the cap. Found in P26 |
| D173 | P26 ships at +260 / +81; validation fails closed; typed surface by augmentation | The redesign of D172 landed: featureless app 87,408 / 29,019 (+260 / +81 over P25, inside the 500 / 150 share, under the gate 87,900 / 29,210), total graph 140,394 / 45,569, engine chunk (8,025 / 3,005) not in it, validating app 92,269 / 30,636 at startup (reported). PR #105, 0.2.0-next.15. Accepted from the four proposals: OKM1030 is `okm check` (and the same function when an app that imported `okmodel/validate` builds a client), not a TypeScript error; the `Input` mark follows the literal `schema({ validation: true })` only for now; `insert.validate`, `check`, `pick`, `omit` and the Standard Schema surface are runtime-only and not on the table type, because adding them measured 17,645 instantiations against the 17,300 ceiling; `insert()` stays typed `Insert` with `InsertBody` carrying `Input`. Two findings from review. (1) Fail-open: the spec text says a schema that stores `validation` without `import "okmodel/validate"` does not run rules, so a forgotten import silently disables validation on writes. Decision: it fails closed. When a schema enables validation (schema or table level, any form) and `okmodel/validate` has not registered, `connect()` throws a new OKM12xx error naming the cause; it never runs unvalidated (same principle as D162, a failed import fails the call). (2) The typed surface should not stay runtime-only, and the 200-table probe is at 17,282 / 17,300 instantiations (0.10% under) and 6,997 / 7,100 types, so P27 cannot add query types without a decision. Decision: P26b moves the `insert.validate` family and the table-level / object-form `Input` mark into a TypeScript module augmentation shipped with `okmodel/validate`, so an app that imports validation gets the types and an app that does not pays nothing in the featureless probe; the probe must not move. Before P27 the probe ceilings get an explicit decision with Ali if P27 needs room. Found in P26 |
| D174 | P26b stops; fail-closed moves to the write path, typed surface waits for the probe decision | P26b measured both follow-ups and both missed hard limits, so nothing was committed. Typed surface: module augmentation costs +1 instantiation and +2 types at best (17,283 / 6,999 against 17,282 / 6,997), +17 / +11 for the extracted-interface form; the 200-table probe is at 17,282 of 17,300 instantiations (0.10% under) and 6,997 of 7,100 types (1.5% under), so there is no room without a ceiling decision. Fail closed at `connect()`: +298 / +114 for a schema-flag-only check, +568 / +158 with the table walk, +670 / +210 in full, against a +100 / +40 stop line and 492 / 191 of room under the app gate; the two sentences naming the cause alone are +102 / +52. Decision: the check does not belong at `connect()`. Validation only matters on write, and the write chunk is already lazy and already sees the table's rules and the effective setting. So the fail-closed check lives in the write chunk (and in `okm check`, which is tooling): a write that would validate, with `okmodel/validate` not registered, throws OKM1201 before any statement is sent. It costs nothing in the featureless startup graph. The window between `connect()` and the first write is accepted; there is no path that sends an unvalidated write. The typed surface (`insert.validate` family and table-level / object-form `Input` mark by module augmentation) is deferred to the start of P27 and decided together with the query-probe ceilings, because P27 (relations, paging, aggregates) needs probe room anyway; Ali decides the new ceilings once, with numbers from P27's first measurement, and the featureless app gate and cap do not move for this. Until then `insert.validate` and the family are runtime-only (D173). Found in P26b |
| D175 | P26b accepted; room left; probe ceilings authorised for P27 | P26b (PR #106, 0.2.0-next.16) put the fail-closed check in the write chunk as OKM1201 (D174): featureless app 87,408 / 29,001 (+0 / -18 against P26), under the gate 87,900 / 29,210 by 492 / 209; validating app 92,557 / 30,729; query probe unchanged at 17,282 instantiations and 6,997 types. Room under the 91,000 / 30,000 cap for P27 to P29 is 3,592 / 999 against shares of 1,200 / 330 (P27), 600 / 180 (P28), 1,400 / 400 (P29), which leaves 392 / 89 of slack, so every step stays on its stop line. Because the app gate sits below the cap, a step that passes the gate moves it under D160 at the measured result plus 3%, never above the cap. The 200-table probe has no room (0.10% under on instantiations), and P27 needs query types for `aggregate`, `page`, `manyThrough` and the typed validation surface (D174). Decision: P27 may raise the probe ceilings from 17,300 / 7,100 to at most 18,000 instantiations and 7,400 types (about +4%), reported with before and after numbers per feature; going beyond that stops the step with numbers. This is a one-time re-baseline, not a standing allowance; P28 and P29 must stay inside the new ceilings. Approval: running the P27 prompt is Ali's approval of this change. Found in P26b |
| D176 | P27 stops at +1,425 / +431; one redesign; findings routed; probe policy | The first P27 build measured 88,833 / 29,432 for the featureless startup graph, +1,425 minified and +431 gzip over 87,408 / 29,001, against a share of 1,200 / 330 (225 / 101 over) and over the app gate (87,900 / 29,210). Shared core chunk +1,322: `lazyRead` plus `page` and `aggregate` methods in `client.ts` 591, `manyThrough` loading in `plan.ts` 463, `needThrough` calls in `planners()`, `loadWrite` and `loadArchive` 181, `Keyset` and `build` hooks 117; entry +103 (the `manyThrough` branch in `resolveRelations`); lazy chunks `aggregate` 3,974, `page` 3,502, `through` 613 are not in startup. Decision: one redesign, in which the `manyThrough` relation object carries its own emitter and the planner passes its helpers in as arguments (layering holds), which removes about 480 and puts the total near +950; no second redesign, and the stop line stays 1,200 / 330. If it lands and passes the app gate, the gate moves under D160 (measured +3%, never above the 91,000 / 30,000 cap, so it will sit at or just under the cap and leave no slack), and P28 and P29 (600 / 180, 1,400 / 400) then fit only on their stop lines; a cap decision with Ali may be needed at P29 or P30. Query probe: the gated `query-200` is now 16,919 instantiations and 6,219 types because the validation surface moved to augmentation (D174), below the old 17,300 / 7,100, so the one-time raise to 18,000 / 7,400 authorised in D175 is not used and the gated ceilings return to 17,300 / 7,100. The gated probe no longer exercises the features, and an ungated cost is a blind spot: feature rows measured `query-200-validate` 24,885 / 6,412, `page` 18,785 / 6,290, `page` and `aggregate` 20,894 / 6,575, with `manyThrough` 24,049 / 6,729 (each `page` call about +1.9K and `aggregate` about +2.1K instantiations; `manyThrough` +24 / +28 over `many`). Decision: the feature-using probes are gated with their own ceilings at the measured final result plus 3%, rounded down, reported old and new. Findings routed: (1) aggregates decode with the source column's codec, not as `number`: `sum` and `avg` of a numeric column follow the numeric codec, `count` stays a number; done in P27. (2) The client rejects any object value on insert (OKM1121), including Temporal dates, which Cursor reports is the default timestamp codec, and an array insert requires every key even when typed `| undefined`: both are input bugs to verify and fix in a small P27b PR on real Postgres before 0.2, not in P27. (3) `iStartsWith`, `iContains`, `iEndsWith` are in section 12 but not implemented; they are not in 0.2, section 12 says so, and `ilike()` with an escaped pattern is the way until 0.3. (4) The `through` chunk loading on the first operation of any schema that declares `manyThrough` is accepted. Found in P27 |
| D177 | P27 ships at +870 / +324; gates at the cap; what is left | The redesign of D176 landed: featureless app 88,278 / 29,325 (+870 / +324 over P26b, 6 gzip bytes under the 330 stop line after `lazyRead` was simplified, which is the same design with fewer bytes), the `manyThrough` emitter now lives on the relation object in `through.ts` and there is no `through` chunk (an app that declares `manyThrough` has the emitter in its startup graph; `page` 3,502 and `aggregate` 3,974 stay lazy). PR #107, 0.2.0-next.17. The app gate passed and moved under D160: app 90,900 / 30,000 (gzip equals the cap because measured +3% would be 30,206), postgres.js 41,600 / 14,600, PGlite 39,300 / 13,980, `pg` 42,400 / 14,920, Bun.sql 41,200 / 14,470; the connect entries measure 40,396 / 14,180, 38,222 / 13,574, 41,186 / 14,494 and 40,087 / 14,052. Room under the 91,000 / 30,000 cap is 2,722 / 675, against stop lines of 600 / 180 (P28) and 1,400 / 400 (P29), which leaves 722 / 95; the gate now sits at the cap, so any step that passes it needs a cap decision with Ali. Probes: gated `query-200` 16,919 / 6,219 under the restored 17,300 / 7,100; `query-200-validate` 24,885 / 6,412 gated at 25,631 / 6,604; `query-200+page+aggregate+through` 24,070 / 6,730 gated at 24,792 / 6,931; the `page` row (18,785 / 6,290) and `page+aggregate` row (20,915 / 6,576) are printed only. Spec differences accepted as decisions: (1) `sum` and `avg` keep the column's value type and refuse a column that is neither a string nor a number (OKM1124); `sum` over a `text` column passes the types and fails at runtime, recorded in known limits; (2) `aggregate` returns nested objects (`row.sum.amount`), `orderBy` takes `groupBy` fields only, `limit` or `.all` is required with `groupBy`, no `having`, no `bucket`, hidden fields refused; (3) `page` requires `limit`, and its cursor is an opaque string of the order signature plus the values as Postgres wrote them (timestamps keep microseconds); (4) OKM1130 also covers a cursor that `page()` did not return; (5) `manyThrough(table, { through, from?, to? })`. Spec sections 10, 6.2 and 6.4 were merged. The OKM1121 object-value rejection on insert and the array-insert key rule go to P27b. Found in P27 |
| D178 | P27b accepted: a column takes the objects its codec declares; list inserts take different keys | Verified on Postgres 17 before fixing. (1) Reproduced: `insert`, `update` `set` and `where` refused every object with OKM1121, including Temporal values for `timestamptz`, `timestamp`, `date`, `time`, `timetz` and `interval`, json and jsonb, arrays, bytea, ranges and points. A `Date` is not an input of any default codec, so it stays OKM1121 and is not a bug. (2) Fix: each codec names the object kinds it takes (`accepts`, as `Object.prototype.toString` spells them), carried to the planner on the column; a column with none refuses every object, so the operator-object guard is unchanged for text, integer, uuid, bigint and the rest. `t.custom` gains an optional `accepts`; without it a custom column refuses objects. One planner function (`takeObject`) serves insert, `set`, and the operands of `eq`, `lt`, `lte`, `gt`, `gte`, `between`, `inList`, `notIn` and `not`. A range object without `empty` is OKM1210 before any statement. (3) Spec differences accepted: in a `where` a bare object is refused for every column and `eq(value)` is the equality form for any object value, not only json and jsonb. (4) The array-insert key finding does not reproduce at runtime (different keys, `undefined`, `null`, defaults, tenancy, archivable, validation, conflict clauses and chunking already match single inserts); it was a types bug, the list overload required every key; fixed in `types.ts`. (5) Found: `interval` and `timetz` cannot be read back (see D179). Size: featureless app 88,393 / 29,385 (+115 / +60, exactly on the stop line); no gate or ceiling moved; `query-200-validate` 24,888 and `page+aggregate+through` 24,070, both about 3.0% under their ceilings. PR #108, 0.2.0-next.18. Found in P27b |
| D179 | `interval` and `timetz` read-back: fix in P28 under its own stop, else known limit | Postgres sends `01:30:00` and `01:02:03+03`, the decoders take only an ISO-8601 duration and a `±HH:MM` offset, so a read that selects such a column fails with OKM1210; writes work. Decision: P28 carries a small second commit that makes both decoders accept what Postgres sends, with a real-Postgres round-trip test. It has its own stop line: if it moves the featureless app by more than +30 / +15 on top of presets, or needs a design, the commit is dropped, the entry stays in known limits for 0.2, and the step reports the numbers. No second design on its own. Found in P27b |
| D180 | P28 presets ships at +330 / +168; choices recorded | Presets are in (PR #109, 0.2.0-next.19): featureless app 88,723 / 29,553 (+330 / +168 over P27b, inside the stop line of +600 / +180), runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (unchanged), connect entries 40,695 / 14,390 (postgres.js), 38,455 / 13,763 (PGlite), 41,419 / 14,668 (pg), 40,320 / 14,243 (Bun.sql), all under their gates; no gate, cap or probe ceiling moves. `query-200` is 17,062 / 6,259 (+143 / +40) under 17,300 / 7,100, `query-200-validate` 25,031 / 6,452 and `query-200+page+aggregate+through` 24,212 / 6,770 under their ceilings. How it is built (D162 to D165, D171): the core only records a chain (`tasks.pending().ownedBy(id)` is a linked list of `[before, name, args]` on the handle) and dispatches to a lazy `presets.js` chunk when a statement is planned; reads, writes and archive each import it only when the chain is not empty, and the planner accepts one private tagged operator, `and`, which joins the caller's `where` and the presets' predicates after the tenant predicate and the active set. Choices made, accepted: (1) the builder has one method, `where`; `or([...])` goes inside it, and there is no method that removes or replaces a predicate (D125, D126). (2) A preset does not count as the filter `update` and `delete` require; `where` or `.all(reason)` is still needed (OKM1102), and `archive` and `restore` follow. `insert` ignores the chain. (3) OKM1040 is reused for a duplicate name (table and trait, or two traits), and its fix names both sources. (4) A reserved name is a type error in `table()` and `trait()`, OKM1040 in `trait()` when built, and OKM1040 for a table at `connect()` before the first query, not in `schema()`: the check needs the 23-name list in the startup graph, which measured +530 / +166 on top of the dispatch and would pass the stop line. (5) `inspect()` names the preset, the fields it filters, who defined it and where; it does not print predicate text, because values are parameters; `sql()` holds the predicate. (6) A preset read's `sql()` and `inspect()` are asynchronous, because the chunk loads when the statement is planned. (7) A table's own presets are typed on the table's declared columns, not on columns a trait adds. (8) `PresetQuery` is exported from `okmodel/pg`, and `registerPresets()` from `okmodel/safety`. Found in testing and fixed: `aggregate` wrote the `where` it was created with and so ignored presets; its statement builder now receives the call it builds for. Not measured: no probe row uses presets, so their type cost is not gated (P29 adds a gated presets probe row). D179 outcome: `decodeDuration` and `decodeTimeZone` read what Postgres sends and keep the ISO forms; a real-Postgres test writes and reads both types. The round trip found a second bug on the write side: Postgres refuses a leading minus, so a negative `Temporal.Duration` failed on `insert`; it is now written with a sign on each field. The featureless app did not change for this commit (an app that declares neither column does not bundle the codecs). Left in `known-limits.md`: mixed signs (`1 day -01:00:00`), a non-default `IntervalStyle`, and a seconds offset on `timetz`. Room under the cap now 2,277 / 447 against P29's stop line of 1,400 / 400, which leaves 877 / 47. Found in P28 |
| D181 | P29 transactions ships at +215 / +225; races proved on four drivers; choices accepted | `tx`, `batch`, row locks, `afterCommit`, advisory locks, per-call `{signal, timeout}` and `connect({timeouts, hookm})` are in (PR #110, 0.2.0-next.20). Featureless app 88,938 / 29,778 (+215 / +225 over P28, stop line +1,400 / +400), runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (unchanged), connect entries 40,990 / 14,591 (postgres.js, gate 14,600), 38,957 / 13,974 (PGlite, gate 13,980), 41,402 / 14,770 (pg, gate 14,920), 40,251 / 14,285 (Bun.sql, gate 14,470); no gate, cap or existing probe ceiling moves. Room under the 91,000 / 30,000 cap is 2,062 / 222, and the postgres.js and PGlite connect entries have 9 and 6 gzip bytes left, so any growth in client or connect code now needs a cap decision with Ali. Probes: `query-200` 17,078 / 6,271, `query-200-validate` 25,047 / 6,464, `query-200+page+aggregate+through` 24,231 / 6,795, all under their ceilings; two new gated rows at measured +3% (D176): `query-200+presets` 19,180 / 7,128 (ceilings 19,755 / 7,341) and `query-200+tx` 24,283 / 6,948 (ceilings 25,011 / 7,156). The presets row shows a chain of two presets with arguments plus `find` costs about +2.1K instantiations over `query-200`. A first typing of `tx` that passed the table-name union as a type argument cost +629 on `query-200`; a `Root` flag computed inside the callback type cost +16. How it is built (D162 to D165, D171): the core only dispatches; `transaction.ts`, `batch.ts`, `lock.ts` and `hookm.ts` load on first use. `tx()` reserves one connection and gives the callback a facade pool over it, so every code path keeps its affinity. A transaction that had a statement fail refuses to commit even when the callback caught the error. Nested `tx()` is `SAVEPOINT okm_tN`, `ROLLBACK TO`, `RELEASE`. Retry only on kinds `serialization` and `deadlock`, a fresh transaction per attempt, jittered pause, one deadline for all attempts. A connection lost at COMMIT is `outcome_unknown` (OKM1401), never retried, and nothing is sent on it after. Races on real Postgres on postgres.js, node-postgres, Bun.sql and PGlite where each can (38 cases each): serializable conflict with retry, real deadlock, `skip` and `nowait`, statement killed by timeout and by abort, cut commit and cut batch through a TCP proxy, pool reuse, savepoints. Skips: PGlite has one connection, no `cancel` and no socket to cut; Bun.sql has no `cancel` and no notices. Choices accepted: (1) `timeout` is milliseconds as a number, as `connect({timeout})` already is; the spec example `"5s"` is changed. (2) A nested `tx()` takes no options (OKM1121). (3) `batch` takes writes only; a read in the list is OKM1121 (spec edited). (4) A table named `tx` or `batch` is shadowed by the method. (5) `rls` tenancy is not built; inside `tx()` the tenant is the column predicate on every statement. (6) A `batch` inside `tx()` emits no `hookm.onTransaction` phase; a savepoint opened by `tx()` does. (7) `find({lock})` on the root client compiles and is OKM1830 at runtime. (8) On PGlite and Bun.sql a `tx({timeout})` or `tx({signal})` cannot kill a statement waiting on a lock; the callback is released and the rollback queues behind it (known limit for 0.2). (9) `idleInTransaction` is a client-side timer, not the server setting. Found by the cut-commit test and fixed in the adapters: postgres.js crashed on a write to a closed reserved socket; node-postgres needed an `error` listener and `release(true)`; Bun.sql parked a lost connection; all three keep a `lost` flag, send nothing after it and drop the connection. Not built or not tested: Neon, D1 and other batch-mode drivers (no adapter in the repo). Found in P29 |
| D182 | P30 gate for 0.2: property tests ship, feature-full app measured, one open finding | The gate adds four property tests on real Postgres with a fixed seed (20261005, `OKM_PROPERTY_SEED`; scale `OKM_PROPERTY_SCALE`; `OKM_PROPERTY_REPORT=1` prints coverage): `isolation.property`, `safety.property`, `statements.shape`, `archive.correctness`. Each is checked for sensitivity by dropping a predicate or swapping a column on the wire. They found no isolation, safety, statement-count or archive defect. They found one defect in `batch` (P30-1): a `restore` blocked by an archived parent (kind `invalid`) and a write with a wrong `expect` (kind `not_found`) are checked in `finish`, after the batch has committed, so the caller sees the error with `batchIndex: null` and the other writes of the batch stay. The spec says all commit or none does. Not fixed in P30: a fix needs the check inside the atomic unit, which a batch-mode driver (Neon, D1) cannot do after the fact. Proposed (not decided): (a) `batch` refuses `restore` and `expect` with OKM1121, or (b) the restore blocking and the count check move into SQL that raises, so the unit rolls back. Resolved by D183. Size: featureless app 88,938 / 29,778 (unchanged, no runtime code added), feature-full app (`scripts/app-full.ts`) 113,705 / 37,506, +24,767 / +7,728; the entry-chunk size pass would take at most 31,173 / 11,081 out of the plain startup graph and is not built. One dead function (`withTenantScope`) is removed. Found in P30 |
| D183 | P30 finding resolved; hygiene and size choices | (1) `batch` refuses `restore` and any write that carries `expect` with OKM1121 (option (a) of D182): a check that needs the statement's result cannot run inside an atomic unit on a batch-mode driver, so the rule is the same on every driver and fails closed instead of reporting after commit; `tx()` is the way to do it. Option (b) (move the checks into SQL that raises) is not 0.2; it can be reconsidered when a batch-mode adapter exists. The fix goes on the P30 branch as a second commit with a regression test (every write kind in a list, blocked restore and wrong `expect` both refused before any statement) and a stop line of +30 / +15 on the featureless app. (2) Duplicated helpers (`noteArchive`, `requireFilter`, `primaryFields`, `isPool`, `statsOf`, `asParams`, `refuse`, `isRecord`) are not deduplicated in 0.2: sharing them moves bytes onto the startup graph or pulls the write chunk into the archive path, and room is 2,062 / 222. A dedupe is allowed only as a step that shrinks or keeps the bytes. (3) The entry-chunk size pass is not in 0.2. It is the first step of the 0.3 train, before P40, because the extension, function, view and role features need startup room; it starts with a measurement (the report's 2,700 to 4,000 gzip estimate is unmeasured), has a stop line, and no gate or cap moves without Ali. (4) Registered codes never thrown: 24, mostly for later trains; the three that belong to 0.2 specified behaviour (OKM1110 engine version, OKM1191 no snapshot plan, OKM1702 unverifiable tenant SQL) are checked in the same follow-up commit: each is either thrown where the spec says it is, or the spec says it is not reachable in 0.2 (typed raw SQL is M2; no public runtime path takes raw SQL on a tenant table). OKM1111 is added to the spec error table; OKM1000 in the spec is a range label, not a code. Found in P30 |
| D184 | P30 follow-up accepted; two findings go to 0.2.1 | The follow-up commit (`11310fd`) makes `batch` refuse `restore` and any write with `expect` (OKM1121, before any statement), adds `batch-refusals`, `snapshot-reads`, `raw-sql-paths` and `codes-not-reached` tests, and fixes the spec text for OKM1110, OKM1191 and OKM1702: each is not thrown in 0.2 (the one engine-dependent feature `uuidv7()` is OKM1812 when `requires` is declared; every read is one statement; no public runtime path takes raw SQL, enforced by a test that fails if one appears). Featureless app 88,938 / 29,770 (no change), gates and cap unmoved. Two findings are not release blockers and go to a 0.2.1 patch, tooling-only so no runtime bytes: (1) `t.id()` defaults to `uuidv7()`, and on Postgres 15 to 17 with `requires` undeclared the DDL fails with the raw driver error `function uuidv7() does not exist` and no OKM code. The migration tooling (`okm migrate apply` / `check`, which read the server version) raises OKM1812 with the fix (`requires`, or `t.id({ default: "uuidv4" })`) before running DDL; nothing is added to `connect()` because the connect gates have 9 and 6 gzip bytes left. Until then it is a known limit and the README already says the default needs Postgres 18. (2) The types accept `restore` and `expect` inside `batch`; only the runtime refuses. Narrowing the types costs probe instantiations; it is measured in 0.2.1 and done only if it stays inside the probe ceilings, otherwise it stays runtime-only and documented. Found in P30 follow-up |
| D185 | P31 (0.2.1) accepted; batch types stay runtime-only; apply-time engine check | `okm migrate apply` and `okm push` scan the migrations for `default uuidv7()` before any statement (after the advisory lock, before `okm_meta`) and ask the server for its version only when a hit exists. On Postgres 15 to 17 without a `uuidv7()` function it stops with OKM1812 and creates nothing; the message names the column, the migration, the server version and both fixes (`schema({ requires })` on 18, `t.id({ default: "uuidv4" })`). A server below 18 that already has its own `uuidv7()` function is accepted, so 0.2.1 does not refuse a setup that worked in 0.2.0; a function created inside the same migration run does not count. The scan reads every migration passed to apply, not only unapplied ones (the result is the same). Code is in `src/tooling/migrate/engine.ts`; nothing in `connect()` or the runtime entry, featureless app and every connect entry +0 / +0. `okm migrate check` does not exist in 0.2 (P53A); when it lands it calls `assertUuidV7Available`. Batch types: narrowing so the compiler refuses `restore` and `{ expect }` in `batch` was measured and is not built. The full form (second overload per write) moves `query-200-validate` to 27,366 against a ceiling of 25,631; the one-generic form moves `query-200` to 18,443 against 17,300; the brand-only form (+2 / +1) covers `restore` and `.expect(n)` but not the `{ expect }` option. Decision: runtime-only stays; the half-enforced brand is not built, because a rule that looks enforced and is not is worse than a documented runtime rule. A printed `query-200+batch` probe row (no ceiling) is added. Headroom correction: the connect gzip gates have 11 bytes (postgres.js, 14,589 of 14,600) and 8 bytes (PGlite, 13,972 of 13,980), not the 9 and 6 of D184 (those were computed from the earlier D181 figures). PR #113, issue #112. Found in P31 |
| D186 | First release run failed before npm; CI and release hardening in PR #114 | The first Release runs on v0.2.0 (#5 and the retry) failed in `publish` before npm: `release.yml` ran `bun run check` without Deno (and without `setup-node`), so `test:runtimes` reported "deno is not on PATH, and CI must run it". Nothing was published; the cause was never npm. PR #114 fixes it and hardens the rest: (1) `publish` sets up Node 22 and installs Deno v2.9.7 (same lines as `ci.yml`) before Check; the Node 24 step for `npm publish` moves after Check. (2) A guard in `tests/release-gate.test.ts` parses `ci.yml` and `release.yml` and fails if any job that runs `bun run check` differs from `ci.yml` in its Deno lines or Node version, so the two cannot drift. (3) Actions move to Node 24 releases, pinned by full SHA: checkout v5.1.0, setup-node v5.0.0, upload-artifact v6.0.0, download-artifact v7.0.0 (the oldest Node 24 major of each; `setup-bun` was already Node 24); the Node 20 warning is gone. (4) Every job runs on `ubuntu-24.04` instead of `ubuntu-latest`, because the label moves to Ubuntu 26 from 2026-10-19; moving to 26 is a deliberate later change. (5) Release policy: `scripts/release-check.ts` exempts a PR whose changed files are all under `.github/` from the version-bump and changelog rules (a three-dot diff against the base; an empty diff is not exempt; any file outside `.github/` brings both rules back). PR #114 itself changes `scripts/` and `tests/`, so it cannot use its own exemption; it is merged once with that one check red, as a bootstrap, because every other job is green and the red check is the rule being relaxed. The version stays 0.2.0: v0.2.0 was never published, so after the merge the tag is deleted and re-created on the new `main` and Release is run again. Seen, not changed: the `tag` job has `actions: write` that nothing appears to use; `ci.yml` checks out with full history and `release.yml` does not (nothing in `check` reads history). Found in P30 release |
| D187 | The second release failure was the publish argument; 0.2.0 is folded into 0.2.1 | Release run 37264007346 passed the tag job, all eight Postgres jobs and Check, then failed in `npm publish packed/okmodel.tgz`: npm reads a path without `./` as the git shorthand `user/repo` and tried to clone `github.com/packed/okmodel.tgz`. It was never an npm permission or trusted-publisher problem. The fix is `npm publish ./packed/okmodel.tgz`, and `tests/release-gate.test.ts` now fails when any `npm publish` argument in any workflow does not start with `./` or `/`. Because 0.2.0 never reached npm, it is not released on its own: the fix lands in PR #113, 0.2.1 is the first public release and includes everything listed under 0.2.0, and the changelog says so. The `v0.2.0` tag is deleted from the remote and not re-created, so no release tag is moved a third time. Lesson: a release workflow cannot be proven by its tests alone; the first real run is the proof, and each failure so far (Deno missing, bare publish path) was invisible to every earlier gate. |
| D188 | P39 closes with no code change; sync contracts stay sync | P39 measured the startup graph and stopped at its own stop line: no lazy move saves 200 gzip bytes on the entry without turning a synchronous call into an asynchronous one. The candidates and their gzip effect on the featureless app (connect entries in brackets): catalog build cluster -5,017 (not in connect), `sha256` -1,493 (-175), `array-literal` -608 (0), `nearest` -401 (-403 postgres.js, -399 PGlite), `words` (reserved words) -403 (-451, -432), `filters` -226 (0), `enum` -158 (0; entry file -178). Every one except the `enum` hook sits behind a call that is synchronous today (`schema()`, `.catalog`, `throwNamed`, `fitIdentifier`, `filters()`, `.array()`, `assertIdentifier`), and `plan.ts` and `client.ts` are the plain `find` path whose `sql()` and `inspect()` return values, not promises. Decision: those calls stay synchronous. `schema()` building and validating at call time, and failing at the call site, is part of the contract (D138, D162); a lazy chunk would make it a promise. No gate, cap or ceiling moves. The connect headroom stays at 11 bytes (postgres.js) and 8 bytes (PGlite) and is not widened in advance: 0.3 features load as lazy chunks behind opt-in objects (D162 to D165, D171) and must leave the featureless startup graph and every connect entry unchanged; a step that cannot do that stops with exact bytes and the cap question goes to Ali. The `enum` registration hook (-158 / -178) is not worth a step by itself and is taken only if a later step already touches `enum.ts`. P39 has no issue and no branch. Found in P39 |
| D189 | P40 splits: extensions now, domains in P40b; gzip measured without chunk ids; startup share +200 / +70 | P40 stopped after the extension commit (`9b8f8e3`). (1) The +1 to +4 gzip bytes on connect entries in the first attempt were the lazy chunk id (a content hash) changing, not code. `scripts/size.ts` now replaces each lazy chunk id with a fixed-length constant before gzip; minified size is the real size. The normalised baseline on main's code is 88,938 / 29,609 for the featureless app (was 29,770), postgres.js 14,443, PGlite 13,825, `pg` 14,619, Bun.sql 14,136, runtime entry 2,026. Gates, caps and ceilings are unchanged, so the real headroom under the app gate (90,900 / 30,000) is 1,962 min and 391 gzip, and 157 / 155 / 301 / 334 gzip on the four connect entries. (2) Extensions follow D162, D171: `extension()` lives on `okmodel/ext`, the definition object carries its own catalog record, `schema()` only calls `contribute(peers, built)` on each declared object when `.catalog` is first read, the document reader and the plan live in the lazy tooling chunk. Errors OKM1810 (undeclared use), OKM1811 (not available on the server), OKM1813 (duplicate), OKM1814 (downgrade or non-relocatable move); an upgrade plans offline as `path-unverified` and apply checks `pg_available_extensions` and `pg_extension_update_paths` before any statement. Provides are methods on the object and are not stored on the record (a stored list would drift against introspection). (3) The startup share for P40 is +200 / +70 (was +150 / +60) so that `pgTrgm.gin()` and `gist()` can carry their index method and operator class into the catalog index and the plan: that line measured +54 / +10, and without it a trigram index would be planned as a btree, which is wrong. It ships working with a Postgres test that the index is created `USING gin (...) gin_trgm_ops` and round-trips through introspection with no drift, or it does not ship and `gin()` / `gist()` throw. The gate does not move. (4) Domains are a catalog `type` subkind and measured +902 / +235 on the featureless app through the enum path (`domainType`, `pushDomain`), which leaves 96 gzip under the gate for P41 to P43. That is too much for one subkind, so domains leave P40 and become P40b, its own branch after P40 merges; `t.domain` stays on OKM1061 until then. P40b is a design pass first: the target is a domain that rides the generic `contribute` path or shares the enum emission, at most +60 / +25 on the startup graph. If it cannot, it stops with the exact bytes and the cap question goes to Ali. Not planned in P40: ordering by `similarity()` (planner code on the connect graph, budget 0). Found in P40 |
| D190 | P41 and P42: objects through `contribute`; typed calls and builder views deferred; P42 share +280 / +50 | P41 (PR #117) shipped `fn()` and `trigger()` as catalog objects through `contribute` at +80 / +18 on the featureless app (89,198 / 29,688), 0 on the runtime entry and every connect entry. Two things are deferred: (1) typed call fragments (`fn.slugify(col)` in where, projections, ordering): the cheapest version measured +77 minified on every connect entry against a budget of 0, so it needs a later decision (a connect cap, or a lazy planner path) and is not in 0.3 as built; (2) a `LANGUAGE sql BEGIN ATOMIC` body is stored as the server reprints it, so a plan from the live catalog back to the author text replaces the function when the reprint differs (the same family as the domain check, D189; candidate for the P44 hygiene step). Commit 2 (`db.views.*.find`, tenancy classification, OKM1820) measured +267 / +48 through the cheapest design found (`schema()` loops the view list and calls `install` / `hook`; find, tenancy and codecs stay in `okmodel/view`), which is +167 / +8 over the P42 share of +100 / +40. Decision: the share for P42 becomes +280 / +50 in total, which keeps the featureless app at 89,465 / 29,736 against the gate 90,900 / 30,000, so no gate, cap or ceiling moves and 264 gzip stay for P43 and the P44 hygiene step. | D191 | Column type spellings compare equal in the diff | A plan compared the schema spelling with `format_type` as text, so `timestamptz` and `timestamp with time zone` were a type change.
