Domain bases already store the long spelling when the domain is built, and that path is not the column diff. Decision: one function, `canonicalTypeName`, used on both sides of the comparison only. The catalog field stays as written. `timestamptz`, `timestamp`, `time`, `timetz`, `varchar(n)`, `char(n)`, and the integer, boolean, float, and `decimal`/`numeric` aliases map to the long spelling, with length, precision, scale, and `[]` kept. An unknown name is compared as written. `varchar(20)` to `varchar(30)`, and `timestamp` to `timestamptz`, still alter. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P44 |
| D192 | P50 is classification and the linter core; safe rewrites are P50b | Every plan step kind has one class: `expand`, `contract`, or `unclassified` for raw SQL only. Drop default, a required column with no default, drop identity, `SET GENERATED ALWAYS`, and `ALTER ROLE` are contract. The linter reads the plan and the two catalogs and does not connect. Destructive, backward-incompatible, and data-dependent findings are errors. Locking findings are warnings until P50b emits the safe form, then they become errors. Type preferences are warnings on `okm check`. An override is `-- okm-allow OKM15xx: reason` on the line above the statement. An empty reason, or a code the statement did not trigger, is OKM1510, and an override silences only the code it names. `okm generate` writes the file and prints findings. `okm migrate plan` and `okm check` exit non-zero on an error. `okm migrate apply` re-lints and refuses with OKM1510 before any statement. Safe rewrites (concurrent index create and drop outside a transaction, `NOT VALID` plus `VALIDATE`, `SET NOT NULL` through a validated check, a volatile default split from column creation, a unique constraint built from a concurrent unique index) are P50b. OKM1706 and OKM1823 stay declaration-time until P50b. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P50 |
| D193 | Safe rewrites run only on a table that already exists | On a table present in the before-catalog, the planner emits the safe form. A table created in the same plan keeps the plain statements. An index is `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` or `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, and a drop is `DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, outside a transaction, lock `SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE`. A check or foreign key is `ADD CONSTRAINT … NOT VALID` then `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT`. Postgres documents `SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE` for validate, plus `ROW SHARE` on the referenced table for a foreign key, and `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` on that table when the foreign key is added. `SET NOT NULL` adds `CHECK (col IS NOT NULL) NOT VALID` under a generated name, validates it, sets not null, then drops the check. A volatile default (`gen_random_uuid()`, `uuidv7()`, or a catalog function marked volatile) adds the column nullable, sets the default, fills existing rows with one `UPDATE` (batching is P52), then that not-null sequence when the column is required. The fill is a backfill action, so a protected target refuses it unless `--allow-protected`. The `SET NOT NULL` of that new column stays kind `add-column`, so the plan stays expand. A stable or constant default, and a call the catalog cannot classify, stay a plain `ADD COLUMN`. A unique constraint or primary key builds the unique index concurrently, then `ADD CONSTRAINT … USING INDEX`. Brief `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` steps run under the existing `lock_timeout` retry. Resume of a failed concurrent index drops the invalid index (`pg_index.indisvalid`) and rebuilds, and does not repeat a finished step. OKM1534, OKM1535, OKM1536, and OKM1537 are errors and fire only when the SQL is not this form. OKM1538 stays a warning. A validate that follows `widen-check` does not fire OKM1531. OKM1706 and OKM1823 stay declaration-time and are not copied into the linter. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P50b. Issue #123 |
| D194 | Plan row estimates stay out of the linter and the migration file; a declared table rename renames default names | `okm migrate plan` prints a row estimate from `pg_class.reltuples` when one target is configured, or `--target` names one, and that database answers. It never runs `count(*)`. `reltuples` of `-1` prints `rows unknown (table not analyzed)`. A relation absent from `pg_class` prints `new table`. No target, several targets without `--target`, or a target that cannot be reached prints the lock alone and does not error. The query is one read-only transaction and follows the other read-only commands: `plan` is allowed on a protected target, and a pooler host is not refused. A safe-rewrite step is labelled `safe rewrite applied`. `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on more than 1,000,000 estimated rows, outside a safe rewrite, prints a plain note and is not a lint finding. Estimates are not lint input and are not written to SQL, catalog files, or `okm_history`. `okm generate` stays offline. A partition or inheritance parent is estimated from its own `reltuples` only. The number is only as fresh as the last analyze. A table `renamedFrom` plans `ALTER TABLE … RENAME TO`, then renames the primary key, unique and check constraints, foreign keys, indexes, and identity sequences whose names are the defaults `{table}_…`. A custom name is left as it is. An add and a drop with no declaration is still OKM1530. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P51. Issue #79 |
| D195 | A backfill is a batched step in the migration file, and apply runs one target | A `backfill` step stays plain SQL. The header is `-- backfill table=<table> key=<col[,col]> batch=<n>`. `$1` is the exclusive lower bound and `$2` is the inclusive upper bound; null opens that side. The runner finds each boundary with a keyset query on the primary key, including a composite key and `uuid`, and never with `count(*)`. The statement is idempotent. Planner output for a D131 removal and for the volatile-default fill uses `where` the old value or `where` the column is null. A table with no primary key is OKM1546 at plan time. There is no `backfill()` export and no `okm backfill` command. Apply runs the step outside the migration transaction, one transaction per batch, under `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`, with the existing lock-timeout retry and a pause between batches. Defaults are `defineConfig({ backfill })`: 1,000 rows, no pause, 30 seconds. `batch=` overrides the batch size. Each committed batch writes `okm_backfill` on the target; apply creates that table if it does not exist. A finished step is still an `okm_history` row. `okm_history` is not changed. A rerun resumes from the stored key. A protected target is refused on every batch unless `--allow-protected`. Column tenancy is one pass; tenant iteration is M5. `TargetRunner` takes one target, its units, retries, and checkpoints, and returns a per-target report. `okm migrate apply` calls it with a list of one. Bounded concurrency, canary, class flags, and the control database are M5. The row estimate is not stored. No byte moves on the runtime entry, the featureless app, or the connect entries, and no type-cost ceiling moves. Found in P52. Issue #80 |
| D196 | P53 is `okm migrate check`; startup compatibility is P53b | P53 is migrate tooling only. `okm migrate check` replays every migration into a scratch schema through the normal apply path, plans the introspected schema back to that file's catalog, checks that the previous catalog is still satisfied unless the recomputed class is `contract`, requires the last catalog to match the schema, and lints the history. The class is recomputed from step kinds, or by planning again when a step has no kind. A hand-edited `-- class:` line is not trusted. `defineConfig({ lintFrom })` is the adoption baseline: files before that id are not linted by this command. Apply still lints pending migrations only. A protected target is refused, because the scratch schema is a write, and `--allow-protected` does not apply. A fork is the replay failure `migration X was generated from a different parent than Y` (OKM1547), not a separate commutativity algorithm. OKM1521 stays reserved for P53A. P53b is a separate branch: the startup compatibility states in `connect()`, `okm migrate status` states, and loading the serialised catalog in production. P53b touches the connect entries, whose byte budget is nearly zero, so it does not share a pull request with P53. No byte moves on the runtime entry, the featureless app, or the connect entries, and no type-cost ceiling moves. Found in P53. Issue #81 |
| D197 | Startup compatibility uses the stored class; the serialised catalog loads once | `connect()` keeps the hash fast path: equal catalog hashes return without reading `okm_history` or `.okm/catalog.json`. When the hashes differ, startup reads `okm_history` and uses the `class` column as stored. It does not plan the SQL again. Ahead by expand migrations succeeds. Ahead by a contract migration, or behind the app, is OKM1520. The message names the migration. The fix says to apply that migration or change the deploy order. `requireMeta` is unchanged: a missing hash is still skipped unless the option is set. `okm migrate status` prints the same situations: `current`, `behind by expand`, `behind by contract`, `ahead by expand`, `ahead by contract`, and `failed at step N (resume with okm migrate apply)`. The command stays read-only on a protected target. A `catalog.hash` file makes the mismatch path load `.okm/catalog.json` instead of rebuilding the catalog from the schema. Concurrent connects in one process share one parse. A missing file, or a hash that does not match, is OKM1027. No new error code. The loader stays on the mismatch path, so the featureless app graph and the connect startup graphs do not gain it. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P53b. Issue #127 |
| D198 | Reference rows live on the schema; an empty target installs the head catalog | A migration file stores catalog JSON, not a second copy of the DDL. The head snapshot is the last catalog. Installing it plans that catalog from an empty catalog, so grants and roles are included and a new table keeps the plain statements. `reference` rows stay on `table({ reference })`. They are not copied into the catalog and they do not change the catalog hash. Apply and `provision(target)` insert a missing key and never update or delete, so a label change does not update, a dropped declaration does not delete, and a new key is inserted on the next apply without a DDL migration. An empty target (no `okm_meta`, `okm_history`, or `okm_backfill`, and no other relation, enum, domain, or extension in the namespace) records `provisioned@<migration id>` and does not replay steps. A target that already has those history tables stays on the replay path. A non-empty target with no history is OKM1851. Provision on an empty protected target is allowed without `--allow-protected`. `provision(target)` is one configured target. Registry `create(id)`, schema-per-tenant, and database-per-tenant are M5. `okm migrate check` stays the P53 replay. `--provision` compares a snapshot install with that replay after `canonicalTypeName` (D191) and reports OKM1521. OKM1542 flags a data statement outside backfill. Reference inserts are not migration steps, so they are not flagged. No new error code. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P53A. Issue #82 |
| D199 | `okmodel/testing` is a real pool, a seeded factory, a statement count, and `okm seed` | `testing(schema, { driver })` returns `{ db, factories, expectQueries, isolation, close }`. The driver is `open()` from `okmodel/pg/pglite` or `open({ url })` from `okmodel/pg/postgresjs`. A `pglite()` helper is not added: it would live on the connect entry, and that graph does not grow for this package. `testing` awaits either a pool or a promise of one. It applies `renderCatalog` before connecting unless `migrate: false`. `okm seed` passes false because the target is already migrated. Factory defaults: a column is required when it is writable, not guarded, has no client `fill`, and is not nullable, defaulted, identity, or generated. The fill is taken from the column type (text, uuid, integer, numeric, boolean, JSON, the first enum label, or a `Temporal` value the column accepts) by asking `column.encode`. A value the codec rejects is not used. If every candidate is rejected, the error names the column. The tenant key is guarded and is the `for()` scope; omitted, it is generated. `x.ref` reuses the latest row of that table in the same tenant, or inserts one. `with({ child: n })` sets the child's foreign key from a `many` relation, otherwise a `one` relation, otherwise the catalog foreign key. Generators are a mulberry32 sequence in this package, no dependency. The default seed is `1`. Seed `0` is shifted so the first step is not a fixed point. The same seed replays the same calls. `create` returns the inserted row as a record, not a recursive row type, so the existing type probes do not gain a factory recursion. `expectQueries` counts statements the wrapped pool sent during `fn`. It does not count transaction control after comments are stripped: `BEGIN`, `START TRANSACTION`, `COMMIT`, `COMMIT WORK`, `END`, `ROLLBACK`, `ROLLBACK WORK`, `ROLLBACK TO`, `SAVEPOINT`, `RELEASE`, `SET TRANSACTION`, `SET LOCAL`, and `SET SESSION CHARACTERISTICS`. A data statement inside `tx` is counted. A mismatch throws kind `invalid` and lists the counted statements. The version check is awaited before the harness is returned, so it is not part of the count. `isolation()` treats a table as tenant when its predicate accepts a scope, skips a table whose rules start with `global `, and lists that reason. It inserts as tenant B and runs `find`, `one`, `count`, `exists`, `include` when a relation exists, `update`, and `delete` as tenant A. A leak names the table, the query, and the row. Column tenancy is the 0.4 strategy. Schema-per-tenant and database-per-tenant are M5. `okm seed <file>` loads a module whose default export is a function and calls it with the harness bound to the selected target. `assertTargetPolicy` runs first, so a protected target is OKM1850 unless `--allow-protected`. Several targets without `--target` are OKM1853. No target, or an unknown name, is OKM1845. The command prints `target <name>` and the factory insert counts. No new error code. The subpath is not imported by the runtime entry, the featureless app, the feature-full app, or the connect entries. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P54. Issue #83 |
| D200 | Gate 0.4: the protection table matches the code, and a reprinted default cast is not drift | `protected.policy` runs every class in spec 19.7 against a protected target and an unprotected one, through the CLI, `applyTarget`, `runTarget` (including each backfill batch), `seedProject`, and `provision`. Expected results are the table. Read-only classes (`plan`, `status`, `okm check`, `okm ext check`, drift, and the same function for `verify`, `pull`, catalog export, and `inspect`) are allowed. `okm migrate check` is OKM1850 and `--allow-protected` does not lift it. Expand, reference rows, and provision of an empty target are allowed. Contract, unclassified raw SQL, `okm push`, backfill, `okm seed`, and history repair are OKM1850 and run with `--allow-protected`. A non-empty target with no history is OKM1851, which is spec 19.6, not the protection code. `OKM_ALLOW_PROTECTED` does not exist: setting it does not lift a refusal. A target named `production` is not protected unless the flag is set. `verify`, `pull`, catalog export, and `inspect` have no command in 0.4; the function allows them, so there is nothing to bypass. History repair has no command; the function blocks it, and nothing else calls it. A contract the linter rejects is OKM1510 before the policy runs; both gates stand, and the enumeration uses an override so OKM1850 is the code under test. `okm check` and a doctor role connection call the same function for the read-only class. Drop and rollback stay refused even with `--allow-protected`; they are not rows in the 19.7 table. Finding, correctness bug, fixed: after provision, `okm check` planned `SET DEFAULT` because Postgres reprints a text default `'x'` as `'x'::text`. Postgres 15.19 and 18.6 also reprint `varchar(10)` as `'x'::character varying` (no length), `char(3)` as `'x'::bpchar`, and `timestamp(3)` / `timestamptz(3)` / `time(3)` with the precision kept on the column and omitted from the cast. `numeric(10,2)` reprints as the literal with no cast (`1.50` stays `1.50`; `1.5` stays `1.5`). Comparison strips one trailing cast when the cast's base type is the column's, with length and precision ignored for those character and time families. The stored catalog text is unchanged (D191). A cast to a different type, a different value, and a timestamp literal whose `T` Postgres reprints as a space still plan `SET DEFAULT`. Domain-check and function-body reprint stay deferred. No new API, error code, gate, cap, or ceiling. The runtime entry stays 5,288 minified and 2,026 gzip. The featureless app is 89,461 / 29,720, with 1,439 and 280 bytes left under 90,900 / 30,000. Found in P55. Issue #84 |

| D201 | P60 topology runtime; connect entries get no more bytes | `connect({ primary, replicas })` loads `src/runtime/topology.ts` through a dynamic import only when the first argument is an object and not a pool. A string or a pool stays the previous client and does not load that module. The promise opens one pool per endpoint (`max` is the default; a replica `pool.max` overrides it). The primary is named `primary`, never `replica-0`. Replica names default to `replica-1`, `replica-2`. A probe on each replica runs `SELECT 1` and a replay position every `probe` (default 1s). Two consecutive failures open the circuit; the next delay is `probe * 2^min(max(failures-1, 1), 8)`, capped at 30s. Timers are unref'd and `close()` clears them and closes every pool. `replication.position` is detected per endpoint and stored; routing does not read it. Until P61 every operation uses the primary and `inspect()` stays `single-endpoint`. `routing.select` (P62), `consistency` and `maxLag` (P63), and `fallback` (P61) throw OKM1061. A bad shape is OKM1120. Migrations refuse a target that carries `primary`, `replicas`, `weight`, or `pool` with OKM1845. A shared helper measured larger than four inline copies (featureless app 89,597 / 29,770, postgres.js 41,122 / 14,483, PGlite 39,096 / 13,866, node-postgres 41,532 / 14,651, Bun.sql 40,381 / 14,168) and was not kept. The inline branch is featureless app 89,561 / 29,757 (+100 / +37 over 89,461 / 29,720), postgres.js 41,088 / 14,473 (+98 / +27), PGlite 39,062 / 13,858 (+105 / +32), node-postgres 41,498 / 14,643 (+96 / +27), Bun.sql 40,347 / 14,158 (+96 / +24). Runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (+0 / +0). The lazy topology chunk is 7,084 minified and 2,512 gzip. Stop line for P61, P62, and P63: routing, selection, and consistency live entirely in the lazy topology chunk. The connect entries get no more bytes. Bytes left under the gates after P60: postgres.js 512 minified / 127 gzip, PGlite 238 / 122, node-postgres 902 / 277, Bun.sql 853 / 312. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P60. Issue #85. D202 spends the `route` key on the connect entries and leaves the rest of this stop line in place. |
| D202 | P61 read routing spends the route key only | Reads on a topology use the first replica whose circuit is closed. The session is the topology handle, one per `connect()`, shared by `for()`, `unscoped()`, and a connection from `reserve()`. A successful write sets a flag and later reads use the primary until P63. `route` on a read and `db.using` with `"primary"` or `"replica"` override that. `onRoute` receives the choice. A throw is swallowed. `inspect()` stays `single-endpoint`; routing reasons there move to the M2 dev inspector. A string or pool serves either `route` from its one endpoint and has no `using`. OKM1843, OKM1844, and OKM1840 are raised from the lazy router, from `lock.ts`, and from the transaction facade. `client.ts` gains only the copy of `route` inside `callOptions`, plus the allowlist entries in `plan.ts`. Nothing else was added to the startup graph. Measured startup, minified / gzip, and gzip left under the existing gates: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (+0 / +0, 224 gzip left under 2,250); featureless app 89,751 / 29,780 (+190 / +23, 220 left under 30,000); postgres.js 41,137 / 14,506 (+49 / +33, 94 left under 14,600); PGlite 39,135 / 13,883 (+73 / +25, 97 left under 13,980); node-postgres 41,547 / 14,673 (+49 / +30, 247 left under 14,920); Bun.sql 40,395 / 14,177 (+48 / +19, 293 left under 14,470). Each connect entry and the featureless app keep at least 60 gzip bytes. `query-200` stays 17,087 instantiations and 6,280 types. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P61. Issue #86 |
| D203 | P62 replica selection stays in the lazy chunk | An automatic read runs every replica, then health (a closed circuit), then consistency and lag (a no-op until P63), then capacity, then `routing.select`. `select` is `weighted` (the default), `roundRobin`, `leastConnections`, `latencyAware`, or a function. `weighted` is nginx smooth weighted round-robin, with state on the topology handle. Equal weights rotate in config order. `weight <= 0` stays OKM1120 (`replicas[i].weight must be a positive number.`), so `weighted` only sees positive weights. `roundRobin` ignores weights. `leastConnections` uses the router's in-flight count around each execute, not `stats().inflight`. Ties keep config order. `latencyAware` is an exponentially weighted moving average with alpha 0.2. The first sample is stored as itself. Successful reads and the health probe both feed it. A replica with no sample loses to one that has one. Ties keep config order. There is no jitter. A function receives `{ name, weight, inflight, latencyMs, lag }` (`lag` is null until P63; `latencyMs` is null with no sample) and `{ op: "read" }`. It must return one of those candidate objects or its name. Any other return is OKM1120 and the message names the return value. The router does not catch a throw. An `OkmError` thrown from the function stays that error. A plain `Error` is still mapped by the client. The function runs only for an automatic read with at least two candidates. `route: "replica"` and `using("replica")` do not call it; with a function configured they take the first eligible replica in config order. A named strategy still runs for that route, and capacity does not. Saturated means the endpoint has a configured `max` and `stats()` reports `idle === 0`, `waiting > 0`, and `size >= max`. No `max` means not saturated. If every healthy replica is saturated, an automatic read uses the primary with reason `fallback:saturated`, or OKM1844 when `fallback` is `"error"`. One connection failure retries the read once with the same strategy over the replicas that remain. `consistency` and `maxLag` still throw OKM1061. Startup minified bytes are unchanged. Gzip moves are the bundler renaming same-length locals: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (+0 / +0, 224 gzip left under 2,250); featureless app 89,751 / 29,795 (+0 / +15, 205 left under 30,000); postgres.js 41,137 / 14,504 (+0 / -2, 96 left under 14,600); PGlite 39,135 / 13,884 (+0 / +1, 96 left under 13,980); node-postgres 41,547 / 14,673 (+0 / +0, 247 left under 14,920); Bun.sql 40,395 / 14,178 (+0 / +1, 292 left under 14,470). Each graph keeps at least 60 gzip bytes. The lazy topology chunk is 16,140 minified and 5,194 gzip (was 12,894 / 4,160). `query-200` stays 17,087 instantiations and 6,280 types. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P62. Issue #87 |
| D204 | P63 consistency is one client-wide watermark | The watermark lives on the topology handle, one per `connect()`. The root, `for()`, `unscoped()`, `using()`, and `reserve()` share it. The router is a shared pool and cannot see which `db.for()` client issued a statement. A per-`for()` watermark needs bytes in `client.ts`, so it stays deferred. The client-wide watermark is the conservative 0.5 behaviour: a write on any of those clients moves the watermark for all of them. Carrying a position across processes stays deferred (spec section 25). After a write commits, the watermark is `pg_current_wal_insert_lsn()` read after the commit, before the write's promise resolves. Autocommit `execute` and `batch` read it on the primary pool. Any primary connection is a safe upper bound. `tx()` reads it on the reserved connection immediately after a successful `COMMIT`. A `ROLLBACK` or a failed commit changes nothing. The read is not inside the transaction, and `COMMIT` and the select are not one message. One extra round trip is accepted. It runs only when replicas are configured and `routing.consistency` is `"session"` (the default). `"eventual"` and a lone primary do not read it. The watermark is monotonic: LSNs compare as `BigInt` from the `X/Y` hex form, and a smaller reading is ignored. A failed position read marks the handle position-unknown. The generation increments every time that flag is set. A primary probe raises the watermark and clears the flag only when it started in that generation and the flag is still set; a probe already in flight only refreshes the lag baseline. Automatic reads use the primary (`fallback:position-unknown`, or OKM1844 with `fallback: "error"`) until the next successful position read, from a later write or from a primary probe. `route: "replica"` and `using("replica")` are OKM1843 while the position is unknown. A replica serves a read when its last seen replay position is at or beyond the watermark. That cache is a lower bound, so a hit needs no round trip. Otherwise one on-demand `pg_last_wal_replay_lsn()` runs through the same `ReplicaState` seam as the probe, then the next candidate, then the fallback. Healthy replicas that miss the watermark or `maxLag` report `fallback:behind`. `routing.maxLag` is a string: `"5s"`, `"500ms"`, `"2m"`, `"16MB"`, `"512KB"`, `"1GB"`, or `"4096B"` (`KB` is 1024). A bare number is OKM1120. Byte lag is the primary insert position, sampled on the primary probe and refreshed on write, minus the replica replay position. Time lag uses `pg_last_xact_replay_timestamp()` only while the replica is behind the primary. A caught-up replica has lag zero, so an idle primary does not make replicas look stale. The timestamp column is on the replica probe only when `maxLag` is a duration, and that SQL stays in the lazy chunk. `maxLag` applies to automatic reads and to `route: "replica"` and `using("replica")`. Positions need `replication.position`, already detected per endpoint at connect. A primary or a replica without it cannot be checked: after a write, automatic reads use the primary and a required replica is OKM1843. `maxLag` excludes a replica without position support. A read with no write yet and no `maxLag` still uses replicas. `"eventual"` needs positions only for `maxLag`. `ReplicaCandidate.lag` is that byte lag, or `null` when either position is unknown. The wrote flag is gone. Cursor pages are separate operations, each checked against the watermark. `consistency` and `maxLag` leave the OKM1061 list. `routing` accepts `probe`, `select`, `fallback`, `consistency`, and `maxLag`. The consistency stage is async. The shared scratch list is copied off before the await. A successful `COMMIT` is observed on the reserved connection wrapper in `topology.ts`. `transaction.ts` is unchanged. Nothing was added to the connect entries, `client.ts`, `plan.ts`, or the runtime entry. Startup minified bytes are unchanged from D203. Gzip moves are bundler renaming: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (+0 / +0, 224 gzip left under 2,250); featureless app 89,751 / 29,801 (+0 / +6, 199 left under 30,000); postgres.js 41,137 / 14,502 (+0 / -2, 98 left under 14,600); PGlite 39,135 / 13,889 (+0 / +5, 91 left under 13,980); node-postgres 41,547 / 14,671 (+0 / -2, 249 left under 14,920); Bun.sql 40,395 / 14,183 (+0 / +5, 287 left under 14,470). Each graph keeps at least 60 gzip bytes. The lazy topology chunk is 21,194 minified and 6,576 gzip (was 16,148 / 5,196). `query-200` stays 17,087 instantiations and 6,280 types. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P63. Issue #88 |
| D205 | P64 proves the topology on streaming replication | The CI compose topology (one primary, `replica-a`, `replica-b`) already existed. P64 adds the conformance file and the harness helpers the tests need: an advisory lock around replay pauses, stop and start of one replica container, and a TCP delay proxy. `tests/topology-conformance.test.ts` is discovered by `scripts/postgres-suite.ts` and is not in `POSTGRES_EXCLUSIONS`. Named tests: `routing.auto` (configured replicas take reads with no option), `routing.classes` (writes, `batch`, locking reads, advisory locks, `tx()`, and every statement inside a transaction stay on the primary, asserted by endpoint), `routing.strict` (paused replay and both replicas stopped are OKM1843 for `route: "replica"` and `using("replica")`; `fallback: "error"` on an automatic read is OKM1844), `pool.separation` (one pool per endpoint, OKM1846 stays on that pool, `reserve()` on postgres.js and PGlite), `tx.affinity` (one primary backend for the transaction, its savepoint, and its batch), `consistency.position` (the write is visible, or the read uses the primary). Commit position: `pg_current_wal_insert_lsn()` after commit is past the in-transaction insert LSN and past the in-transaction `pg_current_wal_lsn()`, with `synchronous_commit` on and off, for one write, several writes, `tx()`, and `batch`. With synchronous commit off, the post-commit `pg_current_wal_lsn()` was behind the post-commit insert LSN on that sample. A paused replica, and both paused, fall back with `fallback:behind` and the row is the one just written. A seeded interleaving of writes, pauses, resumes, and reads had zero missing rows. The cached replay LSN only moves forward. A position read that fails after commit, and a role without execute on the WAL functions, both keep a paused replica off automatic reads (`fallback:position-unknown`) and make a required replica OKM1843. An idle primary with `maxLag: "500ms"` still serves a caught-up replica. Selection on the same topology: weighted shares, `leastConnections` under `pg_sleep`, `latencyAware` through an 80 ms delay on replica b, `maxLag` of `4096B` and `500ms` on paused replay, circuit open and close when a replica container stops and starts, replica failure under a find loop, a custom `select`, and primary fallback. A run of identical characters compresses, so the byte-lag case uses 12,000 random bytes. CI measurements, 40 inserts after 3 warmup, extra cost as the difference of the two samples' percentiles. Postgres 15: p50 0.24 ms (session 0.92, eventual 0.68), p95 0.36 ms (session 1.16, eventual 0.80). Postgres 18: p50 0.19 ms (session 0.90, eventual 0.71), p95 -0.48 ms (session 0.98, eventual 1.46); the negative p95 is one slow eventual insert. Fallback rate with consistency session, one writer of 20 inserts and two find loops, replicas replaying: Postgres 15 was 0 of 24 in 29 ms, Postgres 18 was 0 of 27 in 30 ms. A local sample was 1 of 24. Replica share was 64 of 64 reads on both majors, 8 rounds of 1 write and 8 reads. Smooth weighted round-robin now sets `current` to 0 for a replica that did not take part in the pick. Weights 5 and 1, one pick with only the heavier replica eligible, then 24 reads: before `baaaaabaaaaabaaaaabaaaaa` (first is the lighter), after `abaaaaabaaaaabaaaaabaaaa` (first is the heavier). Both windows are 20 and 4. The classifier is unchanged. A `select` that calls `nextval`, including a view with that body, is a read and fails on the standby. Raw SQL in M2 needs an explicit class. The fix is only in `src/runtime/topology.ts`. Startup minified bytes are unchanged from D204. Gzip is unchanged on this sample: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (224 gzip left under 2,250); featureless app 89,751 / 29,801 (199 left under 30,000); postgres.js 41,137 / 14,502 (98 left under 14,600); PGlite 39,135 / 13,889 (91 left under 13,980); node-postgres 41,547 / 14,671 (249 left under 14,920); Bun.sql 40,395 / 14,183 (287 left under 14,470). Each graph keeps at least 60 gzip bytes. The lazy topology chunk is 21,379 minified and 6,689 gzip (was 21,194 / 6,576). `query-200` stays 17,087 instantiations and 6,280 types. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P64. Issue #89 |
| D206 | GitHub issues and pull requests follow one standard | Labels are `type:` (`feat`, `fix`, `perf`, `docs`, `test`, `chore`, `refactor`, `security`), `area:` (`contracts`, `dialects`, `adapters`, `runtime`, `tooling`, from the directories under `src/`, plus `ci` and `docs`), and `needs:` (`decision`, `repro`, `design`). The GitHub defaults are removed. Every issue and pull request has exactly one `type:` label and at least one `area:` label. A pull request title is `type(scope): summary`. The branch is `pNN-short-name`. The body ends with `Closes #N`. The pull request milestone is the issue milestone. One milestone per release train, closed when that release ships. The project board has a Step field, saved views by status, by milestone, and a roadmap, and every item links its pull request. Merges are squash only and the branch is deleted. Issue forms set the type. `.github/workflows/pr-meta.yml` has two jobs: `labeler` sets `area:` from paths (`.github/labeler.yml`) on opened, reopened, and synchronize, then `lint` checks out the base and runs `scripts/pr-lint.ts`. They share one file because a label added with `GITHUB_TOKEN` does not start another workflow, so a separate lint workflow could run before the `area:` label exists. `lint` runs when `labeler` succeeded or was skipped. A `dependabot[bot]` pull request skips the closing line and the milestone; its title and labels are still checked. Dependabot groups GitHub Actions and npm updates weekly, with a `chore(deps)` prefix and the `type: chore` and `area: ci` labels. `.github/release.yml` groups generated notes by `type:`. The release workflow is unchanged: it still publishes from `changelog.md`, and its `github release` job closes the milestone through `scripts/github-release.ts`. No change under `src/` or `packages/`. The written standard is `docs/github.md`. Found in P64A |
| D207 | P65: the reference app is the end-to-end proof, and two defects it found are fixed | `packages/reference-app` is a private workspace package, a project tracker on public entry points only: workspaces as column-tenancy tenants, members, projects and tasks (`timestamps()`, `archivable()` with a cascade to tasks), comments, a `task_status` enum, the `task_is_open` function, the `active_projects` and `open_tasks` views, the managed `ref_app` role with default grants, and three migrations from `okm generate` (initial, an expand with a batched backfill, a contract). Its schema is the one source: the P65A fixture and its test are removed. The suite runs in the existing `postgres` jobs (15 and 18 on a pull request) and the `tarball` job, with no new check names. **What it proves:** every use case on real Postgres through `okmodel/testing`, the board in one statement and the report in two (`expectQueries`), `isolation()` on every tenant table; two preview jobs at once, each with a database provisioned from the head snapshot and sharing no rows; a rehearsal on a `CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE` clone of a populated database (`okm migrate check`, then expand, backfill, and contract), with the original unchanged and refused by `okm migrate check` as a protected target; reads on a replica, read-your-write with replay paused on both replicas, the report on a replica (`route: "replica"`); a typecheck against the declarations `okmodel` resolves to, including the packed tarball. **What it does not prove:** a UI, a server, load, or soak; schema-per-tenant and database-per-tenant (M5). A read-only reporting role is a deliberate non-goal: fine-grained grants are M2, so the app uses the managed application role only. The role's password and its `SELECT` on `okm_meta` are infrastructure steps (known limit). **Fixes:** (1) two applies on databases of one cluster both found a new managed role absent and every apply but the first failed on `pg_authid_rolname_index` with a raw driver error; on main, 20 of 20 rounds of four parallel provisions failed (60 of 80 applies). `createRoleOnce` in `src/dialects/pg/role/sql.ts` runs the plan's `CREATE ROLE` in a `DO` block that ignores `duplicate_object` and `unique_violation`. Its callers are the only two places that execute `CREATE ROLE`: the step runner (`runUnit` in `src/tooling/migrate/runner.ts`, which also creates the migration role and serves `migrate apply` and `migrate check`'s replay) and the snapshot install (`installSnapshot` in `src/tooling/migrate/snapshot.ts`, which serves an empty-target apply, `migrate check --provision`, and `provision()`). `okmodel/testing` renders `schema.catalog`, which has no roles. The `pg_roles` check stays the fast path and later `ALTER ROLE` steps still run. (2) `schema()` puts views in the query model, so `isolation()` tried to insert into a tenant view and failed with OKM1120; `readFacts` now skips entries with no table object in the catalog. A view that hides the tenant key stays OKM1820 in `okm check`; a read-only isolation check for views is an M2 follow-up. **Cost:** the runtime entry, the featureless app, the four connect entries, and `okmodel/pg` are byte-identical to main; `okmodel/testing` is +103 minified and +30 gzip (139,951 / 44,959), which `scripts/size.ts` reports and does not gate; type probes are unchanged. **Found and left as known limits:** two concurrent `ALTER ROLE` steps on one role fail with `tuple concurrently updated`; on a protected target a migration with a backfill stops after its expand steps (OKM1850 at the backfill, resumable with `--allow-protected`); the expand needs a reviewed `-- okm-allow OKM1531` on its own temporary not-null check (`docs/linter.md`), and the contract a reviewed OKM1512 |
| D208 | The startup hash leaves out roles and grants, and catalog checks read scratch schemas as `public` | Found by the reference app (P65) and fixed in P65A. (1) `okm migrate check` sets `search_path` to each scratch schema before it introspects it, because `pg_get_viewdef` and `format_type` qualify every name that is not on the path. A declared view and a function that takes a schema enum no longer fail with OKM1547 or OKM1020. Replayed steps rewrite `in schema "public"` to the scratch schema too, so default privileges stay there. (2) View scratch (`sealViews`) creates the catalog's functions after the stub tables and before the views, so a view that calls a declared function passes `okm check`. (3) `archivable()` writes the partial unique predicate as Postgres prints it, `(archived_at IS NULL)` or `("archivedAt" IS NULL)`. Stored catalogs with the old text change once. On a database created with the old text, `connect()` is OKM1520 and `okm check` is OKM1510 (OKM1529) until `okm generate`, an `okm-allow OKM1529` line, and `okm migrate apply` rebuild the same index. `okm migrate check` keeps OKM1547 at the migration that first created the index, as before the fix; no command rewrites a shipped migration and `lintFrom` does not lift it. (4) Function grants name argument types from `oidvectortypes(proargtypes)` with spaces removed, in introspection and in `okm doctor`, so a function with named arguments no longer drifts and doctor no longer skips it. (5) Grant targets and default privileges use the schema they are applied to, so `--provision` grants stay in its scratch schema. (6) Role, grant, and default-privilege objects are not part of the startup hash. A `schema()` catalog never has them, so `connect()` hashes it unchanged, with no runtime bytes. `okm_meta`, `okm_history`, `.okm/catalog.json`, and `.okm/catalog.hash` store the catalog without them (`startupCatalog`, `startupHash`). `okm check` and `okm doctor` still verify them. A catalog without roles hashes byte-for-byte as before (pinned in `tests/roles.test.ts`). `okm migrate apply` restamps a stored hash to the startup hash only when it equals the pre-D208 hash of a migration already in `okm_history`, including when nothing is pending, and prints `restamped <id>`. It is a `history-repair` write, so a protected target needs `--allow-protected`. Any other hash is left and stays OKM1520. (7) `db.views` is typed from `schema({ views })` on connected, scoped, and routed clients: camel-cased column names, each `string \| null`, with `find` (limit or `.all(reason)`), `one`, `count`, and `exists`. Type only. Sizes against main: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, featureless app 89,751 / 29,801, postgres.js 41,137 / 14,502, PGlite 39,135 / 13,889, Bun.sql 40,395 / 14,183, and `okmodel/testing` 139,848 / 44,929 are unchanged. node-postgres is 41,547 / 14,672 (gzip +1; the minified entry differs only in content-hashed chunk names of the same length). The archivable app is 99,664 / 32,956 (+23 / +4). Type cost: every query probe +28 instantiations and +31 types (`query-200` 17,115 / 6,311; `query-200-validate` 25,084 / 6,504 under 25,631 / 6,604; page, aggregate, and through 24,268 / 6,835; presets 19,226 / 7,176; tx 24,320 / 6,988); inferred-200 14,300 / 4,585; per added table 67. One declared view with a typed `find` adds 1,234 instantiations and 864 types over the same probe without views. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Not in this step: the raw driver stack when the database does not exist, exporting `Connected` and `RoutedClient`, and the application role's manual `GRANT` on `okm_meta`. Issue #141 |
| D209 | Gate M1 and release 0.5.0 | No correctness bug and no specification contradiction that needs a change under `src/`. The release matrix on Postgres 15, 16, 17, and 18, suite and tarball, including the reference app, passed with no failure. The routing, client-close, and selection timing tests passed on every major under the full suite, so their timeouts stay. Measurable DX, left: when ports 55432–55434 are taken, `bun run db:up` publishes other ports and records them only in `GITHUB_ENV`, so a later local process still dials 55432. CI writes those ports into the job env and is unaffected. Measurable performance, not a gate: this sample's app startup cold import is 15.061 ms, the node-postgres connect cold import is 18.338 ms, and the PGlite adapter entry is 15.410 ms, each above the 15 ms local reference. The CI cold-import gate is 25 ms and applies only to the runtime entry (2.347 ms). Nothing new is added for a watermark per `for()` client, a position carried across processes, or routing reasons on `inspect()`. Those stay deferred. **What 0.5.0 contains:** `connect({ primary, replicas })`, automatic read routing, `route` and `db.using`, `routing.select` and consistency, `onRoute`, OKM1840, OKM1843, OKM1844, OKM1845, and OKM1846 (D201–D208), the reference app, and this gate. The README states that surface and points at the reference app. Gated bytes match D208: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (812 minified and 224 gzip left under 6,100 / 2,250); featureless app 89,751 / 29,801 (1,149 / 199 left under 90,900 / 30,000); postgres.js 41,137 / 14,502 (463 / 98); PGlite 39,135 / 13,889 (165 / 91); node-postgres 41,547 / 14,672 (853 / 248); Bun.sql 40,395 / 14,183 (805 / 287). `okmodel/testing` is 139,951 / 44,959, reported. The lazy topology chunk is 21,380 / 6,698 (D205 recorded 21,379 / 6,689), not gated. Type probes are unchanged from D208. Within 3 percent of a ceiling, and unchanged: `query-200` 17,115 instantiations (185 under 17,300); `query-200+extensions` 17,149 (151 under 17,300); `query-200-validate` 25,084 / 6,504 (547 under 25,631, 100 under 6,604); page, aggregate, and through 24,268 / 6,835 (524 under 24,792, 96 under 6,931); presets 19,226 / 7,176 (529 under 19,755, 165 under 7,341); tx 24,320 / 6,988 (691 under 25,011, 168 under 7,156). No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. **Watermark sample** (`packages/bench/src/topology.ts`, Postgres 17, primary and two standbys, 200 timed inserts in each consistency mode after 10 warmup, then 200 writes while reads continue to at least 2,000). Recorded run: session p50 0.48 ms and p95 0.71 ms, eventual p50 0.33 ms and p95 0.61 ms, extra p50 0.15 ms and p95 0.10 ms. Fallback was 1 of 2,002 reads (`fallback:behind`). A second run on the same topology was extra p50 0.07 ms and p95 0.32 ms, with 2 of 2,001 reads on the primary. There is no gate on these numbers. The OKE `store.sql` prototype starts after this release. M1 is complete. Issue #91 |

| D210 | A field value is a leaf in the effective-filter walk | A write whose where has no effective predicate is OKM1102 on `update`, `delete`, `archive`, and `restore`. `undefined`, `{}`, and an object whose own values are all `undefined` have none. A field value other than `undefined` is a predicate: `null`, a scalar, an array, an object, and an operator. The walk does not enter a field value. It enters the where object's own keys, `and` (some branch has a predicate), `or` (every branch has a predicate, otherwise OKM1121; `or([])` matches nothing), and `not` only when `not` is the where itself. `emitWhere` skips `undefined` and an empty record, the same as before this walk, and does not call the predicate check. No preset, filter, tenancy, or relation code builds an empty `or()` branch. `or()` with a non-array or more than one argument is OKM1121 at the call (`Pass an array: or([a, b]).`); a non-array value in the planner is the same code, not `false`. An unknown key on the first argument of `update` is OKM1120, and the message says `timeout` and `signal` go in the second argument. `tx(fn, options)` is OKM1120 (`options go first: tx(options, fn)`). A bad option on `find`, `one`, and `count` returns a rejected query, so `safe` and `catch` see OKM1120. D125 is unchanged: a bare object in a where is OKM1121 for every column, and its fix names `eq`. `eq(value)` is the equality form, so a timestamp, a date, bytes, or a jsonb object (including `{}`) filters the matching rows. A bare object is still a predicate, so the planner raises OKM1121 and not OKM1102. A `Date` stays OKM1121 (D178). **Not reproduced:** `page` and `aggregate` already returned a rejected query (QA-M11). The tenant predicate was already on every tenant-table alias (QA-S4); the property test records it, including object-valued equality. **Sizes** against D209, minified / gzip, after D125 was restored. Runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (+0 / +0). Featureless app 90,535 / 30,026 (+784 / +225). postgres.js 41,918 / 14,746 (+781 / +244). PGlite 39,929 / 14,119 (+794 / +230). node-postgres 42,331 / 14,904 (+784 / +232). Bun.sql 41,179 / 14,440 (+784 / +257). One trim (shared `or()` strings and a smaller rejected read) saved 20 minified bytes and raised gzip, so it was not kept. Gates that were over on the earlier measurement are measured plus 50 minified and 20 gzip, rounded up. Restoring D125 left every graph under its gate, and no gate is lowered. Type probes are unchanged from D209. `query-200` stays 17,115 instantiations, 185 under 17,300. No other gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Found in P67. Issue #145 |
| D211 | Hand-edited migration SQL is linted per statement | The linter splits each step with a pure lexer in the tooling layer. It removes `--` and nested `/* */` comments. It keeps `'...'` with doubled quotes (`standard_conforming_strings` on), `E'...'` with backslash escapes, `"..."` identifiers, and `$tag$` dollar quotes. A different tag inside a dollar quote does not close it. Statements split on a top-level `;`. An unterminated string, identifier, block comment, or dollar quote is one opaque tail, and the lexer does not throw. Each statement is linted. The place is `step N` when the step has one statement and `step N statement M` (from 1) when it has more. Step numbers stay. Earlier and later steps are read the same way, so a later `USING INDEX` and a validate after `NOT VALID` are seen in another statement of that step and after a comment. `-- okm-allow CODE: reason` stays on the step. It silences that code on every statement of the step that triggers it. A code no statement triggers is OKM1510 (D192). `DO` and `CALL` are OKM1542 with the reason `the body is not analysed`, silenced only by `-- okm-allow OKM1542: reason` (D99). A dollar-quoted body of `CREATE FUNCTION` or `CREATE PROCEDURE` is not linted as statements. `ALTER COLUMN … TYPE` matches the same rules as `SET DATA TYPE`, with and without `USING` (OKM1524, OKM1527, OKM1533, OKM1538). A blank line inside a quote, a dollar quote, or a block comment stays in the step. A blank line outside still separates steps. `okm migrate plan`, `okm migrate apply`, and `okm migrate check` use this linter, so an error is OKM1510. Check and apply read the files. Plan lints the plan it generates. The planner still writes one statement per step, and a generated migration lints as before (D193, D196). Reference-app migrations had no findings before and have none after. No new error code. No gate, cap, or ceiling moves. Sizes against main, minified / gzip: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026; featureless app 90,535 / 30,026; postgres.js 41,918 / 14,746; PGlite 39,929 / 14,119; node-postgres 42,331 / 14,904; Bun.sql 41,179 / 14,440. `okmodel/testing` is 141,107 / 45,330, reported. Type probes are unchanged from D210. `query-200` stays 17,115 instantiations, 185 under 17,300. Found in P68. Issue #146 |
| D212 | A pull request skips real Postgres unless it asks | D151 ran the suite on 15 and 18 and the tarball on 18 for every pull request. Those suite jobs take about ten minutes and they set the wait. `bun run check` already runs PGlite and the in-process wire server. A pull request now runs `check` and `attest`. The label `needs: postgres` runs the same shorter matrix: the suite on 15 and 18, the tarball on 18. A pull request update is one Actions run. The suite and the tarball are jobs in that run when the label is already present. Adding or removing the label, without a push, starts `.github/workflows/postgres-pr.yml` and does not cancel the run. `bun run verify` is the run on this machine, one version or `--all`. The release gate and the weekly run still cover 15 through 18 for the suite and the tarball (D150). `main` still requires only the `check` status. Tooling and CI only. The runtime entry is unchanged. Issue #152 |
| D213 | CI runs the check steps in parallel | `bun run check` on one runner was the remaining wait, about six minutes, because format, lint, types, tests, and the package checks ran one after another. A pull request update starts that one run. CI runs the steps as `area / piece` checks (`CI / lint / static`, `CI / types / compiler`, `CI / test / <group>`, `CI / runtimes / portable`, `CI / package / checks`, `CI / attest / measure`, `CI / label / paths`, `CI / lint / pull request`). A long test group is split: `migrate` and `migrate pg`, `drivers` and `rows`, `schema`, `columns`, and `schema pg`, `tenancy`, `client`, and `client pg`, plus `package`, `contracts`, and `safety`. The required status on `main` is `gate / check`. It passes when every slice passed and fails when one did not. `bun run check` on this machine stays one command. The release workflow still runs that command. No runtime change. Issue #152 |
| D214 | Runtime reliability for a replica, a close, and error text (P69, QA-M1, QA-M5, QA-L8, QA-L9, QA-L11, QA-L12) | A read that a replica refuses or drops falls back to the primary on postgres.js, node-postgres, and Bun.sql, and `onRoute` reports `fallback:unhealthy`. The deep check walks `cause`, each `AggregateError.errors[]` entry, nested `code` and `errno`, and the Bun.sql codes. It has a depth of 8 and a cycle guard. It lives in the lazy topology chunk, and the startup graph keeps the shallow check. A driver error with an empty message takes the first inner message, or `driver error`. Closing a client before `connected` settles leaves a clear `unavailable` error on `connected` and no unhandled rejection. 42P01 and 42703 map to `undefined_table` and `undefined_column`, with a fix that names `okm migrate apply` and `okm migrate status`. TLS and certificate codes map to `unavailable` with reason `tls` and a fix that names `sslmode`. OKM1104 lists each accepted constraint once and names the tenant key once. OKM1200 reads `Validation failed: <field> <reason>`. Calls that share one caller signal share one abort listener. Cap for this step, once: no connect graph grows more than +800 minified or +250 gzip over D210, and the runtime entry stays within +100 minified or +40 gzip. Measured against D210: runtime entry unchanged at 5,288 / 2,026; featureless app +313 / +121; postgres.js +464 / +152; PGlite +464 / +138; node-postgres +464 / +144; Bun.sql +464 / +152. Each connect gate is raised to the measured value +50 minified and +20 gzip, rounded up to the next 50 and 10 (table below). Type probes are unchanged. | D216 | QA timeouts, packaging, CI, docs, and the 0.5.1 gate (P71) | QA-M3: reproduced on the P70 tree. Bun.sql with a row locked in another transaction and `timeout: 300` never settles, and neither does a caller `signal`. PGlite with the outer client used inside `tx()` queues behind the transaction; PGlite also ignores `statement_timeout` and blocks the JS event loop while a statement runs, so a JS race can never beat it. The fix was built and verified: a client-side race in the lazy watch path rejects with kind `timeout` or `cancelled` at the deadline (302 ms and 301 ms measured), the pooled connection stays busy until the statement really ends, a reserved connection is destroyed on release, and the PGlite outer-in-`tx()` call throws OKM1192 (5 ms, no false positive on an unrelated queued call). Sizes against main (runtime 5,288 / 2,026; app 90,851 / 30,157; postgres.js 42,382 / 14,898; PGlite 40,393 / 14,257; pg 42,795 / 15,048; Bun.sql 41,643 / 14,592): the Bun.sql graph fit at +108 / +39, but the PGlite graph measured +191 / +92 against the +150 / +50 cap for this item, with a single-site floor near +178. Per the escape hatch the code was removed, no gate moves, and M3 is a known limit: `timeout` and `signal` do not settle an in-flight statement on Bun.sql and PGlite, and the outer-client PGlite call queues. OKM1192 stays reserved and is not thrown. QA-R1: the pack step (`prepack`/`postpack` around `bun pm pack`) strips `workspaces` from the tarball manifest only; `tarball-smoke` asserts it. QA-R2: the suite preflight probes the primary, names the URL when down, and exits 1 under `CI=true`; each harness-gated skip counts toward `N Postgres tests SKIPPED`. `loadPostgresGate` itself never throws for a missing database: the always-on CI slices run gated files with no database and skip by design (D212). QA-R3: a `temporal / node` matrix job (22 and 24 with `temporal-polyfill`, 26 natively) joins the `gate / check` needs; the suite workflow proves the D215 pid probe refuses a transaction-mode pgbouncer with OKM1854 (round-robin rotation makes it deterministic), so it runs on label, weekly, and release without touching `release.yml`. No Bun-adapter lock-timeout job: M3 was removed, so there is no lock-timeout behavior to cover. QA-R4: `type-cost` measures `inferred-250` (17,650 instantiations) and fails above 21,180 (measured +20%); time is reported, not gated. QA-D1 to D8: D3, D4, and D7 already matched behavior; D1, D2, D5, D6, and D8 are fixed as documented in the changelog. The gate: runtime entry unchanged at 5,288 / 2,026; every other gate identical to main; `query-200` under 17,300; no gate, cap, or ceiling moves except the new R4 ceiling. No new ErrorKind, no new public option, no new exported symbol. Issue #149 |
| D217 | M2 Depth is four trains, and Access is first | M2 ships as 0.6 Access, then 0.7 Query depth, then 0.8 SQL lane, then 0.9 Tooling. Access is first because tenancy and grants are the isolation boundary the later trains have to obey, and the deferred 0.5.x items sit on that same code. The OKE `store.sql` prototype starts after 0.6, on the public API only. That replaces the start D209 recorded after 0.5. Step ranges: 0.6 is P80–P89 (P80–P86 are scheduled; P87–P89 are not), 0.7 is P90–P99, 0.8 is P100–P109, 0.9 is P110–P119. Each train ends with a gate step and a release commit, like P66. P86 releases 0.6.0. The later gates release 0.7, 0.8, and 0.9 when their prompts are written, and each does the D115 hygiene before `bun run bump release`. Lazy loading: every M2 feature loads lazily, or the step stops with the numbers first and does not move a gate. That is the D201 rule for the whole of M2. After 0.5.1 the featureless app has 13 gzip bytes left (90,851 / 30,157 against 90,900 / 30,170) and each connect entry has about 20. P80 measures where the bytes go and proposes gate options. Ali approves that proposal before P81. No gate moves in this decision. Assigned from the deferred list: 0.6 takes hidden columns in `where` and `orderBy` (QA-S1, D215; a decision with examples first, recommendation refuse with OKM1120 and an opt-in per column), tenant `onConflict` on a (tenant key, column) unique (OKM1104), QA-L4 constraint names and a rename design for the extra unique beside the composite primary key (§9.2 still requires it), the materialized-view `refresh()` handle (§5.7), the Bun.sql and PGlite timeout and signal race as a lazy design (QA-M3, D216 numbers), strategies `path` and `composite` (§9.1), strategy `rls` (§9, §9.4), column-level grants and the read-only reporting role (§5.7, D207), ownership (`ignored` and `owner:`, §5.1), the application role's `SELECT` on `okm_meta` (D208), the read-only view isolation check (D207), and `filters()` operators and relations beyond D215. 0.7 takes tools 2, 4, 5, and 7 to 15 (§13), `versioned` and `sortable` (§8), `morph` (§6.2), and `.stream()` (§10). 0.8 takes the SQL builder lane, typed raw SQL with an explicit class (§16, D205), `explain()` and the dev inspector with routing reasons (§5.4, §15.1), `okmodel/otel` (D80), the Neon adapter (§4), `okm driver test` (§4.2, §20), and typed `fn` calls and builder views if the budget allows (D190). `okmodel/pg/pg` and `okmodel/pg/bun` shipped in 0.2 (D156) and are not planned again. 0.9 takes the linter and safe rewrites still missing after 0.4 (§19.1), `okm pull` and plpgsql dependency verification on a scratch database (§5.7, §19.4), several catalogs, `external()`, and catalog export (§3.6), extension packs and the rest of `okm ext` (§4.1), the GitHub Action (§19.8), the programmatic schema API (D26), `okm import drizzle` (§19.4), and the M2 gate. `schemaPerTenant` and `databasePerTenant` stay M5. `table({ policies })` stays `later` except for the part `rls` ships (§6.4, §9.4). Labels `area: tenancy` and `area: grants` are added for this train. The path labeler does not set them. Found in P72 |

| D221 | Tenant onConflict names the unique without the tenant key (P81) | `onConflict: { on: "title" }` matches `UNIQUE (tenant key, title)`. The conflict target is the unique's columns in constraint order, including the tenant key, so Postgres hits that constraint. The inserted row is still stamped with the caller's tenant, and the update touches only the row that shares that key. Naming the tenant key in `on` stays OKM1104, matching the accepted-list sentence that the key is not named. A unique that does not include the key, including `.unique({ global })`, still matches the names the caller wrote. | D222 | `refresh()` is not added (P81) | §5.7 says to refresh a materialized view with `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`, that a `refresh()` handle waits for 0.6, and that materialized views own indexes and a `refresh` declaration. `REFRESH ... CONCURRENTLY` needs a unique index (OKM1822). The declaration `refresh: "concurrently"` already exists on `materializedView()`. The section does not define the handle: no arguments, no return type, and no statement of whether a plain view has it. This step does not invent that shape. The SQL form in known-limits stays the way to refresh. Issue #161 |
| D223 | The extra tenant unique keeps its TypeScript name key (P81, QA-L4) | The unique beside a tenant table's composite primary key is required by §9.2 and is not dropped. Tenancy stores it under the object key `{declared primary fields joined by _}_{tenant field}`, and the columns are those fields plus the tenant field. The constraint name is `{sql table}_{that key}_key`. Table `tasks`, casing snake, primary field `id`, tenant field `tenantId`: `tasks_id_tenantId_key` on `(id, tenant_id)`. The primary key is `tasks_pkey`. A column `.unique()` uses the TypeScript field alone, so `title` is `tasks_title_key` on `(tenant_id, title)`. An index uses SQL names, so `(tenant_id, title)` is `tasks_tenant_id_title_idx`. `archivable()` runs after tenancy and turns those uniques into partial unique indexes. The index name key is the SQL column names joined by `_`, so the same primary columns become `tasks_id_tenant_id_idx` with predicate `(archived_at IS NULL)`. `renameTable` and `renameColumn` keep the stored constraint name and nameKey. The new schema compiles the name from the current TypeScript fields. The planner pairs the same shape, ignoring nameKey, and emits `RENAME CONSTRAINT` when the name changed. `tasks` renamed to `items` renames `tasks_id_tenantId_key` to `items_id_tenantId_key`. Field `id` renamed to `taskId` with `.renamedFrom("id")` renames the column to `task_id` and the constraint to `tasks_taskId_tenantId_key`. Neither path drops the constraint. A name key made of SQL names would change `catalogHash` for every existing tenant table and plan a rename. The generator is unchanged. The pinned hash of the small `tasks` schema is a9c2ca6f3853e4eca2d7e2c679588cb36ff2109ebd7b3848a5d6245ef6b73f29. Issue #161 |
| D224 | The Bun.sql and PGlite in-flight timeout stays a known limit (P81, QA-M3) | D216 built the behaviour and removed it: a client-side race settled at the deadline, the connection stayed busy until the statement ended, a reserved connection was destroyed on release, and a PGlite call on the outer client inside `tx()` threw OKM1192. That fix grew Bun.sql by 108 / 39 and PGlite by 191 / 92. P81's gate is stricter for Bun.sql: it must not grow. The smallest hook in the lazy watch chunk shrinks every connect graph by 45 minified and is not that fix (D218). PGlite blocks the JavaScript event loop while a statement runs, so a timer in that chunk cannot beat an in-flight statement. The fix is not rebuilt on top of the hook, because the measured Bun.sql growth already misses the gate. No gate moves. `timeout` and `signal` still settle when the statement ends. | D230 | The Postgres suite is four shards, and the pooler proof is not on that path | A labelled pull request was waiting on `suite / 15` and `suite / 18`. Across the last five labelled runs the Postgres suite step was 554–644s and the job was 602–689s. Checkout, setup Bun, install, and build were each about a second. Starting Postgres was 18–29s. Installing pgbouncer was 10–16s and the pooler proof was 2–3s, and both ran after the suite on the same job. `tarball / 18` was 40–46s. `gate / check` did not need the suite: it finished in about 3s while the suite was still running, after lint, types, test, runtimes, temporal, and package. The long step is one database running every file. The files that stalled that run (isolation 74s, selection 70s, consistency 63s, tx 54s, then compatibility, the driver suite, topology conformance, and routing) are pinned to four shards, `isolation`, `selection`, `consistency`, and `tx`. Every other file is dealt in sorted order. Each shard has its own topology, so schemas and roles cannot collide. The shard that imports `dist/` is the only one that runs `bun run build`. Bun's download cache is restored from `bun.lock`. The Postgres image is pulled once per job. pgbouncer and the pooler proof are a `pooler / <version>` job beside the shards. `fail-fast` stays false. A pull request still runs 15 and 18, and release and weekly still run 15 through 18, through the same `versions` input. No test file is dropped. `gate / check` now needs `suite` and `tarball`. A slice that should have run must be `success`, including a Postgres slice while `needs: postgres` is present. Without the label those two are skipped and the gate allows the skip. The required check name is unchanged. No runtime change. The runtime entry stays 5,288 / 2,026. |

It is not in `connect()`. A view over tenant tables is `WITH (security_invoker = true)`. Other strategies omit it. Policies protect a missing predicate. The default-casing notes catalog moves from `79c039a8f2826eca3e01aa3c23214790639228c56dda9c89b68754fe602f9544` to `d1996ab9c5b461dc7d9a0f05cf151e9876a19e8b83cc6a2df7c779b73bb750aa` because that expression could not be applied: Postgres folded the bare name to `tenantid`. The column-tenancy fixture hash stays `df7f8549fea5cde8e35a21e84541900abfe30fd605f4af0e7e98792f66aab32b`. The path schema hash stays `6f52772adfc4551686d62973049f3f5482a7db07b83d8b0ed90db062350a6ccf`. No stored foreign-key edge was added. The planner pulls the referenced unique in front of the foreign key, and drops reverse that list. `parseCatalog` builds a materialized-view index with `materializedViewIndex`, so the document no longer depends on a column that is not in the catalog. Generate, apply, and check accept one column or several, unique or not. Table-index documents round-trip as before. Apply revokes `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, and `DELETE` on a materialized view from a role whose table defaults include those privileges. The catalog stays select only. `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA` that does not add the grantee fails with OKM1825 (`kind` `forbidden`). The fix is `ALTER SCHEMA <schema> OWNER TO <migration role>`. No new error kind. `okm migrate check` makes an existing migration role the owner of the scratch schema and retargets `ON SCHEMA` as well as `"public".`. The namespace grant recorded from a scratch schema uses the logical schema name. `withOwner(..., "ignored" \\| "external")` is read from `table.name`. Generate and provisioning omit those tables. `okm check` ignores them. `assertCreateRole` accepts `rolsuper` or `rolcreaterole`. | D237 | CRLF migration files and Windows paths (P88W) | Migration SQL is normalized when it is read. `\r\n` and a lone `\r` become `\n` before `parsePlan`, and that text is what apply, `okm migrate check`, the linter, status, and a backfill header see. A non-empty file that still has no steps, and no `-- no steps` line, fails with kind `invalid` and reason `no-steps`. It does not print `nothing to apply`, so a hand-written `truncate` is OKM1542 in LF and in CRLF. A leading UTF-8 BOM is stripped from migration SQL, catalog JSON, and `catalog.hash`. A NUL byte is treated as UTF-16: migrations use kind `invalid` and reason `utf16`, and a catalog uses OKM1027 with `fieldReason` `utf16`. No new error kind. The catalog hash is SHA-256 of the file after the BOM is removed and every `\r` is removed, in the writer and in the loader. `serializeCatalog` and `catalogHash` of an LF document are unchanged, so an upgrade from 0.5.1 or from an applied 0.6.0 catalog keeps its hash (D116). Config paths resolve from the project directory for schema, tables, migrations, out, seed, status, check, provision, apply, and dev. An absolute spec and a `..` spec both work. `artifactDir` drops a trailing backslash as well as a slash. `callerLocation` splits a frame on a backslash as well as a slash. `okm generate init` writes `.gitattributes` (`migrations/*.sql text eol=lf`, `*.catalog.json text eol=lf`, `.okm/** text eol=lf`) only when that file is absent. `engines.node` is `>=22.18`. Older Node prints that and the `bun --bun okm` command, then exits. Measured with `bun run size` inside `bun run check`: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, cold import 1.821 ms. The rule sees `\n`, `\r\n`, and a lone `\r`. `okm check` and drift omit policies when `relrowsecurity` is false, and they already compare `relforcerowsecurity` and the policy expression through the policy record. No catalog field was added, so an existing 0.6 catalog hash does not move. The first scoped statement on each endpoint runs one cached query for the role and the policy expressions. OKM1707 names the endpoint. `reason` stays `superuser`, `bypassrls`, or `owner`. A policy that does not match stays kind `invalid`. No new error kind. Inside `tx()`, `set_config('app.tenant', $1, true)` is queued at transaction start, before any savepoint, and it is set again when the statement's scope differs from the last one applied in that transaction. A rolled-back savepoint clears that memory so the next statement sets it again. The reporting role stays able to set `app.unscoped` itself. That is documented: the role is not tenant-safe against its own SQL. No public API change. The guard lives in the lazy rls module. The featureless app does not import it. | D241 | 0.6.0 release blockers from the second QA pass | Column grants and revokes lowercase only the privilege keyword. Column identifiers stay the stored bytes and are quoted, including `tenantId`, `Mixed Case`, a reserved word, and a snake_case name, for `roles.reporting` and for `roles.columns` select and update. A postgres.js connection that loses its socket inside `tx()` is not written again. A later statement would schedule `socket.write` with `setImmediate` after the socket is null, and that timer throws outside the query promise. Leaving the write unsent keeps `globalThis.setImmediate` as the process installed it. The statement and the transaction reject with `unavailable`, or OKM1401 when the loss is during `COMMIT`. The process does not see an uncaught exception, and the next call uses a new connection. node-postgres maps a socket error that arrives before the next statement to the same unavailable. Bun.sql maps `connection must be a PostgresSQLConnection` the same way. `okm migrate check` lints before it replays. The replay locks `search_path` to the scratch schema, revokes `public` for that transaction, restores the ACL before a clean commit, and rolls back when the public catalog hash would change. A non-transactional statement that names `public` is refused before it is sent. Statements of different scopes on one connection run one at a time, each with its own `set_config`. `tx({ timeout })` and `batch({ timeout })` on Bun.sql and PGlite are OKM1120, reusing the per-call helper. A `signal` is not refused. OKM1550 flags a create or alter policy on a tenant table that is not the emitted expression, not only `USING (true)`. The policy guard is OKM1708. OKM1707 stays the role. The guard resolves tables with `to_regclass` on the search path. It still runs once per endpoint and generation, so a policy weakened while the process stays up is noticed on reconnect. CRLF normalisation rewrites `\r\n` inside SQL string literals. A scoped read is about 0.3 ms, a column-tenancy read about 0.06 ms, and a statement inside `tx()` about 1 ms slower. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No public API beyond these fixes. package.json stays 0.6.0. No tag and no publish. Runtime entry 5,288 minified and 2,026 gzip. The featureless app is 90,824 / 30,166, under 90,900 / 30,170. PGlite connect is 40,297 / 14,269, under 40,450 / 14,280. postgres.js connect is 42,162 / 14,866. The postgres.js adapter adds 14,900 minified bytes over the runtime entry, under 15,093. Issue #198 |
| D242 | 0.6.0 blockers from the third QA pass | `okm migrate check` no longer revokes `public` or updates `pg_namespace`. The replay creates the scratch schema inside one transaction, sets `search_path` to it, and ends with `ROLLBACK`. `pg_current_xact_id()` must be unchanged. OKM1551 flags `COMMIT`, `END`, `ABORT`, `ROLLBACK`, `BEGIN`, `START TRANSACTION`, `PREPARE TRANSACTION`, and `COMMIT PREPARED` in a step, including a `COMMIT` inside a `DO` body. A bare plpgsql `BEGIN` or `END`, `END IF`, and `ROLLBACK TO` do not match. `-- okm-allow OKM1551: reason` lets apply run the step. Check still refuses it. `VACUUM`, `CREATE DATABASE`, `DROP DATABASE`, and `ALTER SYSTEM` are skipped and named. Concurrent index, reindex, and refresh run without `CONCURRENTLY`. `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` runs in the transaction. A check passes as a superuser and as a `NOSUPERUSER` `NOBYPASSRLS` owner. A postgres.js reserved connection installs `onclose` on the first statement. A later statement, savepoint, or `COMMIT` after the socket closes is not written. The error is `unavailable`, or OKM1401 when a `COMMIT` may have happened. A node-postgres or Bun.sql stream interrupted by a lost connection is `unavailable`. A postgres.js `stream()` can still end a page early with no error, and a later `cursor.next()` on a dead socket can throw outside the promise. That loop is about 264 minified bytes and waits on 0.7, after adapter and featureless-app bytes are reclaimed. No catalog hash change. No public API change. package.json stays 0.6.0. P90 reclaims bytes first: PGlite and node-postgres gzip headroom, the OKM1120 timeout message in the lazy registry, one copy of `refuseWhenLost` and `arm`, and featureless-app headroom. P91 is the postgres.js stream loop, a dedicated missing-table reason, and migrate-check cleanup. P92–P98 are the query-depth features (tools 2, 4, 5, and 7–15, `versioned`, `sortable`, `morph`, `.stream()`). P99 is the gate and the 0.7.0 release, with the D115 hygiene before `bun run bump release`. No source, public API, gate, or catalog hash changes in this decision. Issue #220 |
| D244 | A lost connection is a driver code or a connection SQLSTATE (P89C) | `isConnectionFailure` does not read error text. A driver `code` of `CONNECT_TIMEOUT`, `CONNECTION_CLOSED`, `CONNECTION_ENDED`, `CONNECTION_DESTROYED`, `ECONNRESET`, `ECONNREFUSED`, `EPIPE`, `ETIMEDOUT`, or `ERR_POSTGRES_CONNECTION_CLOSED` is lost. A SQLSTATE in class `08` or `57P01`–`57P03` is lost. Any other server SQLSTATE is not lost, whatever the message says, including `53300`. `grant CONNECT on table` (`0LP01`, message `invalid privilege type CONNECT for relation`) and a check named `"no_CONNECT_zz"` (`23514`) reject with that database error. The connection can be used again and it holds no lock. `pg_terminate_backend` stays lost on postgres.js, node-postgres, and Bun.sql. `isConnectionErrno` also treats `ENOTFOUND`, `EHOSTUNREACH`, `ENETUNREACH`, and `EAI_AGAIN` as lost. Message matches in the topology unreachable check are gone. Bun.sql still recognises two exact driver texts that carry no code, `Connection closed` and `connection must be a PostgresSQLConnection`, and stamps SQLSTATE `08006`. A closed pool, and a postgres.js or Bun.sql reserved connection that is already closed, reject with SQLSTATE `08006`. A message-only closed-pool error, with no code and no SQLSTATE, is not lost. node-postgres stamps `code` `CONNECTION_CLOSED` when the client has already emitted `error`, and does not read the query message. No new error kind. No public API change. No catalog hash change. A live session that was misclassified as lost skipped rollback and was not released. Apply uses one connection, so the drain `select 1` waited on that session while it held locks. A backend that is actually gone does not hold locks, and the existing terminate tests already exit. That release path is unchanged. Issue #222 |
| D245 | postgres.js stream loop does not fit; migrate check skips a cleanup connection (P91) | A postgres.js `stream()` still uses the driver's cursor. Reproduced on Postgres 17 with `pg_terminate_backend`: killing the backend between pages makes the next `cursor.next()` return `{ done: true }` and no error, with the rows already yielded intact; a `FETCH` write queued with `setImmediate` then throws `TypeError: null is not an object (evaluating 'socket.write')` outside the iterator promise. The replacement is a reserved connection and `FETCH`, loaded by `import()` on the first `stream()` call. Measured with the size script: the lazy chunk is `src/adapters/pg/postgres-stream.ts` at 454 minified bytes and pulls in `src/adapters/pg/cursor.ts` at 244.
