PGlite 40,204 / 14,201, node-postgres 41,893 / 14,732, and Bun.sql 41,387 / 14,462 stay put. The loop is not in the tree (D201). No gate moves. `hintOf` already returns reason `undefined_table` for SQLSTATE `42P01`, with a fix that names `okm migrate apply` and `okm migrate status` (D214). §14's nearest-name hint for an unknown table, field, preset, or option is `nearestName`. There is no B15 section in the design docs. The editor diagnostic already names `missing`. No new error kind. `okm migrate check` does not open a cleanup connection when the replay rolls back and `pg_current_xact_id()` is unchanged, including when a later error still rolls that transaction back. A step that commits still drops the scratch schema on a new connection, and a lost connection still confirms the drop. The truly-lost release path in D244 is unchanged. No catalog hash change. No public API change. Issue #209. D256 does not ship that loop. `.stream()` walks keyset batches, so the product gap closes without those adapter bytes. |
| D246 | First-or-create reads the winner in a second statement; a repeated update target is OKM1120 (P92) | Both tools were already in the runtime. `insert` with `{ on, return: true }` and `update` of a list already had their public types, so those types are unchanged. The audit found two gaps. First-or-create was one statement: a data-modifying CTE that unions the insert with a select of the existing row. That CTE shares one snapshot, so a concurrent insert that commits while the statement waits is invisible and the loser returns no row. §5.3 allows 1–2 statements. The insert is now `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (target) DO NOTHING RETURNING`. When an input key is missing from that result, a second statement selects those keys. `READ COMMITTED` gives that statement a new snapshot, so both callers get the one row and the existing row is not overwritten. The lookup runs only on that path. Insert rows win when both appear. A partial unique on an archivable table adds `and t."<archivedAt>" is null`, or `and t.archived_at is null` when casing is `"snake"`. A primary-key conflict does not. Tenant tables keep D221: the target includes the tenant key, and the keys are taken after the stamp. `.sql()` shows the planned insert only. Inside `tx()` both statements use the reserved connection. Inside `batch()` the lookup is a second batch, and a failure's index maps back to the operation. The lookup chunks at 2,048 parameters. The per-row update list was already one `UPDATE` with `CASE` per field, chunked the same way as insert, tenant-scoped, and `{ count }` when nothing is returned. A miss is not an error: `count` is the rows changed, the same shape as a single-target update. An empty list plans no statement and returns `{ count: 0 }`. Guarded fields stay OKM1190. A hidden field stays writable, as on a single update (§6.7). Naming the same resolved target twice, including an `id` and a `where` of that same id, is OKM1120 (`update on <table> lists the same target twice.`, fix `List each row once.`). An empty `set` on a list item is OKM1120 `update set is empty.`. The new code stays in `write.ts`, `conflict.ts`, and `batch.ts`, which already load on first use. No public type change. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. Measured with `bun run size` inside `bun run check`: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, cold import 1.802 ms. Featureless app 90,714 / 30,148 (was 90,835 / 30,169), cold 10.645 ms, stubbed 5.612 ms, total graph 210,727 / 67,600 (was 208,799 / 67,179; the extra bytes are the lazy write path). postgres.js 42,173 / 14,855, cold 10.988 ms, stubbed 3.768 ms, adapter incremental 15,069 of 15,093. PGlite 40,204 / 14,197, cold 15.833 ms, stubbed 3.174 ms. node-postgres 41,893 / 14,728, cold 11.881 ms, stubbed 3.077 ms. Bun.sql 41,387 / 14,467, stubbed 2.675 ms. The PGlite connect sample in this run is above the 15 ms local reference; the stubbed sample is not, and cold import is not gated. Connect minified bytes match. Gzip moved by at most 6 bytes. Bun.sql is +5 and the startup chunk stays 40,522 bytes with the entry at 865, so that move is minifier identifier assignment. Headroom under the app gate 90,900 / 30,170 is 186 / 22. query-200 is 17,171 instantiations and 6,328 types (129 under 17,300). A where value that is a class instance is compared by its own text: Temporal uses `toString`, `Date` uses `toISOString`, and bytes use hex. A value with no stable text gets a token unique to that call, so two different values are not reported as one target. A list of one item skips the check. The returned row is matched to the input key by encoding it again, so a timestamptz or date unique still returns the existing row, including when the other caller committed it. An empty `set` is OKM1120 (`update set is empty.`). No gate moves. Issue #210 |
| D247 | Aggregate time buckets load in their own chunk (P95) | `bucket(column, unit, { tz, as, weekStart? })` is a tagged helper, like `eq`. It goes in the existing `groupBy` array beside field names. JSON cannot create one (`Symbol.for("okmodel.bucket")`). The aggregate chunk tests that symbol and does not import the helper. Validation and SQL load from `bucket-group.ts` only when `groupBy` contains a bucket. A plain aggregate keeps the old SQL (`group by t."status"`, no `date_trunc`). Units are second, minute, hour, day, week, month, quarter, and year. `as` is required, names the result key, is usable in `orderBy`, and must not clash with a group field, another bucket, or count, sum, avg, min, or max (OKM1120). An empty `as` is refused. Day, week, month, quarter, and year return `Temporal.PlainDate`, the local calendar date the period starts. Second, minute, and hour return `Temporal.Instant`, the period start. A `date` column refuses those three units before any SQL (OKM1120). A nullable column includes null. A NULL timestamp is one group: the `as` key is present and the value is null. `tz` is required on `timestamptz` and refused on `date`. `timestamp` without a zone is refused, and the message names `timestamptz` and `date`. An IANA name is checked with `Intl.DateTimeFormat` before any SQL (OKM1120). `weekStart` is monday (the default, ISO), sunday, or saturday, and only with unit week. Calendar SQL is `(date_trunc(unit, column AT TIME ZONE tz))::date`. A sunday or saturday week shifts in local time before and after the trunc (one day, or two), not inside it. Monday does not shift. A `date` column omits `AT TIME ZONE`. Clock SQL is the three-argument `date_trunc(unit, column, tz)`, which exists on Postgres 12 and later. The zone is one bound parameter. The expression is written once, in the select list, and `GROUP BY` and `ORDER BY` use select-list ordinals, because a second copy would bind a new parameter and Postgres would treat it as a different expression. There is no `having`, no gap filling, and no `onDay` helper. Tool 5 stays deferred. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate move. A lazy chunk still costs about 20 gzip on a connect entry through minifier name pressure. The startup graph does not contain `date_trunc`, and replacing the bucket module with empty functions measured the same featureless-app byte. On the postgres.js connect startup graph, identifiers go from 8,237 to 8,239, one-character names from 3,386 to 3,352, two-character names from 981 to 1,016, and identifier characters from 27,133 to 27,177. That decides the size strategy for the rest of 0.7: another lazy chunk spends short names in every connect entry. The featureless-app gate has 29 gzip left (30,141 under 30,170). Measured with `bun run size` against current main (D246): runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, cold import 2.424 ms (an earlier sample was 1.945 ms). | D249 | Atomic operators stay the documented four; a claim is lock skip plus limit (P93) | The audit found the operators already shipping. `inc`, `json.set`, `arr.append`, and `arr.remove` compile in `write.ts`, which loads on the first write. `json.set`, `arr.append`, and `arr.remove` were already typed on those columns. `inc` was typed on every update field. `find` already takes `lock: "update"` or `"share"` and `wait: "nowait"` or `"skip"` inside `tx()` (OKM1830). There is no `findMany`. `lock: "skip"` on a read is OKM1120 and the message names `lock: "update"` and `wait: "skip"`. `nowait` stays a read wait. `wait` on `update` is OKM1120. The gap was the update claim (tool 14). `update` of one target accepts `lock: "skip"` together with `limit`. The statement updates the table and selects the physical row with `tableoid` and `ctid`, skip locked, and a bound limit. `ctid` alone repeats on another partition and on an inheritance child, so a limit of one could change a row the filter did not select. The query table model has no partition flag and no inheritance-parent flag. The catalog records declarative partitioning on the table definition and does not record an inheritance parent. A primary key is optional, so the claim does not switch to it. Every claim matches both columns. A path-parent update that used to rewrite by `ctid` matches the same pair. A path list update still applies the caller's where and does not rewrite by `ctid`. The write owns that statement, so it runs on its own or inside `tx()` and `batch()`. Rows another transaction holds are skipped. A list cannot claim. `limit` without `lock`, or `lock` without `limit`, is OKM1120. `limit` must be an integer from 1 to 2147483647 (OKM1121). A path-parent move together with a claim is OKM1120. There is no `orderBy` on a claim. `inc` is now only a number or a bigint column. A text column is OKM1124 (`inc adds to a number. title is a text column.`). A `numeric` or `bigint` stored as a string is still outside that type, the same hole as `sum` and `avg`; the statement accepts `inc` when the database type is numeric. A null number stays null. There is no `dec`, no `mul`, and no coalesce helper. A per-row list that sets one field with an operator in one row and a plain value, or a different operator, in another is OKM1120 (`qty is both inc and a value in this update.`). The same operator on every row stays one update. A repeated target stays OKM1120 (D246). New code stays in `write.ts` and `lock.ts`. Both already load on first use. No new lazy chunk. No runtime entry change. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No public return type change. No gate move. | D250 | `find` and `count` take `search` (P97) | `find({ search })` and `count({ search })` match a column built for search. A string is enough when the table has one such column. `{ for, in, match, language }` names the text, the column, how to match, and the language. `in` is required when there is more than one, and the error lists those columns by name. `match` is `natural` (the default, web-search style), `all-words`, `phrase`, or `advanced` (raw syntax). An omitted language uses the database default. `find` returns the closest rows first, then the primary key. An `orderBy` you pass replaces that order. `count` does not order. `search` combines with `where`, `select`, `include`, and presets. `page` refuses `search` in this version (OKM1120). `one` and `exists` do not take it. `matches(q, { match, language })` stays in `where`. `{ mode, config }` is not accepted. `relevance(q, { match, language })` is the `orderBy` form. `search` stores the same operand as those helpers (`searchValue`) and the SQL reader is the one they already use, so the text is the same. The rewrite loads from `text-search.ts` on the first call that passes `search`. That chunk does not import `matches.ts` or `relevance.ts`, so the featureless app still shakes both. Errors name what you passed and what to do. They do not name the column type, except the raw syntax under `advanced`. Highlighted snippets, a search of a plain text column, a helper that fills a search column from text, and keyset pages ordered by relevance are not built. A named query is a preset or a function that returns `find` options. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate move. | D252 | Descendants walk the parent column; sync locks, then writes (P96) | `descendants(id)` and `ancestors(id)` are one recursive statement on a self-relation. `many` walks from parent to child. `one` walks the other way. `relation` is required when more than one self-relation has a different foreign key (OKM1120, and the fix lists the names). `depth` is an integer of steps from the start, or omitted to walk the whole tree. `depth` 0 returns nothing unless `self` is true, which returns the start row. A row whose id is already on the path is not entered (`<> all(seen)`). There is no silent depth cap. A missing id returns no rows, including with `self`. The root has no ancestors. `where`, `select`, and `orderBy` match `find`. The default order is depth, then the primary key. There is no `include`. `where` and a preset filter the rows that come back and do not cut the walk short. Tenancy and the active set apply on the start row, on every step, and on the result, so an archived row is not crossed. A primary key of more than one column besides the tenant key is OKM1120. The statement starts with `WITH`, which the router classifies as a write, so the walk runs on the primary. `ltree` is not read. The extension mechanism defines types, operators, and functions. It cannot change this statement. A later index can. No new extension pack. `sync(id, relation, ids)` sets a to-many relation to exactly those ids. Two statements, one transaction. The first locks the parent row. The second adds and removes links. A single statement took the lock and still inserted from the snapshot it had when it started waiting, so two syncs of one parent could hit the join-table primary key. The second statement starts after that wait and sees the committed links. A join table gains and loses link rows and does not delete the related row. A one-to-many sets or clears the nullable foreign-key columns and leaves the row. The tenant column is not cleared. A non-nullable foreign key is OKM1120 before any write, because clearing the link would delete the row. A `one` relation is OKM1120. A join column with no null, default, identity, or generated value is OKM1120. A client-filled id is that case. Duplicate ids count once. An unknown related id, or an unknown parent, writes nothing and throws `not_found`. The missing-id message gives the count, not the values. The result is `{ added, removed }`, sorted by text. `expect(n)` throws `not_found` when the number of changed links is not `n`, after the statements, the same as `update`. Inside `tx()` the throw rolls back. `sync` does not set `~plan`, so a batch refuses it with the existing OKM1121. Presets apply to the parent row only. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate move. The walk and the sync load on first use (`tree.ts`, `sync.ts`). The walk and the sync share the loader `page` and `aggregate` already use. `expect` is a branch on that loader, so `sync` does not carry its own handle. `plan.ts` and the public types add no runtime bytes. The reserved preset names live in the preset chunk, which the stubbed gate does not include. | D254 | `versioned()` and `sortable()` (P98V) | `versioned()` adds `version integer not null default 1`, guarded and sealed. An update passes the version it read (`update({ where, set, version })`, and each list item the same). The check and the bump are one statement: matching rows are locked, and the write runs only when every one is still at that version. A miss stays `{ count: 0 }`. A stale row is category `conflict` and writes nothing. The message names the fields and the version, and says to reread and retry. No new error kind. `inc` and the other atomic operators still bump `version`. `returning` includes the new version. A claim (`lock: "skip"` and `limit`) updates free rows at that version and conflicts only when nothing was updated and a free row is at a different version. First-or-create and `onConflict: "ignore"` do not change the existing row. An upsert, `.all()`, a missing `version`, `version` on a table that is not versioned, and a path-parent change in the same update are OKM1120. `delete`, `archive`, and `restore` do not check or bump `version`. `sortable(groupBy?)` adds `position integer not null` with no default, guarded and sealed. Positions are dense positive integers, unique per tenant and group. Fractional or rank keys were the other option: one row changes, but two moves into the same gap can pick the same key unless a unique index rejects one, the keys are not dense, and the gap eventually needs a rebalance. Integer renumbering lets the unique index reject a duplicate and keeps `1..n`, and a move rewrites the rows whose positions change. Moves in one group wait on one advisory lock, taken in its own statement before the renumber reads the group. The unique index is checked per row and is not deferrable, so the move writes the new positions as negatives and flips them to positive in the same transaction. A position that is already negative is flipped too. `via()` is OKM1060, because the tenant key is not a column and the index could not separate tenants. The index names the tenant columns itself (D168). On an archivable table it is partial (`archived_at IS NULL`). `move(id, { before })` or `{ after }` returns `{ position }`. A missing id is `not_found`. An anchor in another group is OKM1120. Changing the group column is OKM1120. New rows insert at `max(position) + n`. The lock statement runs first and the max is the next statement in that transaction, so an insert that commits while the lock is waited for is visible. Several groups in one insert lock in sorted key order, then read each max. An insert outside `tx()` is still one atomic batch. A delete leaves a gap. Adding `position` to a table that already has rows is `add-column-required`. `find` and `page` order by `position`, then the primary key, when `orderBy` is omitted. `one()` is not reordered. `move` is declared by augmenting `TableExtras` from `okmodel/traits`, not by a per-table conditional on `TableApi`. `version` on `UpdateTarget` is optional. The runtime requires it on a versioned table. The hash function does not change. A schema that calls either trait hashes differently because the column, and the position index, are catalog objects. The featureless app does not import `okmodel/traits`. The check loads from `version.ts` on the first versioned update, and the move loads from `sort.ts` on the first insert or move. | D255 | `morph` is a closed list and a flat union (P98M) | `morph("commentable", ["tasks", "lists"])` on the table that holds `commentableType` (text, the target's table name) and `commentableId`. The id matches each target's one primary-key column. A tenant key is not that column. Include is `true` and returns the matching target row, or null when the row is missing or the stored type is outside the list, as a flat union discriminated by the type field. `morphMany("comments", "commentable")` on each target is an ordinary many: include, limit, order, and `has` / `none` / `every` follow `many`, including the per-parent limit. `morphTo(name, type, id)` sets both columns. One without the other is OKM1121. A type outside the list is OKM1120. An unknown target, a missing column, a key of more than one column, or an id type that does not match is OKM1020, OKM1021, or OKM1022 and names the table and the column. The relation is not a catalog object, so two schemas that differ only by it hash the same. Deferred, and OKM1120 when called: `has` / `none` / `every` on the morph, `select` / `where` / `orderBy` / `limit` on that include, a nested morph include, `sync` of a morph, and a tree walk through a morph. A preset on the target table does not filter the include. A preset on the query does. No new error kind. No gate move. The resolver lives in `morph.ts` and tree-shakes the way `manyThrough` does. Include SQL is the existing include chunk. The half-set check is the existing write chunk. The startup hook is the table lookup and the tenant fields passed into `resolve` in `schema.ts`. | D256 | `.stream()` walks keyset batches on every driver (P98) | `.stream()` does not open a server cursor and does not read the `stream` capability. That flag still means the driver can open one (postgres.js, node-postgres, and Bun.sql yes; PGlite no). A server cursor stays a later opt-in. The postgres.js `FETCH` loop in D245 stays unbuilt. Each batch is the keyset `page()` already plans: the caller's `orderBy`, then each primary-key column the caller did not name, ascending, nulls last. No `orderBy` means the primary key ascending. The default batch is 1000. `.stream({ batch })` takes an integer from 1 to 2147483647 (OKM1121 otherwise, OKM1120 for any other key). `limit` on the find is the total. `.all(reason)` with no limit streams the whole set. Both together still honor `limit`. `limit` 0 yields nothing and runs no query. A table with no primary key is OKM1120 (`stream() needs a primary key on <name>. A keyset batch orders by that key.`). `search` and a window in `select` are OKM1120. `one()`, `count()`, and `exists()` are OKM1120. `select`, `include`, presets, tenancy, and the archive set are the same as `page()`. Nullable sort keys follow `page()`. Early `break` finishes the current batch, which is an ordinary query, so the connection returns to the pool. Rows another transaction commits during a long stream can be seen or missed. The snapshot is `tx({ isolation: "repeatable read" })`. `signal` goes to every batch. If it is already aborted before a batch, the stream throws `cancelled` (`The stream was cancelled.`) and does not start that query. `timeout` is one deadline from the start of iteration. Between batches, a passed deadline throws `timeout` (`The stream timed out.`). On a driver that can cancel, each batch's statement timeout is the time still left, further capped by the session statement timeout when that is shorter and positive. PGlite and Bun.sql do not take a positive statement timeout, so the budget is checked before each batch and a batch already running finishes. A non-positive timeout is OKM1121. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate move. Measured after rebasing onto D255: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, cold import 1.690 ms. The +57 minified is the stream arguments (+12) and forwarding the pin through `callOptions` (+45), under the 200-byte startup stop. The startup hooks are the stream arguments on the read handle and the pin forwarded by `callOptions`. `read-stream.ts` and the router's pin check load with the stream and the topology chunk (D248). On `connect({ primary, replicas })` the first batch is routed like a find. Later batches stay on that endpoint (`onRoute` reason `stream:<name>`) and a connection failure does not move. Inside `tx()` every batch uses the transaction connection. `versioned()` and `sortable()` shipped as D254. `morph` shipped as D255. |

| D259 | PGlite numeric parameters, an explicit undefined search, the rest of the prototype keys, and a self-child sync (P99 QA) | Four fixes before 0.7.0. No new error kind. No public type change. No catalog hash change. No gate move. PGlite's serializers were identity, so a JS number or bigint that `encodeBigintString` returns unchanged reached the bind writer and failed with SQLSTATE `08P01` (`insufficient data left in message`). Class `08` then classified that as `unavailable` and retryable. The fix is only in the PGlite adapter. `pglite-wire.ts` loads from `open` and is not in the connect startup graph. A number or bigint is its decimal spelling. A boolean is `t` or `f`. Parsers stay identity. An `08P01` is replaced with an error that has the same message and neither a SQLSTATE nor a cause, so the kind is `driver`. `08006` and the other connection failures stay `unavailable`. `find` and `count` keep an explicit `search` key when the value is `undefined`, and that value is OKM1120, the same as `""` and `null`. A call that omits the key does not load text search. `refusePrototypeKey` also refuses `valueOf`, `isPrototypeOf`, `propertyIsEnumerable`, `toLocaleString`, `__defineGetter__`, `__defineSetter__`, `__lookupGetter__`, `__lookupSetter__`, and `then`. The message stays `<where> <name> is a prototype key. Use another name for the result.` The helper stays in the bucket and window chunks. `sync` on a relation whose related table is the owner's table refuses an id list that contains the parent's own id, before any SQL, and writes nothing. Other relations are unchanged. Measured with `bun run size` against D258: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, cold import 1.722 ms, unchanged (812 / 224 under 6,100 / 2,250). Bun.sql 39,254 / 13,591 against 39,275 / 13,590 (−21 / +1). The gzip rises are the search-key check, which is shorter minified than the `!== undefined` test it replaced and compresses less well. postgres.js adapter incremental stays 15,069 of 15,093. PGlite adapter incremental is 11,387 of 12,010 (was 10,931). The wire chunk is lazy, so it counts there and not in the connect entry. query-200 stays 17,239 instantiations and 6,374 types. query-200+page+aggregate+through stays 24,436 / 6,928. The tagged-operator surcharge stays 639. Bun.sql retries a statement whose error is `Connection closed` up to twice, so the query after a backend killed during `.stream()` does not fail. Those decisions stored `uuidv7()` for a bare `t.id()` and for an omitted `defaults.id`. `uuidv7()` exists only on Postgres 18. Supported majors are 15 to 18, so that default fails with OKM1812 on 15 to 17. Pick `uuidv7` for a large write-heavy table: the ids are time-ordered and the index stays local. Random ids are the default elsewhere. The client generators `uuidv4`, `uuidv7`, and `okid` are unchanged. An existing catalog whose id default is `uuidv7()` plans `ALTER TABLE … ALTER COLUMN … SET DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()`. The lock is `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE`. The statement does not rewrite the table. The linter is clean. Keep the old behaviour with `defaults: { id: "uuidv7" }`. OKM1812 stays for an explicit `uuidv7()` below 18. Schema build and apply use `uuidv7NeedsPostgres()` (`uuidv7() needs PostgreSQL 18 or higher. minPostgres: 18.`). No second floor parser. | D263 | Fail closed after a backend kill, and settle postgres.js calls (0.7 QA) | A reserved Bun.sql handle snapshots `closures` after `reserve`. That counter only grows. `dropped` still resets to 0 after a drain, so it cannot detect the reconnect. The next `execute` or `batch` on that handle, when the count has moved, rejects `DriverError("Connection closed", { sqlstate: "08006" })` before any statement on the new connection. The kind is `unavailable` and `retryable` is true. A scoped find does not come back empty. postgres.js copies `onclose` onto `sql.options` at startup and reads that property when a connection closes, so assigning it later is seen. The wait set lives on that options object, one set per pool. A wait with no call deadline is rejected with the same 08006 error only after every slot in that pool has closed, or immediately when the reserved connection that wait uses closes. A queued `sql.reserve()` is started again after a close, because postgres.js takes that reserve off its queue and drops it without settling (`ReadyForQuery` nulls a reserve `initial`). A wait with a deadline is not on the close hook. It ends at that deadline. One helper. No new retry. The first call after a backend kill on an idle connection stays `unavailable` with `retryable: true`. Bun.sql still retries a client read once at transaction depth 0. postgres.js and node-postgres do not. A `42601` is OKM1120 when the message contains `tsquery` or the statement contains `to_tsquery` (advanced, natural, all-words, and phrase all do). Other syntax errors stay `driver`. No new message strings. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No public API change. The postgres.js incremental gate is 16,000 minified bytes, was 15,500, for this close rule. Known limit, not fixed: an unhandled rejection from `fetchArrayTypes` when the backend dies during connect. `ReadyForQuery` returns `fetchArrayTypes()` with no `.catch()`. That function awaits `new Query` of `select b.oid, b.typarray from pg_catalog.pg_type …`. If the server terminates that backend before the query finishes, `closed()` rejects it with `write CONNECTION_CLOSED <host>:<port>` and the rejection is unhandled. Repro: default `fetch_types: true`, several queries in flight, `select pg_terminate_backend(pid) from pg_stat_activity where pid <> pg_backend_pid()`. `fetch_types: false` skips the query and also changes decoding for array types the caller did not register, so it is not set. Known limit, worked around: a queued `sql.reserve()` is dropped without settling when a connection slot closes. Repro: `max: 2`, hold one connection, queue `sql.reserve()`, `pg_terminate_backend` on the other backend. okmodel starts that reserve again. Measured with `bun run size`: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, cold import 6.610 ms. Stubbed featureless app 90,515 / 29,311 against the 90,572 / 29,338 baseline. postgres.js connect 40,255 / 14,035 (was 40,512 / 14,096). PGlite 38,650 / 13,433 (was 38,744 / 13,446). node-postgres 40,195 / 13,984 (was 40,283 / 13,991). Bun.sql 39,822 / 13,861 (was 39,883 / 13,846). postgres.js incremental 15,491 of 15,500. After rebasing onto D262: stubbed featureless app 90,823 / 29,432 against D262's 90,899 / 29,465. postgres.js incremental 15,953 of 16,000. Runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, cold import 4.640 ms. PGlite incremental 11,416 of 12,010. `bun run type-cost`: query-200 17,240 instantiations and 6,375 types. query-200+extensions 17,274 / 6,495, 5 under 6,500. query-200+page+aggregate+through 24,437 / 6,929, 2 under 6,931. The tagged-operator surcharge stays 639. Kill storm, 200 runs of 8 calls: Postgres 15 settled 1,600, hung 0, max 43 ms, 64 unhandled rejections; Postgres 18 settled 1,600, hung 0, max 45 ms, 61 unhandled rejections. Every unhandled rejection's query contained `typarray`. The Bun.sql drain test was 40 unavailable results and 0 empty reads on 15 and on 18. After the close rule, Postgres 17 kill storm settled 1,600, hung 0, max 319 ms, 20 unhandled rejections, every one containing typarray. The Bun.sql drain on 17 was again 40 unavailable and 0 empty. Issue #264 |
| D264 | Gate and release 0.7.0 (P99, P99R) | Hygiene before `bun run bump release` (D115). No new API. No behavior change. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moved. The postgres.js incremental gate stays 16,000, from #264. `pool.separation: PGlite reserve` used Bun's 5s default while its poll allows 10s, and the test is a concurrent test in `tests/topology-conformance.test.ts`, which the consistency shard runs. Under that load the runner killed it at 5s; alone it finishes. It is now `test.serial` with a 30s timeout. The poll still fails at 10s if the insert never lands. That is the Postgres 18 consistency-shard timeout that passed on rerun. Not a product bug. The kill storm on that same shard (`tests/postgres-kill-storm-pg.test.ts`) marked 288 calls hung under the full Postgres 17 suite because the watch was 1.3 s. Alone it was hung 0, max 103 ms, settled 1,600. The statement timeout stays 300 ms. The settle bound is 10 s. Not a product bug. Dead code: no `TODO` or `FIXME` under `src/`. Spike fixtures stay because the type ceilings measure them. No identical helper was merged. Dependencies: `bun audit` found no vulnerabilities in 209 packages. `bun outdated` lists `@electric-sql/pglite-socket` 0.2.11 to 0.2.13, `@types/bun` 1.4.2 to 1.4.3, `oxlint` 1.86.0 to 1.88.0, `oxlint-tsgolint` 7.0.2003 to 7.0.2004, `publint` 0.3.24 to 0.3.25, `temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5 to 1.0.6, peer `@electric-sql/pglite` 0.5.8 to 0.5.10, and `typescript` 6.0.3 whose latest is 7.0.2. `oxfmt` 0.71.0 and `oxc-parser` 0.152.0 are current for their ranges and have newer latest versions. None of those bumps is in this commit: the peer and the TypeScript package are not risk-free dev-tool patches, and the linter, formatter, and publint bumps can change the check. Deferred, still open: the postgres.js unhandled rejection from `fetchArrayTypes`, the dropped queued `sql.reserve()` (okmodel starts it again), a wait with no call deadline (rejected only when the pool or its reserved connection has closed), Bun.sql refusing a 2^64 id, tool 5, the postgres.js `FETCH` stream loop (D245), a Windows CI job, and an OKE Windows audit. `bun run size`: runtime entry min 5288 bytes, gzip 2026 bytes, cold import 4.135 ms, and 3.181 ms inside `bun run check` (gate 6,100 / 2,250). | D265 | Offline-first constraints (cross-cutting) | The sequence is unchanged: PostgreSQL, then SQLite, then MySQL, then shared SQL, then OKModel inside OKEngine. R1–R7 in [`offline-first-constraints.md`](offline-first-constraints.md) are constraints and do not start a phase. The audit is P100 `p100-offline-audit`, the first step of the 0.8 train, and it is report only. Placement is How we work rule 12, the 0.8 row (P100, and the R2, R3, and R4 rows on `okm driver test`), the 0.9 lines for `okm catalog export` and the programmatic schema API (R1 and R5), and the M3 and M4 sentences under Later phases, all in [`okmodel-execution-plan.md`](okmodel-execution-plan.md). OKModel builds no sync cursors, CDC adapters, offline command queues, sync authorization, conflict handling, or realtime orchestration. SQLite support does not commit OKModel to browser or Expo storage adapters. No gate moves. The 0.8 line-up is P100 the offline audit (report only, in parallel with the first heavy step), P101 a type and byte budget report Ali approves before P102, P102 the SQL builder, P103 typed raw SQL, P104 `explain()` and the dev inspector, P105 `okmodel/otel`, P106 `okm driver test` with the R2, R3, R4, and R6 rows, P107 the Neon adapter after P106, P108 and P108B inline relations `as`/`has` after P101 and a named issue Ali approves (`okmodel-relations-inline-design.md`), and P109 the 0.8.0 gate with the D115 hygiene; typed `fn` calls and builder views stay if the budget allows, and the Windows CI job and the OKE Windows audit (#218, #219) run in parallel with no source. |
| D266 | Composed SQL fragments are a tagged operator (P102) | Rule 15 names composed `sql` fragments and the spec had no builder section. The check-constraint `sql` tag stays a string, so the query form is `fragment`. It is a tagged operator named `sql`. The app imports `fragment` and `ident` the way it imports any other operator. `emitLazy` already sends that name to the operator compiler, so `plan.ts` grows no branch. The compiler is `sql-fragment.ts`, loaded with the operator chunk and with a `select` that contains a fragment. A string, number, boolean, or null is a parameter. `ident(name)` is the TypeScript field, the same name `where` uses. The compiler quotes `column.sql`, so casing and `sqlName` apply. A hidden field is OKM1120 with the same fix as `where`. `hidden({ filterable: true })` is allowed in `where` only; a select fragment refuses every hidden field. An unknown name is OKM1120. The table reaches the compiler through the existing operator emit and the select compiler. `plan.ts` grows no branch. `fragment.column` is the where field. A nested fragment is parenthesized. The literal cannot carry a comment, a semicolon, a `$`, or an unbalanced quote or parenthesis (OKM1125), so it cannot close the tenant predicate. The tenant predicate is still applied first. `.as(name)` is the `select` form on `find` or `one`. The result reuses the window slot, so the key is a number, and a non-numeric wire value is OKM1125. An empty identifier is OKM1122. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moves. Measured on this branch: featureless app 90,831 / 29,435 (gate 90,900 / 30,170, 69 minified left). P103 and P104 start from 69, not 77. Runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026, postgres.js incremental 15,953 (gate 16,000), composite types 6,931 of 6,931. Each connect entry grew 8 minified and stays under its gate. Issue #281 |
| D267 | `okmodel/otel` maps the existing observers (P105) | The entry is `okmodel/otel`. It does not import `@opentelemetry/*` and it is not a dependency. The caller passes a tracer. There is no meter: §5.5 and the M2 row name `db.*` span attributes and the fingerprint as the query summary, not a metric. `otel({ tracer, statement? })` returns `hookm`, `onRoute`, and `query`. `hookm` and `onRoute` are the observers `connect` already calls. `query` takes an `inspect()` result, because those observers do not carry a fingerprint, statement text, or a table. A route span is named for the hook's `op` (`read`, `write`, `tx`, or `lock`). `db.system.name` is `postgresql`. `db.operation.name` is that same op. The span kind is client (`2`). The endpoint and the reason are a span event named `route`: they are not database attributes. The span starts and ends in that call. `onRoute` fires once, when the endpoint is chosen, so the span has no duration. A transaction span opens on phase `start` and ends on `commit` (status OK) or `rollback` (status ERROR, `error.type` `rollback`). Depth 0 is named `transaction`. A greater depth is `savepoint`. Overlapping work at one depth pairs last-in, first-out, because the event has no span id. `onError` records `error.type` as the error name and, when the error has an OKM code, that code on the exception, with no message and no stack. A query the caller receives as a throw is not an `onError`. `query(inspect())` sets `db.collection.name` to the table, `db.operation.name` to the call (`find`, `one`, and the other read names), and `db.query.summary` to the fingerprint (§5.5). `db.query.text` is set only when `statement` is true, and only from that inspection. Parameters are never attributes. A tenant id is not an attribute: the spec does not ask for one, and `where` is not copied. The entry is experimental. Nothing in the query path, the runtime entry, or a gated graph imports it. No gate is added. Issue #284 |

An app that does not import them drops `ops/lane.ts`. `raw(table, Class, "read" or "write", fragment\`...\`)` returns one instance per row. The class constructs with no arguments. Own enumerable keys, in initializer order, are the columns. A table field uses that field's decode. Any other key uses the initializer: a number, a boolean, or driver text. Null stays null. `"write"` runs on the primary. A scoped tenant read is wrapped so the tenant predicate, and `archived_at is null` when the table is archivable, sits outside the fragment. The select list must name the one primary key besides the tenant key with `ident`, unaliased, or the call is OKM1702. A raw write on a scoped tenant table is OKM1702 and sends nothing. `unscoped("reason")` skips the tenant predicate. The archive filter still wraps a read. A missing scope is OKM1701 from the existing table guard. A hidden field, including `hidden({ filterable: true })`, is OKM1120. A comment, a semicolon, or a `$` in the literal is OKM1125 (D266). A bad class or a statement that is not one select or one write is OKM1602. `explain(table, query)` sends `EXPLAIN` outside production. In production it does not send the statement: `plan` is null and `params` is empty. On a topology client, `inspect().routing.reason` matches `onRoute`. It treats `CREATE` or `ALTER DOMAIN` and `CREATE [OR REPLACE] FUNCTION … LANGUAGE sql` as able to trigger OKM1552, so a reasoned allow is not OKM1510 before replay. It does not fire for `varchar(n)` widening, `varchar(n)` or `text` to unconstrained `varchar`, `varchar(n)` to `text`, `text` or `varchar` to unconstrained `bpchar`, a numeric precision increase at the same scale, `numeric(p,s)` to `numeric`, `integer` to `oid` and the reverse, `cidr` to `inet`, `xml` to `text`, an unconstrained domain over the old type, or any domain to its base. Pull then replays the objects on a scratch schema, in the catalog's dependency order, with `check_function_bodies` on, and drops that schema. A SQL function or a view whose dependency is missing fails creation and is `unverified`. A plpgsql function that is created is `candidate-only`: creation does not prove the body. A SQL function or a `BEGIN ATOMIC` body that is created is `verified`. The report and a comment above the declaration say which. A pulled plpgsql function lists `dependsOn`, including an empty list, so OKM1824 passes. Aggregates, procedures, event triggers, and rules stay under `not pulled yet` with a count, because the catalog has no kind for them. Policies, roles, and grants stay there too. A missing file, or `public`, keeps the unqualified query text. | D285 | OKM1538 is an expand/contract recipe in 0.9 (P119) | There is no automatic safe form for a type change that rewrites the heap. The documented answer is five migrations: add the new column, backfill it with a `backfill()` step, dual-write with a trigger, switch reads (the safe `NOT NULL` form), then drop the old column. Each is its own migration. The example is `prices.cents` (`integer`) to `prices.amount` (`numeric(12,2)`) in [`type-change.md`](type-change.md), and `tests/type-change-pg.test.ts` runs those migrations on Postgres. `okm migrate type-change` is not a command in 0.9. It is built later only if someone asks for it. No new error kind. No public API. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #317 |

| D287 | A fragment refuses a backslash (B-01) | `scanLiteral` only treated a doubled quote as an escape. Postgres also treats a backslash as an escape in an `E` string and when `standard_conforming_strings` is off, so a fragment that closes a quote with a backslash escape desynced the guard: `;`, `--`, `$`, and parentheses went unchecked and a tenant filter could be escaped. Decision: refuse any backslash in fragment template text with OKM1125 and the fix "pass the value as an interpolation". That covers an `E` string escape, a hex escape, and the scs=off case. Dollar quotes, `;`, `--`, and `/*` stay refused outside quotes; inside quotes they stay plain text. Doubled quotes and `U&'...' UESCAPE` without a backslash keep working. The same `compile` path guards `fragment`, `raw`, and nested fragments; `sql-fragment.ts` only emits parts already scanned. No new public API. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #334 |
| D288 | Bun.sql sessions stay isolated; a committed write stays committed (B-03, B-04) | The Bun.sql adapter no longer parks the last released handle for the next checkout. Every release returns the handle to Bun's pool after `RESET ALL` and `pg_advisory_unlock_all()`, or destroys it when the session is gone. Parking existed only so the harness reset check reused `pg_backend_pid()`; that check now asserts advisory locks on the released pid without requiring reuse. A reset or release failure after the statement or `COMMIT` completed does not fail the call on Bun.sql, node-postgres, or Neon: the connection is destroyed and the successful result stands, so `afterCommit` runs. postgres.js already swallowed reset failures. A failure after the send and before the result stays `outcome_unknown` (OKM1401), is not retried, and does not run `afterCommit`. Bun.sql still stamps `Connection closed` and `connection must be a PostgresSQLConnection` with SQLSTATE `08006`. node-postgres stamps `Client has encountered a connection error and is not queryable` as `CONNECTION_CLOSED`. No new public API, error kind, or catalog hash change. No gate moves. Measured: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (unchanged), featureless app 90,718 / 29,426 (unchanged), Bun.sql connect 39,504 / 13,754 (was 39,717 / 13,839). Issue #335 |
| D289 | Push re-applies, hash re-stamps, check handles triggers (0.9.1) | Tooling and dialect introspection only. No new public runtime API. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling move. `okm push` plans from the live catalog on the target (not the last migration-file catalog), applies under a fresh `push-<n>` id from `okm_history`, and never prints `nothing to apply` when `okm_meta` differs from the head hash. An empty plan that already matches prints `no changes`. When the plan has no DDL but the stored hash drifted (an external table gained a column after the dependent exported a new snapshot), `okm push` and `okm migrate apply` re-stamp `okm_meta` and append a history row (`push-<n>` or `restamp-<n>`) under the apply lock after `okm check` has verified the snapshot. File apply enables that re-stamp only when `defineConfig({ external })` is set, so a foreign or pre-D208 roles hash without external snapshots stays for connect or the legacy restamp. A stale or missing snapshot stays OKM1841. The ownership mark is outside the startup hash; the external table's columns are inside it (D232, D277). `okm migrate check` maps a trigger's called function to the logical namespace when that function lives in the scratch schema, so `timestamps({ enforce: "trigger" })` and hand-written triggers no longer fail OKM1020. View sealing creates missing catalog extensions before stub tables and drops only those it created after the scratch schema goes away, so a first `okm push` of `citext` (or another extension type) succeeds without leaving the extension for a later `CREATE EXTENSION` step. `okm check`, `okm push`, and `okm migrate check` reprint each domain check on the connected server before they plan (`sealDomainChecks` / `reprintDomainCheck`), so an author spelling such as `VALUE > 0` is not permanent live drift or OKM1547 against `((VALUE > 0))` (B-37). The catalog hash keeps the author text. `okm migrate check` still warns OKM1552 when the stored text differs (D276). After an external re-stamp, status stays current and migrate check does not OKM1549 for external-only catalog drift when generate is empty (B-45). OKM1531 does not fire for `VALIDATE` of a temporary `col IS NOT NULL` check that an earlier step added `NOT VALID`, matching the documented type-change recipe (D285, B-36). An unknown CLI flag is `invalid` with a fix that names `okm <command> --help`; that help lists the flags for that command. Backfill progress prints a 1-based step index. Issue #336 |
| D290 | Pull, import, and ext scaffold emit buildable names (B-14 to B-19, B-38) | Tooling only. `okm pull` and `okm import drizzle` write `.references(<export>)` using the table binding, not the SQL table name, so a sanitized parent builds. Export bindings and file stems are unique under lowercase fold (`Tasks` / `tasks` → binding `Tasks` / `tasks_2`, files `Tasks.ts` / `tasks_2.ts`) so row-type names do not collide, and never a Windows reserved device name (`con`, `aux`, `nul`, `com1`–`com9`, `lpt1`–`lpt9`, any case): those get a `_` prefix on the stem. The same database always yields the same names. SQL names stay in `sqlName` when the binding differs. A column `__proto__` is emitted as `["__proto__"]:`. Drizzle import forces composite primary-key columns not null. Missing or failing `oxfmt` prints a notice on stderr, leaves the files unformatted, and still returns the report. `okm ext scaffold` includes composite types (`typtype = 'c'`). No public schema DSL change. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No runtime-entry or featureless-app gate move. Issue #337 |
| D291 | Write safety: unknown keys and sensitive causes (B-39, B-33); B-40 split | (1) **B-39.** `insert`, `update`, upsert / `onConflict`, and first-or-create refuse an unknown key with OKM1120. The message names the key and the table (same shape as `orderBy`). Typos are no longer dropped. Guarded, sealed, and computed fields keep OKM1190 and the rest. No new ErrorKind. Codes OKM1140–OKM1179 stay reserved. (2) **B-33.** When the error's table has a sensitive column, or the error names a sensitive column, `mapPostgresError` sets `cause` to a plain object with only safe fields (`name`, `sqlstate`, `constraint`, `table`, `column`, nested driver `code`). It never copies `detail`, `message`, parameters, `where`, or `internalQuery`. Tables with no sensitive column keep today's cause (`error.cause ?? error`). `includeValues` stays the explicit opt-in that can keep values. Covers postgres.js, node-postgres, Bun.sql, and PGlite through the shared mapper. D58 and D125 are unchanged. (3) **B-40.** Split to its own branch. Wiring the protocol parameter check through the read `client` path grew the featureless app by more than 100 B over the stop line (measured +371 minified with the combined change). No OKM1126 in this step. Issue #339 |
| D292 | Explicit Postgres parameter limit (B-40) | A finished statement over 65,535 parameters is OKM1126 (`invalid`, not retryable). The message states the count and the limit and points at chunking or `= any(array)`. The limit is for the whole statement (filters, `inList`, `limit`, values), not `inList` alone. Reads check lazily in `readHandle` before `execute` (dynamic import of the message chunk). Writes check in the lazy write `Sql.statement` / `withParams` path. Insert chunking (`WRITE_PARAM_BUDGET`) is unchanged. Split out of issue #339 / D291 (unknown keys / sensitive causes land separately) when the first wiring grew the featureless app by more than 100 B. Measured: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026 (unchanged), featureless app +83 minified (90,801 / 29,451 against 90,900 / 30,170). No new public API. No new ErrorKind. No catalog hash change. No gate move. Issue #339 |
| D293 | Roles apply, PUBLIC execute, pooler URLs (B-30, B-31, B-25, B-41) | Tooling only. No new public runtime API. No new error kind. No catalog hash change (roles, grants, and default privileges stay outside the startup hash, D208). No runtime-entry or featureless-app gate move. Apply creates every managed role as the connecting user before `SET ROLE` to the migration role, so a second `CREATE ROLE` is not refused. Before `SET ROLE` or `OWNER TO` it grants itself membership (`WITH SET TRUE` on Postgres 16+; a plain grant on Postgres 15, where `CREATE ROLE` leaves no membership). When it can, it grants `CREATE` on the database and makes the migration role the schema owner so `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA` is not a silent no-op. A missing `CREATEROLE` or `SET` option is a clear `forbidden` error. A failed role unit rolls back and stays re-runnable. With `defineConfig({ roles })`, generated and user functions revoke `EXECUTE` from `PUBLIC` and grant it only to the app role (not reporting); without `roles`, behavior is unchanged. Migrate strips the `pgbouncer` query parameter before the URL becomes a startup parameter; other pooler hints are not interpreted. The pooler pid probe reads eight times and holds another backend between reads so a default PgBouncer (`server_round_robin=0`) is refused; URL heuristics stay. A direct connection is never a false positive. Managed-provider admin roles (RDS, Cloud SQL, Supabase) wait for M-4. Issue #341 |
| D294 | postgres.js and pg storm recovery (B-02, B-43, B-44) | postgres.js sets `backoff: () => 0.05 + Math.random() * 0.2` (seconds, under 1 s with jitter) so reconnects no longer grow to 10–20 s per attempt. `timeouts.acquire` defaults to 10 s on postgres.js and node-postgres when omitted; a timeout stays OKM1846 (kind `timeout`). A module-level `uncaughtException` listener swallows postgres.js `nextWrite` on a null socket (Bun `socket.write` and Node `reading 'write'`) and rebuilds each open pool so the next read is not stuck on a dead queue. Other uncaught exceptions `console.error` and set `process.exitCode = 1` so Bun does not exit 0 when the listener returns (migrate CLI refusals keep stderr and fail). node-postgres arms every client on `connect` and logs idle/`error` events through the notice path without throwing (B-44); checked-out clients keep a listener after the pool removes its idle handler. No new public API, error kind, or catalog hash change. No gate move. Measured: runtime entry 5,288 / 2,026; featureless app 90,718 / 29,587; postgres.js incremental 15,956 of 16,000. Issue #340 |
| D295 | Tree cycles, Action author, driver test cleanup, testing helpers (B-10, B-22, B-05, B-06, B-42, B-35, B-32, B-28, B-34) | (1) **B-10.** An `update` that sets a tree parent to the row's own id still fails at plan time (OKM1120). An update that would point the parent at a descendant is refused at execute with the same code; the lazy write statement uses a recursive guard and matched/updated counts. A non-key `where` is still unchecked. `batch` refuses that guarded update. (2) **B-22.** The sticky plan comment match requires the marker and an allowed author (`github-actions[bot]`, plus `GITHUB_ACTOR` when set). Another author's marked comment is not patched. (3) **B-05 / B-06.** Wire and error driver-suite cases drop leftover `okm_*` tables before the pool closes. (4) **B-42.** `package.json` `engines.bun` is `>=1.4.0`. `okm driver test` refuses an older Bun with a clear message. Cases without a body stay `unsupported`, never `pass`. (5) **B-35.** A throw from a custom `routing.select` that is an `OkmError` keeps its kind; any other throw becomes kind `invalid` with that value as `cause` (not `driver`). (6) **B-32 / B-28 / B-34.** Factories and `isolation()` work under `compositeTenancy()` by treating every tenancy key as scope and filtering them from `ref()`'s primary key. Concurrent creates use a per-call in-flight set (no shared cycle marker). `isolation()` seeds every composite key on view sources and refreshes matviews; outside that helper, call `db.views.<name>.refresh()` after factory inserts. No new public API. No new ErrorKind. Codes OKM1140–OKM1179 stay reserved. No catalog hash change. Tree check stays in the lazy write chunk. Issue #342 |
| D296 | Known limits made honest (DM-1 to DM-12, B-07, B-08) | Docs and tests only. No public API, ErrorKind, or catalog hash change. No gate move. Known-limits, README, guide, spec, and handbook pages match the code after the 0.9.1 fixes: Temporal polyfill on Node 22/24 (OKM1210); `okm dev` does not watch; `okmodel/safety` is opt-in; `.sensitive()` covers causes and spans; untyped `sql.raw` is absent (B-07) and raw projection is class-only (B-08); OKM1401 / committed-write wording stays; minimum Bun 1.4; `okmodel.config.ts` (not `okm.config.ts`); `listen` is a driver capability only; `okm catalog export --json` is a column snapshot and `describe(schema)` is the full catalog; backfill progress is 1-based while status keeps the stored index; a Not tested section (real Neon, native Windows, Action on real GitHub, long soak). `okmodel/pg/neon` already exports `capabilities`. In-repo tests cover doctor, `okm dev`, step numbering, testing helpers, and view camelCase. Issue #343 |
| D297 | Deno is not supported | Deno is expected to stop within a year (Ali, 2026-10-18). OKModel does not support it. Supported runtimes are Node, Bun, and an edge or Workers runtime where an adapter exists. CI does not install Deno. `bun run test:runtimes` runs the portable checks on Node and imports the runtime entry as an edge bundle on Node. Bun runs `bun test`. There is no `deno` export condition and no JSR package. No public API change. No new error kind. No catalog hash change. No `src/` change. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #358 |
| D298 | `--config` and programmatic plan, apply, status (G7) | Global `--config <path>` on every `okm` command that loads a config; default discovery stays `okmodel.config.ts` in the working directory. A relative or absolute path sets the project root to that file's directory so monorepo packages and absolute paths work. A missing file is `invalid` and names `okmodel.config.ts` and `--config`. Docs and the loader agree on `okmodel.config.ts` (not `okm.config.ts`). Public `plan`, `apply`, and `status` on `okmodel/migrate` share the CLI implementation: no process exit, prompts, or stdin. `plan` returns stable step ids, class, SQL, and lint; `status` returns coarse compatibility (`up to date`, `pending`, `ahead by expand`, `diverged`) with pending `${migrationId}:${stepIndex}` ids; `apply` resumes from those history ids and throws the same typed errors. Nothing enters the runtime entry or the featureless app graph. No new ErrorKind. No catalog hash change. No gate move. Issue #367 |
| D299 | `okm migrate baseline` adopts an existing database | Tooling only. `okm migrate baseline` and `baseline(target)` from `okmodel/migrate` record the head snapshot as applied history on a live database without DDL. Before writing, the command introspects the target and plans managed objects against the head catalog. A difference is OKM1858 and prints the plan that would reconcile them; nothing is marked applied. Objects present in the database and absent from the model (other tables, triggers, extensions, functions, and the same kinds) are listed as unmanaged, never dropped or altered, and appear in the human report and in `--json`. A target that already has OKModel history is OKM1857. A repeat on the same `baselined@<migration id>` and catalog hash is a no-op (`already baselined`). The history mark is `baselined@<id>`; status and apply treat it like `provisioned@`. The operation is `history-repair`, so a protected target needs `--allow-protected`. `--dry-run` prints what would be recorded. Writes only `okm_meta` and `okm_history` (and creates those tables when absent). No new ErrorKind. No catalog hash change. No runtime-entry or featureless-app gate move. Issue #368 |
| D300 | `describe()` reports `minPostgres` and extensions; doctor and check use them; Neon capabilities stay the shared flags | `describe(schema)` on `okmodel/migrate` adds `minPostgres` (15–18) and `extensions: { name, reason }[]` beside `logical` and `catalog` (D284). The floor is the project floor, the declared `minPostgres` / deprecated `requires.postgres`, or 18 when a column stores `uuidv7()`. Extensions come from `extension(...)` records, dependency edges (columns and functions), and index `using … _ops` tails mapped to known packs. `okm doctor` and `okm check` connect and refuse with existing codes: OKM1802 / OKM1803 for the major, OKM1811 for a missing extension. On `okm check`, the major runs before author drift and the extension check runs after it. An extension an applied or pending migration creates is not OKM1811 for a missing install; when that package is absent from `pg_available_extensions`, OKM1811 points at `okm migrate apply` after the package is on the server (#400). Several targets and no `--target` print `server checks skipped: N targets, pass --target <name>` instead of connecting. No new ErrorKind and no new OKM code. Catalog hash is unchanged. Neon already exported `capabilities` / `httpCapabilities` (D269, D296); the shared adapter-contract test asserts both modes use only the existing capability names (`transactions`, `stream`, `listen`, `cancel`, `prepared`, `describe`) and that the entry re-exports the registry values. No new capability names. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #369 |
| D301 | Linter accepts the planner's own NOT VALID / VALIDATE pair on a new column | Tooling only. No planner change. OKM1531 already exempts a `widen-check` validate and the temporary `IS NOT NULL` floor validate. It also exempts a `VALIDATE` whose matching `ADD CONSTRAINT … CHECK … NOT VALID` is earlier in the same migration when every column of that check was `ADD COLUMN`ed earlier in that migration with a non-volatile default, or as a nullable column with no default. Pairing matches `notNullFloorValidate`. A check on an existing column, a volatile default, and every other validate stay OKM1531. No new ErrorKind. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #389 |
| D302 | Validated writes keep `~plan` for `batch` and `isolation` (Q1) | `guardWrite` returned a chain handle with `sql` / `expect` / `all` and no `~plan`. `batch` requires `~plan` (OKM1121 otherwise), and `isolation()` calls `client.batch([handle.update(...)])`, so `validation: true` broke both. Decision: the chain exposes `~plan` (and `sql` / `inspect`) after the same validation step; batched validation runs before the plan executes; a failing rule rolls the whole batch back and reports OKM1200 with `issues` and `batchIndex`. `{ validate: false }`, delete, and archive stay unchanged. Unknown-key handling is R1 / D304. No new ErrorKind. No public API change. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #388 |
| D303 | `okm generate` exits 1 on an error finding (Q8) | D192 had generate write the file and print findings without refusing. CI and agents treated exit 0 as success while apply would refuse the same finding with OKM1510. Decision: generate still writes the migration and prints each finding; an error finding then exits 1 with OKM1510 and stderr that `okm migrate apply` will refuse until it is fixed. A warning-only finding stays exit 0. No new ErrorKind. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #395 |
| D304 | Validation refuses unknown write keys (R1) | With `validation: true`, `acceptKeys` threw away unknown write keys (`continue`), so a typo on insert was a silent no-op and a typo-only update became OKM1120 "update set is empty." Decision: validation throws the same OKM1120 as the core write path (key, table, accepted names, did-you-mean). No pass-through of unknown keys to the write planner — the check lives in the validation engine when validation runs. `{ validate: false }` and `validation: false` keep the core path. Same surface as D291: insert, insert list, update (mixed and typo-only), upsert / `onConflict`, first-or-create, `tx`, and `batch`. No new ErrorKind. No public API change. No catalog hash change. No gate or ceiling moves. Issue #398 |
| D305 | Versioned `update` requires `version` in the type (Q5) | D254 left `version` optional on `UpdateTarget` and required it only at runtime. Decision: `versioned()` stamps `~versioned: true` on the table type (same shape as `~archive` for `archivable()`). `TableApi.update` selects a required-`version` target when that flag is present; a non-versioned table keeps `version` optional on `UpdateTarget`. The runtime OKM1120 for a missing `version` stays as the backstop when types are bypassed. Per-row update lists and `expect` are unchanged. No new ErrorKind. No catalog hash change. The per-table conditional measures +26 instantiations and +7 types on the 200-table query probes (extensions 17,312 instantiations, features 6,938 types). Ceilings move by the D176 rule (measured plus 3 percent, rounded down): inferred-200 instantiations 17,300 → 17,831; query-features types 6,931 → 7,146. No size gate move. Types lane starts here (Q5, then Q7, Q6, Q3, R7). Issue #392 |

## Neon conformance notes

Addendum to D269. These rows are postgres.js and node-postgres pointed at Neon endpoints, from the channel-binding audit on [#291](https://github.com/omqkhafi/okmodel/pull/291). They are not the `okm driver test neon` result, which was 64 pass, 0 fail, and 18 unsupported. `select 1` succeeded for postgres.js, node-postgres, Neon HTTP, and Neon WebSocket on primary and primary pool. The audit did not mention `channel_binding`. Those drivers were not changed.

| Driver | Endpoint | Pass | Fail | Unsupported |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| postgres.js | primary | 7 | 75 | 0 |
| postgres.js | primary pool | 7 | 75 | 0 |
| node-postgres | primary | 77 | 4 | 1 |
| node-postgres | primary pool | 4 | 77 | 1 |

postgres.js on both endpoints starts failing at `wire.execute-returns-rows`, and the rest of the wire cases fail after that. Cause: unclassified.

node-postgres on primary fails `wire.acquire-timeout-is-okm1846-and-does-not-use-another-pool`, `tx.tx-signal-cancels-the-transaction-and-rolls-it-back`, `r2.commit-loss`, and `r2.batch-commit-loss`. Cause of each: unclassified. The unsupported case is not named. Which case: unclassified.

node-postgres on the primary pool fails the same way postgres.js does, from `wire.execute-returns-rows`. Cause: unclassified. The unsupported case is not named. Which case: unclassified.

node-postgres prints a warning that `sslmode=require` is treated as `verify-full`. The audit does not establish that warning as the cause of a failure.

The audit names primary and primary pool only. It does not report these two drivers against r1 or r2.

Two postgres.js observations are recorded in D263 and repeated in D264. They are notes for a possible upstream report. They are not filed issues, and they are not established as the cause of the rows above. `fetchArrayTypes` can reject with no handler when the backend dies during connect: `ReadyForQuery` returns that call with no `.catch()`. A queued `sql.reserve()` can be dropped without settling when a connection slot closes. okmodel starts that reserve again.
